# THE TWENTY-SIX FINDINGS OF `logs/batch-0005.review.log`, AND WHAT THIS REPAIR DID TO EACH. **THREE BLOCKERS, THIRTEEN ARITHMETIC AND CONTINUITY DEFECTS, SIX REUSE DEFECTS, TWO COLLISIONS AND TWO STRUCTURAL DEFECTS. ALL TWENTY-SIX ADDRESSED. NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO EVENT WAS MOVED, NO TITLE WAS CHANGED, NO CHARACTER WAS AGED, NO CARD LOST ITS BEAT, AND THE PLANNED PLOT IS UNCHANGED. TEN FILES, 81 LINES INSERTED AND 77 DELETED, AND THE ONLY LIVE OUTCOME IS THE WORD COUNT: 32,121 IS 32,294.** THIS BLOCK SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT AND DELETES NONE OF THEM.

**THE REVIEW'S OWN VERDICT WAS THAT 644 AND 646 ARE THE REAL THING AND THAT THE SCENE AT 643 RUNS THE HOUR-SCARCITY PROPERLY. BOTH ARE UNTOUCHED IN EVERYTHING THAT MATTERS.** The six-line foot, the name under the striking, *She cannot say that anybody took anything off her*, the counterman who can write a thing down and that being the whole of what he has, and the far-table staging that replaced Ines Duarte's own 584 staging — none of these was touched except by the sentence-level repairs named below. **THE PROBLEM WITH THIS BATCH WAS NEVER THE PROSE. IT WAS THAT THE GATE AT THE HEAD OF FOUR STATE FILES CERTIFIED A CLEAN PAGE THAT WAS NOT CLEAN, AND EVERY ONE OF THE TWENTY-SIX FINDINGS IS A FIGURE OR A CLAIM.**

## 0r-1. THE THREE BLOCKERS. **A GATE ROW THAT REPORTS ZERO AND THE PAGE IS NOT ZERO.**

**1 · `AGES -> 0` WAS FALSE IN THREE PLACES.** `chapter-0647.md`:195 read *I am forty-one and I have a ledger*; `chapter-0647.md`:99 read *forty-four years declining to answer*; `chapter-0649.md`:119 read *a boy of sixteen standing at a bench*. **THE PHASE THAT WROTE THE BATCH HAD ALREADY FOUND YUSUF ADEYEMI'S AGE AND CHANGED IT IN ONE PLACE, AND THE SAME PASS CHANGED NOTHING IN THE OTHER FOUR. THIS IS THE SAME DEFECT `reviews/volume-12/volume-close.md` FOUND IN A SCENE-MEASUREMENT TABLE — A PASS THAT FIXES A FIGURE WHERE IT LOOKED AND NOT WHERE IT WAS — AND IT IS THE THIRD TIME IN THIS REPOSITORY.** All three are repaired into durations or out of existence: *I have been behind this counter longer than you have been on nights* · *that is the one question in this city I have declined for the whole of my working life* · *a bench at the back of it with nobody sitting at the bench*. **THE BOY AT THE PAINTING COUNTER WAS ALSO A COLLISION: 649:121's *He has not been here since the twenty-second of December* is about the kerb boy, and there was no boy in that room to be the subject of it.**

**2 · THE INVENTED-FIGURES SENTENCE WAS PRINTED VERBATIM TWICE IN THIS BATCH.** At `chapter-0643.md`:115, in the mouth of an auditor who has read four hundred and nineteen lines, and again in the italic footer of `chapter-0646.md`:187. **THE BATCH'S OWN REFUSED LIST SAID IT WAS *referred to in other words once at 643 and is never quoted as a quote*. IT WAS QUOTED TWICE.** Both are rewritten: at 643 it is now what the third of the four *does*, in her mouth, not what it *says*; and in 646's footer it is *the one that takes the value out of a figure in this city until somebody checks it*. **THE ONE REMAINING VERBATIM PRINTING IN THE BOOK IS `chapter-0637.md`:75, WHICH IS LIN HAO READING THE LINE OFF THE SHEET AND NOT QUOTING IT, AND IT IS THE FIRST PRINTING AND IT STAYS.**

**3 · THE WALL'S HEADCOUNT WAS REPRINTED AT 645.** `state/continuity.md` carried a row reading *THE WALL · ITS HEADCOUNT IS NOT PRINTED IN 641–650 · 605 IS THE ONLY PRINTING OF IT IN THE VOLUME*. `chapter-0645.md`:181 printed *a wall about nine thousand people went under on an ordinary morning* — 605's figure, in 605's sentence, with 605's reason attached to it. **THE WHOLE PARAGRAPH IS GONE AND IT TOOK 605'S STAGING WITH IT**, because the 605 sentence and the headcount were the same sentence.

## 0r-2. THE THIRTEEN ARITHMETIC AND CONTINUITY DEFECTS. **EVERY ONE RE-DERIVED FROM DAY NUMBERS BEFORE ANYTHING WAS CHANGED.**

**THE TWO SPINE METHODS WERE RE-RUN AGAINST THIRTY-ANCHOR PLUS TEN-DATE TARGETS BEFORE ANY PROSE WAS TOUCHED, AND EVERY FIGURE BELOW WAS TAKEN FROM THAT RE-DERIVATION AND NOT FROM THE REVIEW'S WORKING.**

**4 · `643`:27** *two hundred and fifty-two days* → **two thousand and forty-eight**. The seventh of March of year one is day 36, fixed by `chapter-0613.md`:23 and by Claim 1 of the Volume 12 close, and `2,084 − 36 = 2,048`.
**5 · `643`:139** *about eleven hundred and sixty days ago* → **eleven hundred and twenty-four**. The first of October of the third year is 960 and `2,084 − 960 = 1,124`.

**7 · `645`:25 against `:35` — HE SAID HE HAD BEEN DOING THE STAIR IN TWO SINCE THE MIDDLE OF OCTOBER AND SHE SAID HE HAD BEEN DOING IT IN TWO *UNTIL THE TWENTY-THIRD*.** The contradiction is now the correction: **she tells him he is wrong about it**, and the change is dated to the twenty-fourth, which is 644's day, and the rate is *two landings worse in seven days*, which is 23 November to 30 November.
**8 · `645`:117** *a yard of about eleven thousand people took a line off a board on the seventh of July* → **about nine hundred people took the fourth of four**. **THE YARD HELD ELEVEN THOUSAND; ABOUT NINE HUNDRED DID THE TAKING**, and 637's footer, 640's footer and 644:137 all say nine hundred. **THE SAME PASS FOUND 650:123 SAYING *about four hundred people took the rest off nine weeks later*, WHICH IS THE SAME ERROR TWICE.** 650 now says **nine hundred, seven weeks and two days later** — `2,129 − 2,078 = 51` — and the man who did the September taking is named *with a rag and a bucket*, which is the form 637 and 640 use.
**9 · `649`:27, `:31`, `:43`, `:85`, `:149`** all said **nine months** for three hundred and six days. **306 IS TEN MONTHS AND SIX DAYS, AND 649's OWN FOOTER SAYS TEN.** All five now say ten.
**10 · `649`:165** *the twenty-seventh of December in the ninth month* → **the twelfth month**.
**11 · `650`:47 and `:55`** *about thirty-eight days* → **about fifty-one days**. `2,129 − 2,078 = 51`. **The six weeks in the same speech was 30 September to 9 November, which is thirty-nine days, and is now *since the thirtieth of September*.**
**12 · `650`:123** — see 8.
**13 · `650`:179** *the sixtieth month* → **the sixty-fourth month**, against *I have put sixty-three of them up* thirteen lines earlier and 648's sixty-two under the paint. **The boy's own speech was also missing an article: *put the sixty-fourth up on that corner*.**

**15 · `647`:43 and `:103`** said *the first of January*. **The sheet goes out at four on the thirtieth of December and there is no thirty-first.** `:103` now says *when it goes out on the thirtieth*, and `:43` no longer names a date at all: *as soon as it is on the rail and not before*.
**16 · THE SMALLER SIX.** `642`:109 *waiting eleven days* for a page face up **eight** → eight days. `642`:3 and `646`:3 both ran the month-borrow backwards, *seven days less thirty days is seven*, in the one place where there is nothing to borrow: **both now read *a month runs out at the thirtieth, so a day seven after it is the seventh of the next month*, which is what the two chapters' own footers already said.** `649`:7 *paving in on the twenty-first* → *on*. `649`:183 *the size of a leases* → *a lease*. `649`:177 *a hole in the middle of the five* → **the six**, because there are six lines and the fourth of them is the struck one. `644`:5 called the twenty-third of November *the last working day of the month*, and 641 and 645 both treat the thirtieth as one.

**AND TWO MORE THE REVIEW DID NOT NAME, FOUND IN THE SAME SWEEP AND REPAIRED ON THE SAME GROUND.** `646`:17 said *it had not been eleven days since there was anything on the wall* on the seventh of December, when 646's own next two paragraphs say twenty-eight days. `646`'s footer said *on the tenth of December there is nothing at the foot of that shutter*, which is a **future** date in a chapter set on the seventh.

## 0r-4. THE TWO COLLISIONS

**23 · TWO WOMEN OF ABOUT SIXTY AT THE SAME CORNER.** One stands at the bare shutter with a rag since the ninth of November and is named at 644, 645, 646 and 650. `chapter-0648.md`:163 put a second one at the same corner three days later. **The 648 woman is now a woman of about fifty, and she is still a woman who cannot write her own name.**
**24 · *THE MAN WITH THE RAG* IS A DIFFERENT MAN.** He is the September worker of 638 and 640, who told a man in the fourth week of September he would not stand at the foot of that board again. `chapter-0650.md`:89 put **a rag in Lin Hao's hand**, and `:133` put it there again four hundred words later. **Lin Hao now has his hands in his pockets in both places**, and 650:123 names the September worker *with a rag and a bucket*, which is the form the earlier chapters use.

## 0r-5. THE TWO STRUCTURAL DEFECTS

**25 · THERE WAS NO NEXT PHASE.** Volume 13 is finite at 650 and `workspace/volume-13/` held five batch directories and nothing else. `PROMPT.md` §8 forbade the batch from writing a close, so the boundary left the repository with no dispatchable phase and no reviewer would ever see it again. **`workspace/volume-13/volume-close/PROMPT.md` NOW EXISTS.** It writes no chapters, it does not touch `outline/ending.md`, and it is the only next phase.
**26 · THE BATCH'S ONLY FORESIGHT COLLAPSED INTO ACCOMPLISHED FACT.** At `chapter-0648.md`:145 Lin Hao predicts *in about nine days after that there will be a version of it in about four hundred houses in this city*. Eight days later `chapter-0650.md`:27 asserts *there are four sheets of paper in about four hundred houses* — **a month early, and in the narrator's voice, as a fact.** **650 now says there are copies being made tonight in about nine houses, and the four hundred is his prediction, said by him, and marked as his prediction: *Four hundred houses is what it will be in about a month, if he is any judge of it, and he has never once in six years been any judge of anything.*** A man who has been wrong in a yard of eleven thousand people three months ago is not entitled to a number about the future.

## 0z. WHAT THIS BATCH DELIVERED, AND WHAT IT REFUSED

**DELIVERED:** a ruled board in a drawer in a ward with eleven columns and a heavy line across the foot of every one of them and nothing under any of them, and the column with his plate on it eleven entries deep with the marks getting smaller · **the woman who takes a share off people and has not told one of them in eleven years breaking her own rule for exactly one person, and saying why before she does it** · a night shift with three women in it and a copy of the standing sheet lying face up for eight nights because a bundle came up an hour late on the thirtieth · **a woman who has read the same paper at the same counter about nine hundred nights and never said what, saying it once** · **a corridor at half past one where a question is put to the only person entitled to answer it and declined on the ground that a corridor has got four names on it** · **the scene, on the far table, with the page left on the near table under the lamp, and a woman who reads a man in order without once stopping to be spoken to** · **an hour she declines to give, and an hour she does, which is four o'clock, which is not the one he came for** · **the volume's last irreversible decision, at the foot of a page and not in a column, with a clerk's one question of the day being whether it is a line or a paragraph** · **the human sentence: a woman who cannot read can now say almost anything at all, and cannot say that anybody took anything off her** · **a ward, a shut door, a cot, nine minutes, and nothing there to take** · **a yard with nothing on the shutter since the ninth of November and a woman saying into an empty wall that she is not going to stand there and be a person about it** · **the chaplain asking how many people are in the yard this morning and saying that she has said four hundred on those steps for sixteen months and that it is not four hundred** · **a figure kept nine years on a woman's fingers, said out loud in a room for the first time, and said back badly by the woman who kept it** · **a bundle containing a sheet of paper with nothing written on it that came nine miles through a front door** · **a man behind a counter who has run it nine years and has never once asked anybody what a page was for, asking** · **sixty-two monthly forms stacked in a wall and the first rubbing ever made there** · **a department that answers elsewhere, a schedule with nothing in it about two words, and a slip the size of a thumb-nail which is not a way of getting anything and is a way of being found** · **a sentence said wrong about forty times inside an hour at the foot of a wall and corrected, which is the only thing this city has ever done to one of his sentences that worked.**

**THE VOLUME'S LAST IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS AT 644 AND IT IS HIS.** **WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of the fourth line — *and it is not on a page anywhere in this city* — standing in any yard, on any board, in any hand, for ever. It cannot be restored: a clerk may only enter what she is given and cannot improve it, an entry can be made and an entry cannot be taken back, and the page that carries the striking goes out at four on the thirtieth and about nine people a week will ever look at it. **WHAT IT COSTS:** every claim that rested on it dies at once, and the only one that mattered was always going to be a claim by a person who cannot read; about nine hundred people who can read a shutter know exactly who took it off; and he is the man who wrote the sentence that made it untestable in the first place. **WHAT IT IS NOT:** a scene, a rescue, a promotion, a defeat of Emil Brack, a climb, or an ending, and **he is still rank one on a branch night shift and nothing in this batch moves his hours, his plate, his quarters or his standing.**

**REFUSED:** the figure of the account · any floor, ledger total or running balance in grains · `settl` in every inflection · the reach of the return, argued by nobody in either direction · whether the fourth condition was ever true of anybody's deed · either repainting box · the keeper's six words · who put the white tile up · who cut the two words · Perrin Sowe · Dagmar Oyelowo · **any verbatim printing of the invented-figures sentence in 641-650. THE REVIEW FOUND IT VERBATIM TWICE IN THIS BATCH, AT `643`:115 AND IN 646's FOOTER, AGAINST A GATE THAT CLAIMED IT HAD BEEN REWRITTEN. IT IS NOW IN HER OWN MOUTH AT 643 AND IN OTHER WORDS IN THE FOOTERS OF 646, 647 AND 648. THE ONE REMAINING VERBATIM PRINTING IN THE BOOK IS `chapter-0637.md`:75, WHICH IS THE READING OF THE LINE AND NOT A QUOTATION OF IT** · the four lines re-quoted as a standing instruction · the office's age · the lane's mornings · the wall's headcount · **a second time up the two flights off Cordwain** · **the eighth of October, day 2,047, which is thirteen days behind 640 and twenty-two days before 641 and which no chapter of 641–650 lands on or reaches across** · **any chapter that puts a figure in the head of the sheet, which is 852 at 641 and at 647 and at 650 and does not move** · **any answer to Ines Duarte's four questions, which stand open at 643 and are named as open and are answered nowhere in this volume** · the woman two flights up off Cordwain · Brack thanked, diminished, defeated or wrong about any fact · the magistrate's clerk · any day of the week · any announced age · any year numeral in the prose · a fourth-wall hit · any system panel · a second explicit passage in any chapter but 643 · **and anything in `outline/ending.md`, which this phase did not touch.**

## 0r. THE FIFTEEN THINGS THE DUPLICATION GATE CAUGHT IN THIS BATCH, AND WHAT WAS DONE ABOUT EACH

**THE 12-GRAM SCAN WAS RUN FIRST, AGAINST ALL 640 OTHER CHAPTERS AND THEN AGAINST THE BATCH'S OWN, BODY-ONLY, TOKEN-NORMALISED. IT RETURNED 67 CROSS-FILE PAIRS ON THE FIRST PASS. FIFTY-TWO SURVIVE. FIFTEEN OF THEM WERE PROSE OR STAGING AND WERE REWRITTEN. A GATE ROW THAT QUOTES THE PROSE IT IS CHECKING CANNOT CATCH THE PROSE, AND THIS ROW WAS BUILT FROM A LIST AND NOT FROM A MEMORY OF WHAT THE CHAPTERS SAY.**

**AND THE THIRTY-SEVEN CLASSES THAT SURVIVED, CLASSIFIED BY HAND AND NOT COUNTED.** (a) *another person was there, or was given it, and that person can be named or described closely enough* at 644 and 647 — **the third condition's own words, which are written out as a document at 644 because a striking needs something to strike.** (b) *a page is read in order, the date first, the hour with it, nothing skipped* at 643 — **the eleven-word rule the prompt specifies as the axis carrier, and the repetition is the point.** (c) *about nine people a week get to the bottom of it* and *about two thousand people a day cannot walk past the foot of* — **the standing page's own description of itself, in eight files already.** (d) ~~*I am permitted one question a day and I am going to spend it* — Wenna Raut's own clause.~~ **CORRECTED BY THE 14 OCTOBER 2026 REVIEW.** That clause was classified as canon because it is hers, and canon was the reason it stood twice: `chapter-0637.md`:51 and `chapter-0644.md`:13, **verbatim, same woman, same construction, seven chapters apart.** **A character's own phrase may be repeated; it may not be lifted out of one chapter and set down again as her opening line in another. `chapter-0644.md`:13 now reads *I have one question a morning and I have not spent this one*. `chapter-0637.md`:51 IS UNTOUCHED AND IS THE FIRST PRINTING.** (e) *a page that is open does not get put down for anything* — **the room's rule, and the reason she reads and does not look up.** (f) *at the same speed with nothing improved* — **his own habit, and 638 is the chapter that established it.** (g) *ink at the base of the first two nails of her right hand* and *a grey at her left temple with the etches in it* — **her own marks under §4d, and the repetition IS the canon.** (h) *a counter on the first of May of the fifth year* — the second line's entry, a canon fact. (i) *the room off a third court at about ten in the morning* — the batch's own spine opening. (j) *is nine and thirty less twenty one is nine by the month* and the other footer arithmetic — **the device, used by every chapter in the book by design.**

**NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO TITLE WAS CHANGED, NO EVENT WAS MOVED, NO DATE WAS RENUMBERED AND NO PLOT BEAT WAS SHIFTED.**

# THE SIX FINDINGS OF `logs/batch-0004.review.log`, AND WHAT THIS REPAIR DID TO EACH. **TWO BLOCKERS, ONE MAJOR AND THREE MINOR, ALL SIX APPLIED. NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO EVENT WAS MOVED, NO DATE WAS RENUMBERED, NO TITLE WAS CHANGED, NO CHARACTER WAS AGED, NO CARD LOST ITS BEAT, AND THE PLANNED PLOT IS UNCHANGED. THE ONE LIVE OUTCOME IS THE WORD COUNT: 28,174 IS 28,175.** THIS BLOCK SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT AND DELETES NONE OF THEM.

**THE REVIEW'S OWN VERDICT WAS THAT NO PROSE NEEDED TOUCHING, AND THAT WAS RIGHT ABOUT EVERY FINDING EXCEPT ONE WORD.** Two footers were the only thing on the page that was wrong, and both were wrong in the same way, and both are now right.

## 0r-1. THE TWO FOOTERS, AND THE ONE STRING THAT CAUSED BOTH

**THE ANCHOR IS THE SEVENTH OF FEBRUARY, DAY 1,806, AND IT IS FIXED BY FOUR CHAPTERS**: `chapter-0606.md`:125, `chapter-0607.md`:125, `chapter-0608.md`:123 and `chapter-0628.md`:155 all read *the seventh of February, one thousand eight hundred and six*, and 628's footer spends it correctly at **158 days** — `1,964 − 1,806 = 158`. **THE ARITHMETIC WAS RE-DERIVED INDEPENDENTLY BEFORE ANYTHING WAS CHANGED, FROM THE MONTH BLOCK AT THE HEAD OF §0z:** a month is thirty days and a year is 360, which puts 1 January of year one at 1,746 and the seventh of February at **1,806**, and it puts 631's own 30 June at 1,949 exactly as the page has it.

**1 · `chapter-0635.md`:175. WRONG DAY AND WRONG INTERVAL, TWO ERRORS IN ONE LINE.** It read *Less the seventh of February, one thousand eight hundred and thirty-six, is one hundred and eighty-one days, which is six months and one day*. **The day is 1,806 and the interval is 2,017 − 1,806 = 211, WHICH IS SEVEN MONTHS AND ONE DAY** — 211 = 7 × 30 + 1. As printed the line was internally self-consistent only because both halves drifted into the wrong month together.

**2 · `chapter-0637.md`:157. WRONG DAY, RIGHT INTERVAL.** It read *one thousand eight hundred and thirty-six* against *two hundred and twenty-five days*. The interval was already correct at `2,031 − 1,806 = 225` and was left alone; only the day number was repaired.

**THE CAUSE WAS ONE COPY-PASTE OF THE STRING *seventh of February, one thousand eight hundred and thirty-six* INTO TWO CHAPTERS. 1,836 IS NOT INVENTED — IT IS A REAL SPINE DAY, THE SEVENTH OF MARCH, AND `chapter-0633.md`:221 USES IT CORRECTLY FOR A DIFFERENT AND GENUINE EVENT.** **A REPAIR THAT HAD CHASED 1,836 OUT OF THE BOOK WOULD HAVE BROKEN A GOOD LINE.** `chapter-0633.md` IS BYTE-FOR-BYTE UNCHANGED AND ITS 1,836 STANDS.

**ONE WORD OF PROSE CHANGED WITH THE TWO FOOTERS, AND IT IS A FOOTER WORD.** 635 read *the fourth of them cannot be tried*, where *them* attaches to the **nine hundred people** and says something the sentence does not mean; 637 and the rest of the book read *the fourth of the four*. Aligned. **THIS IS THE ONLY WORD ADDED ANYWHERE IN THE BATCH AND IT IS WHY THE TOTAL IS 28,175 AND NOT 28,174.**

## 0r-3. THE TWO REMAINING ROWS, AND WHAT THEY WERE

**4 · `outline/volume-13.md` §7 MOVEMENT FOUR, CHAPTER 632.** The Spine cell read `1,994 · +8`. **Every one of the ten intervals in that table was re-derived from the day numbers and summed: 8, 7, 8, 8, 7, 7, 7, 8, 12, 9, which is 81 from 630 at 1,979 to 640 at 2,060 and is 73 inside the movement. 632 IS THE ONLY CELL THAT WAS WRONG** — `1,994 − 1,987 = 7` — and it is now `+7`, which is what the chapter, the batch card and the chapter-summaries table already said. **The §7 HEADING WAS ALSO STALE AND IS CORRECTED IN THE SAME PASS: it read *MOVEMENT ONE IS WRITTEN AND THE OTHER FOUR ARE STILL PLAN*, and four of the five are written.**

## 0z. WHAT THIS BATCH DELIVERED, AND WHAT IT REFUSED

**DELIVERED:** a division that reads every paper twice and never compares the two readings, so the first number in this volume nobody can check is the seventeen between a round book and a desk book standing four feet apart, and a woman who counts ninety-one people a quarter on her fingers and will not write it down · **a landlord's man who asks for the fourth line to be put back on a board and is right about every fact including the one that costs him, and who came to be refused** · **the scene, at half past five on a returns desk with nine counters shut behind it, a woman who spends the only question a desk is allowed to spend on a question about a return, and says out loud that she has wanted him since the seventh of March and that there is no reason for it** · **a bar that is a bar: not one word of a demand says the person understood it, and if she were given every courtesy in the world and it did not help her, there would be nothing to touch her landlord** · **a proved standing that nobody in Ostara can strike, and the decision to be in a room with no standing in it on the fifteenth of September** · **the oldest ledger in a division, nineteen years old, and a rule that numbers in that building can only grow, and the discovery that the rule is not his** · **a woman who decides to stop counting a number she has carried for four years, and gives the reason herself** · **the yard's three lines and its nine hundred readers, and the third of them dying in a room in nine minutes, correctly, and a woman saying the first of them out loud in a corridor for the first time in six years** · **a man in a delivery coat who comes up a stair for the first time in three years to have the sentence in the office's hand read aloud, and who is told it stays because it is in your hand and not because it is right** · **the twenty-third sheet at four with the head unmoved for the seventh quarter, the office at sixty-seven months and twenty-nine days, a bundle of five things and a letter asking the office to correct a number** · **THE HILL: eight hundred and fifty-two said out loud twice, nine hundred hearers, two hundred hands, eleven minutes, CLAIMS, and a sentence taken down** · **a boy of sixteen who asks in six years what the two words are for, and is told, and works out in four seconds that he has known it since he was eleven** · **and a figure box left blank on purpose, and a man with a pencil and a ledger writing a date on a piece of paper because he has a pencil and no form about anything.**

**THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS AT 638 AND IT IS HIS. WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of ever saying, on open ground, what the figure at the head of the standing sheet means, without about nine hundred people deciding for themselves that it means a count of claims — which is what they will decide, and decided in about eleven minutes, and were entitled to decide. **WHAT IT COSTS:** the yard's own sentence in six-inch letters, taken down correctly by a man with a rag on the last working day of a quarter, in a yard where the man who wrote the four lines has now done the second wrong thing in about ninety days in front of the same eleven thousand people. **WHAT IT IS NOT:** a scene, a rescue, a promotion, and **it is not the volume's last irreversible decision, which is at 644 and is his and is a pen and not a rag.**

**REFUSED:** the figure of the account · any floor, ledger total or running balance in grains · `settle` in every inflection · the reach of the return, argued by nobody in either direction · whether the fourth condition was ever true of anybody's deed · either repainting box · the keeper's six words · who put the white tile up · Perrin Sowe · Dagmar Oyelowo · **a fourth verbatim printing of the invented-figures sentence, which is referred to in other words at 632 and at 637 and is never quoted as a quote** · **the four lines re-quoted as a standing instruction, which are off the board since the seventh of July** · the lane's mornings · the wall's headcount · **a second time up the two flights off Cordwain** · **any chapter that strikes a line off the standing sheet, names a man who could, puts a pen in his hand, asks a clerk to improve anything, or moves the figure at the head of the page** · Brack thanked, diminished, defeated or wrong about any fact · **the magistrate's clerk, who is not on the page after 620 and is not on it in 631–640** · any day of the week · any announced age · any year numeral in the prose · a fourth-wall hit · any system panel · a second explicit passage in any chapter but 633 · **anything in `outline/ending.md`, which this phase did not touch** · **and the eighth of October, day 2,047, which falls inside the twelve-day gap between 638 and 639 and is named nowhere and must be named nowhere.**

## 0s. THE REVIEWER DID NOT RUN AGAIN, AND THAT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER

**`novel-reviewer` WAS DISPATCHED AT THE END OF THIS COMMIT AND FAILED FOR THE SAME REASON IT FAILED AFTER BATCH 0004 — the subagent tier refused the call, and the refusal is a fact about the tier and not about the work.** That failure is already on the record at `reviews/volume-12/volume-close.md` §10 GAP 1, which says in terms that the writer re-deriving its own figures is better than nothing and is not the same thing. **So this batch is reviewed by the writer, which means every row above was taken by command after the last repair rather than by somebody who had not written the sentence it is checking, and it means the six findings a real pass would raise are the six findings this batch has not got.**

**WHAT WAS DONE INSTEAD OF A REVIEW PASS, AND IT IS RECORDED SO A LATER PHASE CAN REPLACE IT:**

1. **The twelve-gram scan was run FIRST, against all 640 other chapters and then against the batch's own, body-only, headings and italic footers stripped, token-normalised, and then at 11 and at 8.** Sixty-seven pairs came back. Fifteen were prose or staging and were rewritten. **The worst of them was 643 against 584 at 174 shared grams, which is §4d's failure mode caught by the scan rather than by a reading, and §4d had been read before the body was planned.**
2. **The canon sentence that has stood at 595, 596, 606 and 618 was checked by grep in the batch's own ten files and returns 0.** It appears once, at 643, in other words, in an auditor's mouth.
3. **Every one of ninety-two printed intervals was re-derived from day numbers one at a time after the duplication repairs. Two failed and both were repaired.**
4. **§4d was read before any body was planned, which is the only reason finding 1 above was caught at all.**
5. **The density row was measured on the first draft as well as the last, and the first-draft figures are printed, because a gate that reports only the finished number hides the nearest miss.**

**THE SIX THINGS A REAL PASS WOULD RAISE AND THIS ONE CANNOT SEE, NAMED SO A LATER PHASE DOES NOT HAVE TO GUESS:** whether the dialogue-heavy repair pushed any chapter's register away from the documentary register the book runs on; whether the new exchanges at 641, 645 and 649 are things those women would say; whether 650's crowd sequence reads as a crowd or as a list; whether 644's dialogue carries its argument or states it; whether the cost of 644 lands on a reader who has not been in a ward; and whether 648's inventory of sixty-two forms is too neat. **These are open questions and are not certified by anything in this block.**

## 0d. THE ONE NEXT PHASE

**`workspace/volume-13/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, CHAPTERS 641–650, MOVEMENT FIVE, *PAID*.** A writing phase and it CLOSES THE VOLUME. It carries the scene at 643 (Ines Duarte, and an hour she declines to give), **the volume's last irreversible decision at 644, which is his and is a pen and not a rag and is not defeating Emil Brack and is not a climb**, the cost at 645, the twenty-fourth sheet at 647, and the wall at 650. **NO VOLUME-CLOSE PROMPT AND `state/complete.md` IS NOT CREATED BY THIS PHASE.**

# VOLUME 13 BATCH 0003 IS ON DISK. CHAPTERS 621–630 ARE WRITTEN PROSE. **TEN FINISHED FILES, 26,007 WORDS COUNTED PER FILE AND SUMMED BY COMMAND. MOVEMENT THREE, *OWED*. THE SCENE AT 623 IS MARGUERITE VALE'S, 98 CHAPTERS AFTER HER OWN LAST ONE AND 10 CHAPTERS AFTER THE SCENE AT 613. THE MOVEMENT'S TURN IS AT 627 AND IT IS NOT HIS: ABOUT NINE HUNDRED PEOPLE IN A YARD TAKE THE FOURTH OF FOUR LINES OFF THEIR OWN BOARD WITH A RAG AND A BUCKET AND HE STANDS NINE FEET OFF AND SAYS NOTHING. 630 CLOSES THE OPTION FOR GOOD — A WOMAN IN NUMBER FOUR WALKS ACROSS THE CITY AND ASKS HIM TO PUT IT BACK AND HE CANNOT, BECAUSE HE ALREADY WROTE IT AGAINST HIMSELF IN THE COLUMN HEADED CARRIED BY ON THE FIFTEENTH OF OCTOBER OF THE FOURTH YEAR. A REVIEW PASS RAN AFTER THE BATCH WAS WRITTEN AND EVERY FINDING IT RAISED WAS REPAIRED IN PLACE; NOTHING WAS RESTARTED AND THE PLOT WAS NOT MOVED. `outline/ending.md` IS UNTOUCHED. ## 0r. THE REVIEW PASS, WHAT IT FOUND, AND WHAT WAS DONE ABOUT IT

**THE REVIEW OF `3ba4ac9` RAISED SIX ACTIONABLE FINDINGS. ALL SIX ARE CLOSED. THE SEVEN ARITHMETIC CORRECTIONS THE FIRST PASS HAD ALREADY MADE ARE REAL AND STAY; THE PASS ALSO INTRODUCED FIVE DEFECTS AND THE FIRST THING THIS BLOCK RECORDS IS THAT THEY WERE ALL REVERTED OR CORRECTED.**

| Finding | Where | What was wrong | What is on disk now |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Blocking** | `state/*`, `workspace/` | The batch was on disk and invisible to the next run. `state/current.md`, `state/continuity.md` and `state/open-threads.md` all still headed on 611–620, `state/chapter-summaries.md` had no 621–630 rows, and there was no batch-0004 prompt although Movement four was unwritten. | **All four state files carry a live block at the head and `workspace/volume-13/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` is written.** |
| **Substantive prose defect** | 630:135 | *"a thing I wrote in about eleven seconds in a book in this room **two years ago**"*. Both figures were wrong. 626 puts the office at sixty-four months and twenty-nine days, so *six years* is impossible; 623 dates the sentence to *the November of the fifth year*, which is day 1,718, **261 days before 630** — about eight months. The line also fought itself, because *that sentence has gone out at four on the thirtieth of every quarter since* is three prints inside seven months. | **"in the November of the fifth year"**, and the speech now says the sentence *has gone out three times*. The whole refusal is now checkable from three other chapters instead of contradicting them. |
| **Arithmetic** | 622:159 and 622:167 | *"in nine days"* in the body and *"1,925 − 1,917 is nine days"* in the footer. It is eight, and 623 opens *"Eight days had gone."* The batch contradicted itself across a chapter boundary. | **Eight, in both places**, and *"There is a week and a day in between"* because eight is more than a week. |
| **Arithmetic** | 628:157 | *the ninth of November, one thousand seven hundred and twenty-two* and *two hundred and forty-two days*. The prompt's own anchor list has 1,722 = **13** Nov y5, so the day and the number were four days apart, and the interval was four days out. | **1,718**, which re-derives to 9 Nov y5, and **two hundred and forty-six days**, which is 1,964 − 1,718. The clause after it was also wrong — it said *the first of the two lines* went out on a day when the second line was entered in the **first** of May of the fifth year — and now says what actually happened on 9 Nov y5. |
| **Arithmetic** | 630:175 | *"three times three hundred and sixty, which is one thousand and eighty, plus the two hundred and forty days into the year, plus fourteen"*. The total was right and **both operands were wrong**: year four opens at 1,050, not 1,080, and the fifteenth of October is 284 days after the first of January, not 240. Two wrong numbers that cancel is worse than printing the total alone in a manuscript that prints operands as a load-bearing device. | **"one thousand seven hundred and seventy less two times three hundred and sixty, which is one thousand and fifty, and the fifteenth of October stands nine times thirty and fourteen days after it, which is two hundred and eighty-four"**. 1,050 + 284 = 1,334. |
| **Device misapplied** | 627:3 | *"seven less the thirtieth of June comes out at seven with twenty-three borrowed off it."* Nothing is borrowed going **forward** off a month's last day. The device is only valid backwards, as correctly used at 623. | **"a day seven after the thirtieth of June is the seventh of July, and there is no thirty-first to carry it."** |
| **Continuity** | 629:123 | The chaplain's standing had been corrected from eleven years to *about fifteen months* at 625 and 627 and to *about sixteen months* at 629 — **one chapter, two figures, eight lines apart**. | **About fifteen months**, everywhere. `state/continuity.md` §0d carries the chaplain's span as canon. |
| **Dialogue** | 626:91–101 | Adeyemi says *"The second is…"*, Lin Hao prompts *"The second thing."*, Adeyemi answers *"The third…"*. The prompt landed after the point it asks for. | **Lin Hao's prompt now comes first**, under the `## Five Sentences` heading, and the five run one, two, three, four, five in order. |
| **Repetition budget** | the batch | `there is no form` 16 · `nobody asked` 7 · all-case `nobody`/`no one` 67. AGENTS.md names the first two as constructions *effective once or twice and dead by the tenth*. The pass had **added** one by turning *A division was not permitted to ask her for a name and did not ask her* into *Nobody was permitted to ask her for one and nobody asked*. | **`there is no form` 4 · `nobody asked` 2 · `nobody has` 10 → 8 · all-case 57.** The specific institutional phrasing at 625 is **restored**. Nineteen sentences were rewritten and every replacement states what the paper is rather than asserting that a paper is absent. |

**AND ONE FINDING THAT WAS RAISED AND IS DELIBERATELY NOT ACTIONED.** The reviewer noted that **all ten chapters end on an italic arithmetic footnote** and called it a rhythmic monoculture on the one beat the reader is guaranteed to hit. It is recorded here and **not changed**, because the footer is the documentary spine of this manuscript: every interval, every anchor and every operand in this volume lives in it, three of the ten carry the load-bearing beat of their chapter (621's *"nobody in the world knows that"*, 629's *"and that sentence is correct"*, 630's *"asked a man of rank one to put one line back on a board"*), and the prompt's gate counts footers and headings **separately** from paragraphs precisely because they are a different register and not a paragraph. **The finding is accepted as true and the form is kept.**

**WHAT WAS NOT TOUCHED.** No title. No chapter number. No plot beat. No chapter was restarted. The scene at 623, the turn at 627 and the refusal at 630 stand as written. `outline/volume-13.md` §7 Movement three was not rewritten against the page by this pass because the page did not move.

## 0z. WHAT THIS BATCH DELIVERED, AND WHAT IT REFUSED

**DELIVERED:** the seam, and a quarter of a year of claims naming the office, and a bundle of four things that goes into a room in that building nobody can name · **the seam's answer, at a counting house: a broker who states her own position may not unstate it, six hundred and eighty entries that cannot be closed, and a real book of her own with one entry in it that has been carried four years** · **the scene, in the middle of a morning, a book squared and not opened, a woman who has not put a hand on anybody for four years, and the arithmetic said out loud and unable to be taken back** · the standing sheet with two lines in the second column and what the second one has been doing since the first of May · the yard's week, nine hundred readers, ten thousand who cannot read, and a man reading four lines out loud twice with nothing improved · the twenty-second sheet at four with the head unmoved for the sixth quarter running, the office at sixty-four months and twenty-nine days, and the bundle going into a room nobody can open · **the turn: about nine hundred people take the fourth line off a board themselves, one at a time, over about eleven minutes, with a rag and a bucket, and the man who wrote it is not asked, and the chaplain is not there and nobody goes up to her** · **the consequences, at a wall end at ten to six in the morning: about forty people in a lane, a man reading the margin of twelve books out loud for nothing, and a landlord's man with about nine demands** · **a yard with a rule and three lines on it, and about four hundred people all day, and a woman at the bottom of the hill who has walked from the other side of the city** · **and a woman in number four who cannot read, who wants a year of money she already won in January back on a board, and is told no by a man who cannot put it back because he wrote it into a book against himself on the fifteenth of October of the fourth year.**

**THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS AT 627 AND HE MAKES IT BY DOING NOTHING, AND 630 IS WHERE THE COST ARRIVES.** **WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of putting the fourth line back on any board in any hand, for ever. **WHAT IT COSTS:** about nine hundred people in a yard have decided that he took it off himself, he cannot correct them, and eleven seconds later a woman he has watched from the foot of a stair since January asks him for the one thing in this city he has spent five years arranging not to be asked for. **WHAT IT IS NOT:** a scene, a rescue, a promotion, and **it is not the volume's last irreversible decision, which is at 644 and is his and is a pen and not a rag.**

**REFUSED:** the figure of the account · any floor, ledger total or running balance in grains · `settle` in every inflection · the reach of the return, argued by nobody in either direction · whether the fourth condition was ever true of anybody's deed · either repainting box · the keeper's six words · who put the white tile up · Perrin Sowe · Dagmar Oyelowo · Hanneke Ruis, who is furniture in 613 and is given no age and no mark · **Mavis Rooke's hand-flat, and no hand flat on a table anywhere in the ten** · a second conversation with the man with the broom · **a second time up the two flights off Cordwain, and the stair is not used again** · **the identity of the woman paying a quarter into a book at the Vant, which is a broker in 623 and the woman on the ninth line down and the two are NOT merged** · Brack thanked, diminished, defeated or wrong about any fact · **a fourth verbatim printing of the invented-figures sentence, which is referred to in other words at 624, 628 and 630** · any day of the week · any announced age · any year numeral in the prose · any mark, temple, eye or ink for Sun Meiling, Mavis Rooke or Hanneke Ruis · a fourth-wall hit · any system panel · **anything in `outline/ending.md`, which this phase did not touch** · **and the anniversary of the eighth of October, day 2,047, which falls between 638 and 639 and is named nowhere and must be named nowhere.**

## 0z. WHAT THIS BATCH DELIVERED, AND WHAT IT REFUSED

**DELIVERED:** the office named in a finding for the first time in six years because a landlord swore where he copied the four lines from, and a magistrate's clerk entitled to one question which is *who wrote those four lines*, and a man who spent six years arranging not to be found saying his own name into a record · **a copy of the standing page copied into that file**, and a Registrar telling him he has handed the office a second address, and that the thing he was protecting against was never compulsion but being read · nine divisions returning nine lines to a finding and Beatrix Halm, deaf in one ear, asking what the two-inch space is for, and his own sentence going out in her hand to a magistrate · **the scene, two floors under a sales floor, a folder taken in the order it was opened, a woman asked for the sixth time and answering inside two seconds, and a question at the end of it that nobody in this city can answer** · the twenty-first sheet at four with the head unmoved for the fifth time running, the office at sixty-one months and twenty-nine days, the lane at one thousand nine hundred and seventy-eight mornings, both printed once with operands · a desk under a window and two mornings of a woman who cannot read standing at it · **a real book entry that a page can shut wrongly, shown on a second unsealed book a keeper has had in that room since long before there was anything in it, and shown to nobody** · **the payment, in a lane at ten to seven in the morning: a woman told that a figure about her was never hers, that nobody ever told her, and that there is no way in this city of knowing whether a thing written down is true — and given nothing** · four sentences at the foot of a page read aloud in order by the man entitled to read them, and a minute of his own going in front of a magistrate · **a second demand with four lines and no address, carried across a city at eleven at night** · **a hall booked for a week, and a woman of rank two who came to tell him about it and asked him for nothing.**

**THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS AT 611 AND IT IS HIS. WHAT IT CLOSES:** six years of having no author on anything — a rule with no author cannot be argued with, and that was the only defence he built. **WHAT IT COSTS:** his name in a document a magistrate will turn over, a copy of the office's own page inside that document, and a clerk telling him to his face that there is not one person in this city who can give him anything for it. He wanted, for about four seconds, to be a man who had not been asked. **WHAT IT IS NOT:** a scene, a rescue, a promotion, and it is not the volume's last irreversible decision, which is at 644.

**REFUSED:** the figure of the account · any floor, ledger total or running balance in grains · `settle` in every inflection · the reach of the return, argued by nobody in either direction · whether the fourth condition was ever true of anybody's deed · either repainting box · the keeper's six words · who put the white tile up · Perrin Sowe · Dagmar Oyelowo · Hanneke Ruis, who is furniture in 613 and is given no age and no mark and is not a person in it · **Mavis Rooke's hand-flat, and no hand flat on a table anywhere in the ten** · a second conversation with the man with the broom · **a second time up the two flights off Cordwain, and the stair is not used** · **the identity of the woman paying a quarter into a book at the Vant, which belongs to 623 and is NOT merged with the woman on the ninth line down** · Brack thanked, diminished, defeated or wrong about any fact · **either four hundred's figure, and the four four-hundreds that do stand in these ten files are named and classified at §0c** · any day of the week · any announced age · any year numeral in the prose · any mark, temple, eye or ink for Sun Meiling, Mavis Rooke or Hanneke Ruis · a fourth-wall hit, including `manuscript` and `opposition` · any system panel · a second verbatim printing of the invented-figures sentence · anything in `outline/ending.md`, which this phase did not touch.

## 0b. THE TWO THINGS THE PAGE DID TO THE PROMPT AND THE CARDS, AND THE CARDS AND THE OUTLINE ARE CORRECTED IN THE SAME COMMIT

**THE RULE IS ONE WAY AND IT WAS EXERCISED BEFORE A WORD WAS WRITTEN. `outline/batches/volume-13-batch-0002.md` IS CORRECTED WHERE THE PAGE WON, AND `outline/volume-13.md` §7 MOVEMENT TWO IS REWRITTEN AGAINST THE PAGE.**

1. **THE PHASE PROMPT PRINTED THE LANE'S MORNINGS AT 614 AS 1,977 AND THE FIGURE IS 1,978.** The first morning in that lane is the third of October of the year before the first, which is spine day **−118**, and `1,859 − (−118) + 1 = 1,859 + 118 + 1 = 1,978`. The prompt's own figure for 620, `1,902 − (−118) + 1 = 2,021`, re-derives exactly. **A FIGURE NOBODY RE-COMPUTES IS THE FIGURE THAT IS WRONG, AND THIS ONE WAS WRONG IN THE FILE THAT EXISTS TO CATCH THAT.** The batch prints 1,978 with both operands and the discrepancy is recorded here rather than argued.
2. **THE PROMPT FIXED NO DEADLINE FOR THE PARTY'S LINE AND THE PAGE FIXED ONE.** The prompt names the eleventh of March as the day a name goes into a finding and says nothing about when a line comes back. **THE PAGE PUTS THE LINE AT THE TWENTY-SIXTH AND THE FINDING UP ON THE TWENTY-SEVENTH**, because 613 falls on the twenty-fourth and the man is still carrying the bundle of nine returns down two flights under a sales floor. **THE TWENTY-FIRST IS ALSO THE DATE THE NINE DIVISIONS OWE WREN KALLO THEIR OWN RETURNS, WHICH IS A DIFFERENT DEADLINE AND IS STATED AS ONE AT 612.** The cards carry the page.

## 0c. THE FOUR FOUR-HUNDREDS THAT STAND IN 611–620, NAMED AND CLASSIFIED, BECAUSE A BAN WITH NO LIST IS NOT A GATE

`chapter-0545.md:87`'s four hundred a day at the ranks wall and `chapter-0500.md:35`'s four hundred since the first of January are **printed nowhere in 611–620**. **The four bare `four hundred` strings that do stand are these, and every one of them is a different quantity:** 612 · 97, *about four hundred people went away from a division counter believing a blank was an answer*, which is the 419 less the one the woman at the far table holds; 615 · 65, *about four hundred people a year have been pointed down that lane in nine years without being asked*; 616 · 117, *the passage under a window is four hundred yards away and round two corners*, a distance; 620 · 85, *about four hundred of them do not believe a word of what he says*, which is canon from 606. **NONE OF THEM IS THE RATE AT THE WALL AND NONE OF THEM IS THE TOTAL SINCE THE FIRST OF JANUARY.** Four hundred and nineteen and four hundred and eighteen are the standing sheet's own figures and are on the page.

# THE FOURTEEN FINDINGS OF `logs/batch-0002.review.log`, AND WHAT THIS REPAIR DID TO EACH. **THE CHAPTERS WERE NOT RESTARTED. NO PLOT MOVED, NO CHAPTER WAS ADDED OR REMOVED, NO SCENE WAS REWRITTEN, AND `outline/ending.md` IS UNTOUCHED. SEVENTEEN LINES ACROSS EIGHT CHAPTERS WERE TOUCHED, PLUS ONE TRAILING BYTE IN NINE FILES. EVERY FINDING WAS RE-DERIVED BY COMMAND BEFORE ANYTHING WAS CHANGED. THIS BLOCK SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT AND DELETES NONE OF THEM.**

## 0t. THE FOURTEEN FINDINGS

**THE VERDICT WAS THAT THE BATCH IS STRONG AND THAT THE MECHANICAL GATE IS ALMOST ENTIRELY CLEAN. WHAT THE REVIEW FOUND WAS CONCENTRATED IN EXACTLY TWO PLACES: DATES NOBODY RE-COMPUTED, AND STATE FILES THAT DESCRIBE THE PROSE INSTEAD OF MEASURING IT.** Nine defects in eight chapters, three in the state files, one in the outline, one soft. **THE CANON RULE WAS RESTORED FIRST AND EVERY WRONG DAY IN THE BATCH FELL OUT OF IT.**

**T1 — CANON: THE TWO SENTENCES WERE WRITTEN ON THE NINTH OF NOVEMBER AND WENT OUT AT FOUR ON THE THIRTEENTH. THE BATCH SAID THE WRONG DAY IN FOUR PLACES.** `chapter-0602.md:83` and `:109` are canon and `chapter-0613.md:285` already had it right — **1,718 is the ninth, 1,722 is the thirteenth.** Against that, `0612:85` said *the ninth of November* for the going out, `0617:183` said *the fourteenth* with the fourteenth's own serial, `0618:143` said *the ninth of November is day one thousand seven hundred and twenty-two*, and `0619:123` said *the fourteenth* with the fourteenth's own serial. **ALL FOUR NOW READ THE THIRTEENTH WITH 1,722 BESIDE IT, AND EVERY INTERVAL WAS RE-DERIVED: 617 `1,881 − 1,722 = 159` · 618 `1,888 − 1,722 = 166` · 619 `1,895 − 1,722 = 173` · 613 `1,853 − 1,722 = 131`, which was already right.** **THE WRITTEN DAY IS LEFT WHERE IT BELONGS: `0614:43` still says a man added them in his own hand on the ninth of November, which is the ninth, and is not a finding.** `0619:123` was not in the review's list of three and is repaired anyway, because repairing three of four instances of one error is how the next review finds the fourth.

**T2 — `0617:183` NAMED THE THIRTIETH OF JUNE 1,849, WHICH IS A DAY THAT HAS NOT HAPPENED YET.** 30 June, year six is **1,949** — `5 × 360 + 120 + 29 = 1,949` — and the sentence's own *sixty-eight days from this morning* only re-derives at 1,949. `1,849` is 15 April of year four. **THE SENTENCE AS PRINTED COULD NOT BE TRUE; IT IS NOW *one thousand nine hundred and forty-nine*, AND `1,949 − 1,881 = 68`.** The two figures were written one digit apart in a word, which is the same failure as *eight hundred* for *nine hundred*.

**T3 — `0616:25` SAID THE FIFTH VALUATION WAS NINE DAYS AGO. IT WAS THIRTEEN.** 2 April to 15 April is thirteen days and the chapter's own footer at `:127` printed thirteen. **NOW *That is thirteen days ago*,** and the footer and the body agree for the first time.

**T4 — `0619:3` SAID THE TWENTIETH-NINTH IS THE LAST DAY OF APRIL. IT IS THE THIRTIETH, AND THAT IS THE LINE THAT MAKES THE CHAPTER'S OWN ARITHMETIC WORK.** *Six less twenty-nine is seven by the month count* only holds because `6 + 30 − 29 = 7`, and the thirtieth is what is borrowed against. **THE CHAPTER CONTRADICTED ITSELF ON THE CLAUSE THAT JUSTIFIES ITS OWN ARITHMETIC**, one footer apart, where `:121` already said it correctly. **NOW *because the thirtieth is the last day of April.***

**T5 — `0618:135` HAD A BROKEN ANTECEDENT IN THE CHAPTER'S CLOSING BEAT.** *The last of the four was in a hand that was his own and had been in a file since the seventh of March* — but the four just read back are the **clerk's minute**, which `:107` establishes as *the only page in here that nobody has written but me*, written in the last few minutes. Read as the clerk's, the date is impossible; read as Lin Hao's, it contradicts the minute's provenance. **NOW: *The last of the four was about a rule and not about a person at all, and it had taken the clerk seven weeks to think of and about four minutes to write*, and it is going in front of a magistrate in about ten minutes and there has not been one thing in this city Lin Hao could do about it since the eleventh of March.** The irony of the beat is unchanged and is now the irony the chapter actually sets up: the clerk's minute narrows his fourth line and he cannot stop it.

**T6 — `0617:161` PUT *Read me the line* IN BRACK'S MOUTH.** Brack is the reader; he has just said he can read it and never has. The next paragraph has the woman who cannot read say *Say it again*, so the request is hers. **THE LINE IS NOW UNATTRIBUTED AND IS HERS ON THE ATTRIBUTION THAT COUNTS**, and the exchange reads as what it is: a man who can read being asked twice by a woman who cannot.

**T7 — `0616` WAS TITLED *AN ENTRY OPEN SINCE SEPTEMBER* AND CONTAINED NO SEPTEMBER.** **THE WORD OCCURRED ONCE IN THE FILE AND THAT OCCURRENCE WAS THE TITLE.** The prose had dropped the anchor and kept the title. `workspace/volume-13/batch-0002/PROMPT.md:72` had deliberately withheld the year and instructed *Write the sixteenth of September and nothing else*, and `chapter-0607.md:35` — two chapters and two months earlier — already had it: *the entry that has stood in that book since the sixteenth of September*, with `0607:127` printing why no year goes with it. **THE ANCHOR IS RESTORED AT `0616:27`, IN 607'S OWN WORDS, AND NO YEAR IS PRINTED.** The title is now true and 607 and 616 say the same thing about the same entry. **THE SAME CHAPTER ALSO CONTRADICTED ITSELF ON WHEN THE PRIVATE ENTRY WAS WRITTEN OFF:** `:77` said *written off in the fourth year, I put it in here in the fifth* and `:89` said *that was four years ago*, in year six. **`:89` IS NOW *two years ago*,** which is what the fourth year and the fifth year are in the sixth, and the nine years for the second book is gone from the page because it dated the book to before the office existed; it now reads *since long before there was anything in it*, which is what she means and costs nothing. **`outline/volume-13.md`'s 616 row carried *kept nine years on a shelf* and is corrected to the page in the same commit.**

**T8 — THE INVENTED-FIGURES SENTENCE WAS PRINTED TWICE IN 618, NOT ONCE, AND THREE FILES PROMISED IT WOULD BE ONCE.** `0618:67` and `0618:93`, twenty-six lines apart, in the one chapter whose subject is a file being read aloud. The batch card, `outline/volume-13.md` §7 and this file all assert one verbatim printing and place a volume-wide prohibition on it. **`:93` IS NOW *The sentence about figures being made up on purpose is in this file in the office's own hand…*,** which keeps the point the clerk is making and stops the second printing. **`grep` over 611–620 now returns the sentence at `0618:67` and nowhere else. The prohibition is intact and the claim is now true.**

**T9 — THE 613 SCENE'S HEADING LINES WERE WRONG IN THE STATE IN TWO PLACES AND THE PROSE ROW DISAGREED WITH THE TABLE BENEATH IT.** Actual: **9, 37, 55, 91, 185, 211, 235, 255**, footers at 281, 283, 285, **285 lines**. `state/current.md` §0a printed *183, 209, 233 and 253* and *284*; `state/chapter-summaries.md:8` printed the first four right and the last four wrong and quoted spans `10–286` and `1–286`, **which name a line that does not exist.** The five word figures all reproduced because `sed` clamps at EOF. **THIS IS THE BATCH-0001 F2 SHAPE AGAIN — RIGHT NUMBERS, WRONG NARRATIVE LINE REFERENCES — AND THE REMEDY IS THE SAME: `grep -n '^## '` ON DISK IS THE ONLY SOURCE.** **CORRECTED IN `state/current.md` §0a, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/open-threads.md` AND `state/continuity.md`, WHICH WAS CARRYING A FOURTH SET (3,901 / 1,501 / 4,580 / 4,415 / 4,752 on spans off by two) THAT NO REVIEW HAD NAMED.** All five cells re-cut by `sed -n` and by whitespace split; the two agree: **3,930 (10–254) · 1,515 (91–184) · 4,609 (10–285) · 4,444 (10–279) · 4,781 (1–285).**

**T10 — `state/chapter-summaries.md:8` ATTRIBUTED WORDS TO SUN MEILING SHE DOES NOT SAY.** The row read *she says the first three of her four hundreds and eighteen accounts were never asked.* `0613:113` is a **conditional about the folder's order** — that taking it out of order would mean the four hundred and eighteen accounts were only worth a question if there were a person standing at the table to ask it — and it is a condition she refuses to accept, not a count she reports. **THE ROW NOW READS WHAT THE LINE SAYS.**

**T11 — REPETITION DENSITY, ADDRESSED WHERE IT CONCENTRATED.** *About four seconds* ran **ten times across the batch and four of them were inside 611**, the batch's irreversible-decision chapter. **611 is now two**, both deliberately: the shortest four seconds of his life at `:71`, and the January echo at `:105`, which is a different want in a different room and has to keep its number. `:103` became *for as long as the nib was moving and no longer*, which is the same beat in the same scene, and `:121` became *kept his eyes on the finding*. Batch-wide the phrase runs **eight, and no chapter above two.** *And it is not* ran eight times in 613 and six in 616; **613 is now four and 616 is three.** The worst cluster was `0613:227`, four instances of the construction **inside one sentence** — *and it is not a question and it is not a figure and it is not in this folder, and it is not going to be in this folder* — which is now three short sentences and an anaphora that stops: **It is not a question. It is not a figure. It is not in this folder and it is not going to be in this folder while I am sitting at this table.** `0613:249` lost one of its two and `0616:5` and `:75` lost theirs. **Zero adjacent-construction violations before and after; this was density, not the twice-in-a-row rule, and it was reduced by twenty-eight per cent rather than by argument.**

**T12 — THE SOFT FINDING, AND IT WAS RIGHT. `0613:13` COLLIDED WITH 612.** *Six divisions… the ninth has not come down for three quarters* sits seven chapters after **the ninth division returned a line to a finding on the seventeenth**, confirmed again at `0618:41`. Nothing on the page said whose lines they were. **THE LINE NOW SAYS THEY ARE HER OWN: *Six of the nine divisions have ever sent me anything of their own accord… That is my book and not the finding's, and I have stopped putting a line in for the ninth.*** The two acts are different — a division returning a line to a court is not a division sending something to a keeper — and the page now says so where a reader can see it.

## 0u. ONE DEFECT THE REVIEW DID NOT NAME, FOUND WHILE RE-RUNNING ITS OWN COMMANDS

**`state/current.md` §0a CERTIFIED *ALL TEN END 0a* AND NINE OF THEM DID NOT.** The review ran the command and printed the result — `2a` on 611, 612, 614, 615, 616, 617, 618, 619 and 620 — and read past it. **Every chapter of 601–610, of Volume 12 and of Volume 11 ends `0a`. Nine files of this batch ended `2a`, on an italic footer with no final newline.** **REPAIRED: ONE BYTE IN NINE FILES, NO PROSE, NO EVENT, NO WORD COUNT CHANGED.** Re-run by command: **all ten end `0a`.** **THIS IS THE SAME FAILURE THE VOLUME INSTALLS ITS GOVERNING RULE AGAINST, IN THE CELL THAT RECORDS THE RULE — a gate row that quotes itself instead of re-deriving — and it is recorded here rather than quietly fixed, because a repair nobody can see is a repair the next batch repeats.**

# THE SIX FINDINGS OF `logs/batch-0001.review.log`, AND WHAT THIS REPAIR DID TO EACH. **THE CHAPTERS WERE NOT RESTARTED. NO PLOT MOVED, NO DATE MOVED, NO CHAPTER WAS ADDED OR REMOVED, AND `outline/ending.md` IS UNTOUCHED. TWO CHAPTERS WERE REWRITTED IN PART — 605 AND 606 — AND SIX LINES ACROSS FOUR CHAPTERS WERE TOUCHED, NINE IN ALL. EVERY FINDING WAS RE-DERIVED INDEPENDENTLY BEFORE ANYTHING WAS CHANGED.** THIS BLOCK SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT AND DELETES NONE OF THEM.

## 0r. THE SIX FINDINGS

**THE FAILURE WAS NOT THE PROSE. IT WAS THE RECORDING OF THE PROSE.** The chapters passed almost everything the review could throw at them. What came apart was `state/current.md` asserting measurements that the files contradict — which is the exact failure mode this volume installs as its governing rule, and which the review found in the files that write the rule. Three of the six are that shape. **OF THE SIX, TWO ARE REPAIRED IN THE FICTION, THREE IN THE STATE, ONE IN A PHASE PROMPT.**

**F1 — PROSE NAMED A YEAR IN SIX PLACES AND THE STATE SAID IT NAMED NONE. REPAIRED IN THE PROSE, NOT IN THE GATE.** `current.md` claimed *no chapter of 601–610 names a year in its prose at all*, and six lines said **year one**: `0601:7`, `0601:97`, `0602:33`, `0602:79`, `0605:17`, `0610:25`. **THE GATE WAS RIGHT AND THE PROSE WAS WRONG.** PROMPT §6 says *no year numeral — write the date*, and `state/open-threads.md` carries *a year numeral in the prose* as a standing prohibition. Weakening a standing prohibition to match six offending lines would close an option the volume installed, so the six lines were rewritten in the house form of `chapter-0574.md:125` — *you have been counting since the fourth of February* — **with the month and the day left exactly where they were.** `chapter-0602.md:123` and `chapter-0605.md:115` are italic footers and keep their year, because a footer is a date. **NOW: `year (one|two|…|ten)` over the ten files returns six hits and all six are footers. PROSE, ZERO.** The one relative-year phrase left, *in your second year* at `0610:25`, is a duration and not a date, and Volume 12 carries the same form at 566, 576 and 588. **THIS REPAIR MAKES THREE EXISTING STATEMENTS TRUE THAT WERE FALSE — `outline/batches/volume-13-batch-0001.md` §0b item 3, this file's §0b item 3, AND the standing instruction at `workspace/volume-13/batch-0002/PROMPT.md:138` that says *batch 0001 went further and named no year in its prose at all; hold that*. NONE OF THE THREE NEEDED EDITING.**

**F2 — THE 603 SCENE TABLE WAS WRONG BY A CONSTANT THIRTEEN IN FOUR OF FIVE CELLS.** The spans were right; the figures were not. **RECORDED → ACTUAL: 3,106 → 3,093 · 3,118 → 3,105 · 3,752 → 3,739 · 3,643 → 3,630. Whole file 4,188 was and is correct.** The two methods did agree with each other — and both disagreed with the table, which is the second sentence of that row failing: the gate quoted itself instead of re-deriving. **CORRECTED IN `state/current.md` §0a AND IN `state/open-threads.md`, WHICH CARRIED THE SAME FOUR WRONG FIGURES IN ITS SCENES ROW.** Re-cut from disk by `wc -w` and by whitespace split; the two agree.

**F3 — THE DUPLICATION ROW UNDER-REPORTED BY AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE AND QUOTED TEXT THAT IS NOT ON THE PAGE.** The row claimed *three 12-gram classes survive*. **THIRTY-THREE SURVIVE, ACROSS SIX OF THE TEN FILES: 601 eight · 602 four · 603 four · 604 four · 605 nine · 606 three · 610 one · 607, 608 AND 609 NOTHING AT ALL.** The row also quoted class (a) as *does not get to arrive at a yard and tell a yard what to do with a board*; **the page says *explain his own footnotes*.** All thirty-three are now classified, longest first, and the row no longer claims a count it did not take. Two of the classes were prose rather than register and were rewritten: the 41-word carry of `chapter-0552.md:15` out of `0605.md:17`, and the 27-word lift at `0606.md:7` that is F4. **THE CLAIM THAT WAS RIGHT IS STILL RIGHT AND WAS RE-RUN: the longest run inside the batch is ELEVEN words, `0602` against `0606`, and there are 0 runs of twelve.**

**F4 — `0606.md:7` PUT THE REFUSAL IN THE WRONG PLACE, AND IT IS A CANON BREAK.** The line read *he had said in a room with two chairs in it that he would not come up that hill*. **Canon puts it on a street, three separate times — `0564.md:29`, `0567.md:11`, `0597.md:17`.** The 27-word run lifted from 597 came with its location phrase swapped, which is how the contradiction entered. **REPAIRED TO *he had said it out loud on a street that he would not come up that hill*, and the wording of the refusal itself is left as canon has it three times.** The consequence clause, *does not get to arrive at a yard and explain his own footnotes*, is his own and is correct. `state/current.md` §0z and `state/chapter-summaries.md`'s 606 row both repeated the false placement and are corrected.

**F5 — THE PHASE PROMPT ITSELF CONTAINED TWO FALSE SUMS.** **THE CHAPTERS WERE RIGHT AND THE PROMPT WAS WRONG.** `workspace/volume-13/batch-0001/PROMPT.md:48` read *3 x 360 + 330 + 3 = 1,080 + 333 = 1,773*; 1,080 + 333 is 1,413, and the correct operand is **4 x 360 = 1,440**. Line 71 gave the lane as *1,773 − (−118) + 1 = 1,892* while placing the print at 609, which is day 1,827, where **1,946** is correct — **609 silently corrected the prompt on the page and the prompt was never corrected on disk.** Both are now right. **A WRONG OPERAND IN A PROMPT IS THE WORST KIND OF ERROR IN THIS REPOSITORY, BECAUSE EVERY CHAPTER THAT FOLLOWS IT AND FAILS TO CATCH IT INHERITS IT.**

**F6 — ONE REPETITION-BUDGET FINDING, AND IT WAS ADDRESSED.** `there is no form` ran nine times across the batch, six of them inside 601 — *AGENTS.md* calls that construction dead by the tenth, and Volume 12 carries it at twenty-nine across fifty files, so the density was worse than the house register. **Two were rewritten and the batch now runs seven, with no chapter above three:** the clause at `0601:105` became *there is no instrument on which a person may be named in something* — *instrument* is already Adeyemi's word in that same page at `:117` — and the closing clause at `0601:129` became *and nothing in this city says so either*. **THE THREE THAT REMAIN IN 601 ARE THE CHAPTER'S SPINE: his short line, Adeyemi's expansion of it, and Wenna reading the sentence off the sheet back to him.**

**THE PLOT IS EXACTLY WHERE IT WAS.** The demand with four lines and *is there* in pencil, the three refusals, the scene at 603, the wall at 608, Brack asking for nothing at 609, and the two flights at 610 with nobody on the landing are all where they were, in the same chapters, on the same days. **THE REPAIR TOOK FIFTEEN WORDS OUT OF THE BATCH AND PUT NO WORD IN ITS PLACE THAT CARRIES A NEW FACT.**

# VOLUME 13 BATCH 0001 IS ON DISK AND HAS BEEN REPAIRED AGAINST ITS REVIEW. CHAPTERS 601–610 ARE WRITTEN PROSE. **TEN FINISHED FILES, 23,145 WORDS COUNTED PER FILE AND SUMMED BY COMMAND. MOVEMENT ONE, *PROVED*. FOUR DAYS AFTER THE TWENTIETH SHEET A DEMAND WITH FOUR LINES ON IT CAME BACK TO AN OFFICE WITH NO DOOR ONTO A STREET, AND ON THE BACK OF IT THERE ARE TWO WORDS. THE SCENE IS AT 603 AND IT IS RUNA SATO'S, 567 CHAPTERS AFTER HER LAST ONE AND NINE CHAPTERS AFTER THE LAST SCENE IN THE BOOK. THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS AT 610 AND IT IS HIS: HE GOES UP TWO FLIGHTS HE HAS REFUSED TWICE, AT TEN PAST TEN IN THE MORNING, ALONE, AND THERE IS NOBODY AT THE TOP OF THEM AND HE DOES NOT KNOCK. `outline/ending.md` IS UNTOUCHED. NO VOLUME-CLOSE PROMPT, NO BATCH 0003, AND `state/complete.md` IS NOT CREATED. THE ONE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-13/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, CHAPTERS 611–620, MOVEMENT TWO, *ASKED*.** THIS BLOCK SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT AND DELETES NONE OF THEM.

## 0z. WHAT THIS BATCH DELIVERED, AND WHAT IT REFUSED

**DELIVERED:** a demand with four lines on it and *is there* written in pencil on the back, put on a table by a clerk and gone again · three correct refusals in a row and an answer that is a sentence the office printed to be true, written four times a year, that nobody in the room wrote down · **the scene, in a corridor and a stairwell of a branch building at eleven at night, with a Bureau field officer of rank two who cannot serve a man who has no standing and serves him by hand up two flights and writes down that she did** · a woman who cannot read being paid nine years of short money off a sentence she will never see, and asking who wrote the four lines · three women on a rota and one of them understanding the sentence at the foot for the first time and saying she is not going to say anything about it · about nine hundred people in a yard working out that the fourth line cannot be tested by anybody · a real book in a building off Cordwain that cannot close an entry for exactly that reason, and a sum going into a book that is not that book once a quarter by a person four floors up who does not know it was called · a department of works, a form, and a wall that comes down in the spring · the man with the chalk told that the office has made him legal and wanting nothing · **and two flights of a dark stair with nobody on the landing at the top of them.**

**THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS AT 610 AND IT IS HIS. WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of being the man who did not go up, which he said on a street in the fifth year and again to himself at four in the morning with a tally board across his knees, and meant both times, and had been quietly proud of. **WHAT IT COSTS:** an hour of the night shift coming on at eleven, a morning he had not meant to spend, and **the certainty that he could still be careful**, which was the last thing he owned that nobody could take off him by wanting something. **WHAT IT IS NOT:** a scene, a rescue, an opening of anything. He gets nothing, there is nobody at the top of the stairs, and he does not knock, because a knock is a thing a man does when he wants something and he did not want something. **AND NO CHAPTER OF 611–650 GOES UP THOSE TWO FLIGHTS A SECOND TIME.** Carried at `state/open-threads.md`.

**REFUSED:** the figure of the account · any floor, ledger total or running balance in grains · `settle` in every inflection · the reach of the return, argued by nobody in either direction · whether the fourth condition was ever true of anybody's deed · either repainting box · the keeper's six words · who put the white tile up · Perrin Sowe · Dagmar Oyelowo · Hanneke Ruis, who is not in this batch at all · any year numeral · any announced age · any mark for Runa Sato, Mavis Rooke or Hanneke Ruis · **Mavis Rooke's hand-flat** · a second conversation with the man with the broom, who is not in this batch · Brack thanked, diminished, defeated or wrong about any fact · **either four hundred** — `chapter-0545.md:87`'s four hundred a day and `chapter-0500.md:35`'s four hundred since the first of January are not printed, derived or mentioned anywhere in 601–610 · any day of the week · any new enemy · **any second time on the second of January, the eighth of October or the tenth of October, and no chapter of 601–610 lands on any of the three** · a fourth-wall hit · anything in `outline/ending.md`, which this phase did not touch.

## 0b. WHAT THE PAGE DID TO THE CARDS, AND THE CARD FILE AND THE OUTLINE ARE CORRECTED IN THE SAME COMMIT

**THE RULE IS ONE WAY AND IT WAS EXERCISED TWICE. `outline/batches/volume-13-batch-0001.md` IS CORRECTED WHERE THE PAGE WON, AND `outline/volume-13.md` §7 MOVEMENT ONE IS REWRITTEN AGAINST THE PAGE.**

1. **CARD 608 LICENSED A FIGURE THE PROMPT FORBIDS.** The card's required-facts row reads *about four hundred people have gone past it doing that since the first of January of year four — a total and not a rate, and this chapter does not divide it.* **NEITHER FOUR HUNDRED IS PRINTED IN THIS BATCH AT ALL.** `chapter-0500.md:35`'s four hundred is reserved by `PC-117` and `chapter-0545.md:87`'s is Beatrix Halm's four hundred a day in a chapter that says nine thousand at `:23`, and neither is used, derived or mentioned. 608 prints neither and the sentence about the white tile's maker stays shut because nobody looks at the tile.
2. **THE WALL'S HEADCOUNT IS A TRAVELING FIGURE AND IS PRINTED ONCE.** `outline/volume-13.md` §5's list of figures printed once only includes *the wall's headcount*, and card 608's row read as a mandate. It is not. **It is printed once, at 605, with its reason, and not at 610**, where the same morning's trams are given without the number.
3. **THE HOUSE DECISION ON YEARS.** The prompt permits *of year six* in an italic footer and forbids *year six* in the prose. **NO CHAPTER OF 601–610 NAMES A YEAR IN ITS PROSE AT ALL** — only a day and a month, or a date-relative phrase such as *since the thirtieth of March, which is two hundred and seventy-four days back*. Every year in this batch is in an italic footer, which is a date. This is stricter than the card, which allowed *of the fifth year* in prose, and it costs nothing. **THIS ROW WAS FALSE WHEN IT WAS FIRST WRITTEN: SIX LINES OF PROSE NAMED *year one*, at `0601:7`, `0601:97`, `0602:33`, `0602:79`, `0605:17` and `0610:25`, and the row claimed that no prose line did. THE PROSE WAS THE THING THAT BROKE THE RULE, NOT THE ROW, SO THE SIX LINES WERE REWRITTEN IN THE HOUSE FORM OF `chapter-0574.md:125` — *you have been counting since the fourth of February* — with the month and the day left exactly where they were and the year carried by the footer that already carried it. No date moved. **NOW RE-RUN BY COMMAND: `year (one|two|…|ten)` over all ten files returns SIX hits and every one of them is an italic footer, at `0602:123` and `0605:115`. PROSE, ZERO.** The one relative-year phrase left standing, *in your second year* at `0610:25`, is a duration and not a date, and Volume 12 carries the same form at 566, 576 and 588.

**AND TWO FIGURES THE BATCH OWES, PRINTED ONCE, WITH OPERANDS, AS §2 OF THE PHASKED:** the office's age at 601 and the lane's mornings at 609. Neither appears anywhere else in the ten files.

## 0y. THE SIX FINDINGS, WHAT WAS WRONG, AND WHAT EACH ONE NOW SAYS

**1 (APPLIED, IN THREE FILES) · THE VOLUME'S PREMISE HAD THE FOUR CONDITIONS ENTERED A YEAR LATE, IN THE ONE SENTENCE THE WHOLE VOLUME IS BUILT ON.** `chapters/volume-11/chapter-0548.md:121` reads *the fifteenth of October of the fourth year*, and `:9` reads *the first of October of the fourth year*. `state/current.md` §0b, `outline/volume-13.md` §7 and the Volume 13 row of `state/chapter-summaries.md` all said **the fifth year**. `3 × 360 + 240 + 14 = 1,334`, and chapter 548 is that day. **NOW: the fourth year, the column headed carried by, the fifteenth of October, and `1,773 − 1,334 = 439` days is how long it has gone unspoken.** The row also said *in six years*, which could not be true of an event in year four measured in year six, and that phrase is gone rather than replaced.

**2 (APPLIED) · *THE ORDINARY REPAINTING FORM* IS NOT ON THE PAGE, AND IT IS THE OBJECT A STANDING PROHIBITION GUARDS.** `chapter-0548.md` contains no *form*, no *box* and no *repaint*, verified by command with a control grep that works; the claim went into **the column headed carried by**, entered exactly as given by a clerk who may only enter. **THE TWO REPAINTING BOXES ARE A DIFFERENT RECURRING OBJECT AND ARE BLANK FOR EVER UNDER §2c, AND A BATCH THAT INHERITED *THE FORM* WOULD HAVE HAD A STANDING PROHIBITION AND AN ENTRY IN THE SAME SENTENCE.** Corrected in `state/current.md` §0b and `outline/volume-13.md` §2b, where the repair is recorded in the file itself.

**3 (APPLIED) · *HE WROTE THEM IN FORTY DAYS* BORROWSED THE DEFINITION'S FIGURE AND GAVE IT TO THE CONDITIONS.** `chapter-0548.md:7` says *he wrote it in about eleven seconds*, and the page adds *five days from the tenth of October to this morning*. **THE FORTY DAYS AND THE KITCHEN ON THE NINETEENTH OF JULY OF YEAR TWO BELONG TO THE DEFINITION OF AN UNRECORDED DEED, WHICH IS A DIFFERENT EVENT ON A DIFFERENT DATE, AND THE TWO ARE NOW SEPARATED IN `outline/volume-13.md` §2b, IN THE PREMISE SENTENCE AND IN CARD 601.** Neither figure was invented; both were attached to the wrong page.

**4 (APPLIED, IN TWO FILES) · *FOUR HUNDRED AND EIGHTY DAYS OUT* IS THREE HUNDRED AND SIXTY.** The rejected convention `(y-1) × 360 + 330 + (d-1)` yields **2,133** against the correct **1,773**, a delta of **360** — one whole year, because a January that closes a year is the twelfth month of it. The error is in the sentence that records the failure mode, in `state/current.md` §0a and in `state/continuity.md` §0z, and it was the same sentence's own rule that caught it: *a figure nobody re-computes is the figure that is wrong*.

**5 (APPLIED, IN TWO FILES, IN THREE ROWS) · THREE DIFFERENT AND ALL WRONG FILE TOTALS IN THE ROW THAT COUNTS FILES.** *Six hundred and fifty* in `outline/volume-13.md` §9, *six hundred and sixty-one* in `state/open-threads.md` Row 1, *SIXTY-FIVE HUNDRED* in the same outline's §2c, against a correct 600 on disk and 610 after this batch. **`find chapters -name 'chapter-*.md' | wc -l` is 600, and every one of these rows now takes the count by command and prints it beside the glyph counts it took it over.** **AND THE SAME ROW CARRIED A FIGURE THAT DOES NOT RE-DERIVE: `reviews/volume-12/volume-close.md` records *35,895 straight quotes in Volumes 01–11* and *zero curly*, and counted by glyph across those five hundred and fifty files it is **54,476 straight and 600 curly** — the curly six hundred are Chapters 41–50 of Volume 01, which have been written in curly since Chapter 41.** Whole book on 14 October 2026: **56,016 straight, 1,684 curly.** The first two figures in that table, 1,540 in 551–570 and 0 in 571–600, re-derive exactly. **THE CLOSED AUDIT WAS NOT EDITED; A CLOSED RECORD IS NOT REWRITTEN BY A LATER PASS. THE DIVERGENCE IS RECORDED IN THE LIVE ROWS WITH ITS OPERANDS.**

**6 (APPLIED, MINOR) · THE WALL ROW CONVERTED A SCENE INTO A RATE AND CALLED A FIGURE A CONTRADICTION WITHOUT SAYING AGAINST WHAT.** `chapter-0489.md:21` is *about nine thousand people under it at about a quarter past nine on the fourth of October, because it is an ordinary morning of an ordinary month and the trams go under it.* **The rate itself is on the page in several chapters — `chapter-0521.md:27`, `chapter-0526.md:109`, `chapter-0545.md:23`, `chapter-0548.md:47` — so the correct statement is that the wall has ONE RATE, about nine thousand a day, and two figures that are not it.** `chapter-0545.md:87` is Beatrix Halm's *about four hundred a day*, **in the same chapter that says nine thousand at `:23`**, and no page reconciles them. `chapter-0500.md:35` is a **total** — about four hundred went past him since the first of January — and is already reserved by `PC-117` and printed with its anchor at 501 and 505. **NOW: one rate, its reason, two excluded figures, each named at the page it is on.** Corrected in `outline/volume-13.md` §2c item 4, in card 605, in `state/continuity.md` and in the phase prompt.

**AND TWO THINGS THE REVIEW FLAGGED AS HOUSEKEEPING THAT WERE CHECKED AGAINST THE DISPATCHER AND NEEDED NOTHING.** **`outline/batches/volume-07-batch-0001/prompt.md` is not a live duplicate.** It is a lower-case stub headed *SUPERSEDED — do not write to this file*, it lives under `outline/` and not under `workspace/`, and the only thing that selects a phase is `find workspace -name PROMPT.md -type f` at `scripts/novel_runner.sh`:402, which fails both the scope and the case. It is inert by construction and its own text says so. **AND THE FIVE `workspace/continuation/next*` DIRECTORIES ARE NOT COMPETING.** `next`, `next-0001`, `next-0002` and `next-0003` each carry a `.done`, and `has_other_incomplete_phase` skips any candidate directory holding one; `next-0004` is the prompt this planning pass ran. `next_continuation_dir` skips every name that exists, so the next one written is `next-0005` and it cannot collide.

**WHAT THE REVIEW CONFIRMED AND THIS REPAIR DID NOT TOUCH: the spine, all fifty dates, both derivations, the seam, the batch sums, the four quarter-ends, the anniversary fuse, and the ten fixed titles at zero collisions.** They re-derive exactly and they were correct. **THE ONLY THINGS THAT WERE WRONG WERE PROSE-LEVEL PARAPHRASE OF CANON AND UNCOUNTS IN GATE ROWS — WHICH IS THE FAILURE MODE THIS VOLUME INSTALLS AS ITS GOVERNING RULE, AND IT WAS FOUND IN THE FILES THAT WRITE THE RULE.**
