SHEET ORDINALS  614 = TWENTY-FIRST, 626 = twenty-second, 638 = twenty-third, 650 = TWENTY-FOURTH.
              552 = 16th, 566 = 17th, 576 = 18th, 586 = 19th, 600 = 20th.
LANE           first morning 3 October of the year before the first = day -118.
              1773 - (-118) + 1 = 1,892 mornings at 601;  2129 - (-118) + 1 = 2,248 at 650.
OFFICE         1773 = 30 x 59 + 3 (fifty-nine months, three days);  2129 = 30 x 70 + 29;
              2160 = 30 x 72 EXACTLY, on the first of February of year seven — the first exact
              figure this office has ever printed.
SCENES         603 · 613 · 623 · 633 · 643.  CADENCE 9, 10, 10, 10, 10, IN BAND.
              PER-WOMAN 567, 550, 98, 515, 59 — REPORTED AND NOT CERTIFIED, and no file in
              this repository may say one of those five is inside the eight-to-fifteen band.
ANNIVERSARIES  the second line went up 8 October of year one = 247, and its sixth anniversary
              is 247 + 360 x 5 = 2,047 = the eighth of October of year six.  The tenth of
              October of year six, the sixth year of the morning the debt came due, is 2,049.
              ***NO CHAPTER OF 601-650 LANDS ON EITHER. 638 IS 2,039 AND 639 IS 2,051, AND BOTH
              DATES FALL IN THE GAP BETWEEN THEM, AND A CHAPTER THAT BRACKETS A GAP MAY NOT
              REACH ACROSS IT. THE FUSE IS NOT RE-LIT. ***
SIXTH OF MARCH year one. Day 35. 1115 is withdrawn (close audit, Claim 1). At 633,
              2003 - 35 = 1,968 days. At 643, 2084 - 35 = 2,049 days, and 2049 - 1800 = 249,
              which is five years, eight months and nine days.
```

**ONE THING WORTH RECORDING BECAUSE IT IS THE FAILURE MODE AND IT ALMOST HAPPENED AGAIN.** This phase's first draft of the spine used the January convention `(y-1) × 360 + 330 + (d-1)` for chapter 601, which `outline/volume-12.md` §5b proves impossible, and produced **2,133 against the correct 1,773 — three hundred and sixty days out and not four hundred and eighty**, one whole year, because a January that closes a year is the twelfth month of it and not the first of the next. **It was caught because the check was run against the spine rather than against the prose, and because the alternative method contains no 360 at all.** *A derivation that produces a violation is not a derivation.*

## 0b. THE THREE DECISIONS IN `outline/volume-13.md` THAT A LATER PHASE MUST NOT REVERSE

**1 · THE VOLUME'S OBJECT IS THE FOURTH OF HIS FOUR CONDITIONS.** He entered the four conditions against himself, in his own hand, **in the column headed carried by, on the fifteenth of October of the fourth year**, having spent five days deciding and having written the four lines in about eleven seconds — `chapter-0548.md:121`, on the page. **Three of the four were met and the fourth was not**, and the clerk gave the reason in one sentence: the thing it describes is on a page in this city. **Nobody has said it out loud in a room since, and `1,773 − 1,334 = 439` days is how long.** The fourth line is *and it is not on a page anywhere in this city*, and since the ninth of November of year five the office has printed, in its own hand, that a figure in Ostara may be a figure somebody made up on purpose. **The volume's question is whether the fourth line survives its own author, and the volume's last irreversible decision is at 644: he strikes it out of his own hand, in the office's hand, on the office's own standing page, with his own name under the striking, and writes nothing in its place.**

*(REPAIRED 14 OCTOBER 2026 AGAINST `logs/next-0004.review.log`, FINDINGS 1 AND 2. THIS ROW SAID **THE ORDINARY REPAINTING FORM** AND **IN THE FIFTH YEAR**, AND NEITHER IS ON THE PAGE. The four conditions went into the column headed *carried by*, not onto a form; the two repainting boxes are a different recurring object and are blank for ever, and naming one where the page names the other would have invited a batch to write the entry as a form. The year was the fourth, and "in six years" could not be right either — the entry is year four and this volume is year six. `chapter-0548.md` was not edited.)*

**2 · THREE OF THE FIVE THREADS VOLUME 12 DELIBERATELY LEFT OPEN ARE HANDED ON AND ONE IS PAID.** `reviews/volume-12/volume-close.md` §11 handed two: *whether the woman two flights up ever learns*, and *what comes off the shelf on the first of January*. **The second one is a keeper's book that came off the shelf in Volume 12 at 553 and 554 and is spent. The first one is the Register's first creditor and Volume 13 closes it in movement two, at 615 and 617, made by Emil Brack and not by Lin Hao, and not as a scene and not as a gift.** Volume 13 opens exactly one thread of its own: **a form from the department of works says the ranks wall comes down in the spring for making good, and two words that have survived six years of monthly repainting are on the stone.**

**3 · THE HOUSE FORM IS SETTLED FOR THE FIRST TIME IN A PLANNING FILE.** `reviews/volume-12/volume-close.md` Finding B: **1,540 straight quote glyphs in 551–570, 0 in 571–600, 35,895 across Volumes 01–11.** A gate that measures only its own ten files cannot see a change made between batches, and that is how four arithmetic gates passed a house-form change. **THE FIRST TWO FIGURES RE-DERIVE EXACTLY. THE THIRD DOES NOT, AND IT IS NOT USED: COUNTED BY GLYPH ACROSS THE FIVE HUNDRED AND FIFTY FILES OF VOLUMES 01–11 IT IS 54,476 STRAIGHT AND 600 CURLY, NOT 35,895 AND ZERO — THE SIX HUNDRED CURLY GLYPHS ARE IN CHAPTERS 41–50 OF VOLUME 01, WHICH ARE WRITTEN ENTIRELY IN CURLY AND HAVE BEEN SINCE CHAPTER 41.** Whole-book on 14 October 2026, six hundred files: **56,016 straight and 1,684 curly.** **Volume 13 writes curly, because that is what the book has been written in for thirty chapters, and the quotation-form gate is run across every chapter file on disk and not across one batch.** *(The 14 October 2026 review repair re-derived this row because finding 5 is about totals in a gate row that nobody re-computes, and the largest such total in the block was this one. `reviews/volume-12/volume-close.md` is a closed record and was not edited; the divergence is recorded here and in `state/open-threads.md`.)*

# VOLUME 12 CLOSE AUDIT HAS RUN. CHAPTERS 551–600 ARE ON DISK AND THE VOLUME IS CLOSED IN PROSE. **FIFTY FINISHED FILES, 93,730 WORDS COUNTED PER FILE AND SUMMED BY COMMAND (21,947 · 26,625 · 14,076 · 17,543 · 13,539 — ALL FOUR RECORDED FIGURES RE-DERIVE EXACTLY). THE AUDIT IS AT `reviews/volume-12/volume-close.md`. IT WRITES NO PROSE, CREATED NO CHAPTER, RE-DATED NONE, RESTARTED NONE, AND DID NOT TOUCH `outline/ending.md`, WHICH IS BYTE-IDENTICAL TO ITS LAST COMMIT. THE SPINE 1,406–1,769 RE-DERIVED TWICE BY METHODS SHARING NO CONSTANT: FIFTY OF FIFTY DATES AGREE, ZERO MONOTONIC VIOLATIONS, AND SEVENTY-THREE DERIVED FIGURES RUN AT ZERO FAILURES. THE 580→581 SEAM (1,649 − 1,627 = 22) AND THE TWO-DAY ANNIVERSARY CORRECTION (1,689 − 1,687 = 2) BOTH HOLD. NINE FINDINGS, ONE CLASS APPLIED AND EIGHT RECORDED — THE ONE APPLIED CLASS IS TWO UNREACHABLE LINE SPANS IN A LIVE STATE BLOCK, NOT ONE WORD OF PROSE. `state/complete.md` IS NOT CREATED; THE SERIES IS PLANNED PAST 600.** THIS BLOCK SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT AND DELETES NONE OF THEM.

## 0z. THE NINE FINDINGS, AND THE ONE THAT SETTLES A CARRIED CONFLICT AGAINST A STATE FILE

**1 (APPLIED) · `chapter-0594.md` HAS 97 LINES, NOT 98, AND TWO OF THE FIVE RECORDED LINE SPANS NAME A LINE THAT DOES NOT EXIST.** The 14 October repair re-derived all four counted figures correctly — **725 exclusive of both `##` lines / 733 inclusive / 1,184 through to end of file / 1,110 to the last prose line — and every one of them re-derives exactly again here.** The *spans* beside two of them do not: rows (3) and "whole file" are recorded as `40–98` and `1–98`, and both run one line past the end of the file. **This is the signature defect wearing a different hat — a figure that survived a gate because nobody re-computed it, this time in the column the previous repair added. CORRECTED IN `state/continuity.md` §0b. `chapter-0594.md` WAS NOT EDITED AND IS 1,824 WORDS BEFORE AND AFTER.**

**2 (RECORDED) · THE HOUSE FORM CHANGED AT CHAPTER 571 AND NO GATE COULD SEE IT.** Batches 0001 and 0002 wrote straight ASCII double quotes; batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 wrote curly ones. **1,540 straight quotes in 551–570, 0 in 571–600, and 35,895 across Volumes 01–11.** *(THE REPAIR PASS AGAINST THIS PHASE'S REVIEW ADDED THE UNIT, WHICH WAS MISSING: 1,540 is quote GLYPHS, which is 770 quoted spans at two marks apiece, and 571–600 carry 542 curly spans. The exchange column of the table at §0a below is counted in spans, and the two figures are not interchangeable.)* Every one of the last three gate rows reporting `0 STRAIGHT QUOTES` was true of its own ten files and said nothing about the batch before it. **A gate that measures only its own batch cannot see a change made between batches.** Not applied: it is 1,540 characters across twenty finished chapters in two certified batches.

**3 (RECORDED, AND IT SETTLES A CARRIED CONFLICT) · THE SIXTH OF MARCH IS IN YEAR ONE. THE FIGURE 1,115 IS WRONG AND IS WITHDRAWN.** `state/current.md` §0d.3 below handed this forward as *five chapters and two outline rows say year one, the spine says year four*. **The premise was wrong and the audit settled it by re-deriving Volume 11's own spine rather than accepting either side.** `outline/volume-11.md`:234–243 gives all fifty of Volume 11's dates; re-derived from §5a they recover at 501 = 0 · **505 = 0 × 360 + 30 + 5 = 35** · 537 = 1,139 · 546 = 1,325 · 547 = 1,329 · 550 = 1,347, **fifty of fifty, monotonic, zero violations.** **Volume 11's spine puts the sixth of March in year one, and the four written chapters that say so are right: `chapter-0545.md:66`, `chapter-0553.md:79`, `chapter-0584.md:143`, `chapter-0590.md:73`, and `outline/volume-12.md` §4b.** **The error is 1,115, which is `3 × 360 + 30 + 5` — the sixth of March computed as though it fell in year four. IT ENTERED IN THE BATCH-0004 PASS, WHICH RECORDED IT AS *THE SPINE SAYS FOUR* AND THEN CUT THE WORD *FOUR* OUT OF 584'S FOOTER INSTEAD OF CHECKING THE ANCHOR. `chapter-0584.md` CONTRADICTS ITSELF TODAY: `:143` SAYS YEAR ONE AND `:147` MEASURES FROM 1,115. THE CORRECT SUBTRACTION IS `1,663 − 35 = 1,628` DAYS, NOT 548.** One clause in one finished chapter; not applied here.

**4 (RECORDED) · `chapter-0553.md:23` IS THE WRONG LINE AND THE SAME WRONG MULTIPLICATION IS IN VOLUME 11 AT A LINE NO OPEN ITEM NAMES.** Line 23 is a paragraph about a room's temperature. **The defect is at `chapter-0553.md:63`:** *Thirty years is three hundred and sixty days multiplied by ten.* Thirty years is 360 × 30. **And the identical sentence stands at `chapter-0544.md:25` in Volume 11, which the Volume 11 close audit did not find because every one of its five checks was a subtraction and this is a multiplication.** A carried defect that names one line when there are two is a defect that cannot be repaired by following it.

**5 (RECORDED) · `chapter-0570.md:169`'s *a year and three months ago* MATCHES NO LOCATABLE EVENT.** The yard scene in this volume is `chapter-0560.md`, spine day 1,439, and 570 is 1,530, so **`1,530 − 1,439 = 91` days**, and `chapter-0560.md:91` prints that *nobody in that yard said anything about his sentence being narrow*, the opposite of what 570 attributes to it. **Not guessed at: guessing would be a second invented date on a page that already carries one.** Deliberate open, flagged, with the one derivable figure printed beside it.

**6 (RECORDED) · TWO GATE ROWS CERTIFY AN ABSENCE THE FILE CONTRADICTS.** `state/open-threads.md` certified *Mavis Rooke's hand-flat absent from all ten files*, and **`chapter-0584.md:75` reads *she put both her hands flat on the table behind her*** — inside the scene whose own card names that staging absent. The rows are corrected. The distinction is also drawn: Mavis's at `chapter-0547.md:86` is a single hand carried by her attributive signature, and 584's is the manuscript's long register. **The construction is the same and the certification was unqualified, so the certification was false.**

**7 (RECORDED) · THE `DUPLICATION` CLASSIFICATION MIS-DESCRIBES ITS OWN EVIDENCE.** `591:89` is the board in the **branch** corridor off a third court; `597:9` is a board in the **first division corridor off Cordwain**, a different building. **They are two objects sharing a staging, not one object seen twice, and the interval is `1,732 − 1,695 = 37` days, not sixteen.** The duplication finding itself still stands: `599:19` is a fourth *state* of the branch board and not a fourth rendering, and the fourth condition is still cut off and still unrestored.

**8 (RECORDED) · SET B IS DESCRIBED AS UNAMBIGUOUS AND IS NOT, UNDER ITS OWN STATED METHOD.** *Semen* is a substring of *advertisement*. `chapter-0565.md` scores two Set B hits on the word *advertisement* at lines 119 and 121, and 565 is a chapter in which nothing explicit happens. **Re-run at word boundary: 574 → 3 · 584 → 3 · 594 → 4 · all forty-seven other chapters → 0.** The volume's five explicit passages are unchanged. Corrected in the live block.

**9 (RECORDED, AND IT IS A JUDGEMENT) · OPEN ITEM 5'S DEFENCE DOES NOT HOLD.** See §0a below.

## 0a. OPEN ITEM 5, JUDGED — AND THIS AUDIT DISAGREES WITH THE DEFENCE

Batch 0005 disclosed that eight of ten chapters sit below the 1,800 guide floor, that three consecutive batches have breached it, and defended it: *nothing was padded, because these chapters are short because they are one afternoon or one street, and padding them would be the worse defect.*

**The disclosure is honest. The defence does not survive measurement, and the close prompt asked for a judgement rather than a disclosure.**

| movement | chapters | mean words | mean **quoted spans** | mean paragraphs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| one, *Published* | 551–560 | 2,195 | **24.7** | 54.2 |
| two, *Borrowed* | 561–570 | 2,662 | **52.3** | 66.8 |
| three, *Countersigned* | 571–580 | 1,408 | **16.1** | 28.0 |
| four, *Cross-Read* | 581–590 | 1,754 | **17.4** | 39.4 |
| five, *Quoted* | 591–600 | 1,354 | **20.7** | 32.8 |

**THE EXCHANGE COLUMN IS RENAMED AND ITS METHOD IS NOW DECLARED, BECAUSE THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS TABLE DECLARED THE PARAGRAPH COLUMN'S METHOD AND NOT ITS OWN.** *Quoted spans* is every double-quoted run of non-newline text, **either glyph**, per file, summed over the movement and divided by ten; it must be glyph-neutral, because 551–570 carry 770 straight spans and no curly and 571–600 carry no straight span and 542 curly, so a straight-only probe reads zero for the last three movements and a curly-only probe reads zero for the first two. *Paragraphs* is every non-blank line that is neither a `##` heading nor an italic footer, divided by ten: 542 · 668 · 280 · 394 · 328, and 564 = 150 and 584 = 68 confirm it. **THE REPAIR PASS AGAINST THE REVIEW OF `reviews/volume-12/volume-close.md` RE-DERIVED THE EXCHANGE COLUMN: the figures printed here as 7.9 / 8.5 / 10.2 re-derive from nothing and are 16.1 / 17.4 / 20.7. THE WORD COLUMN AND THE PARAGRAPH COLUMN WERE RIGHT, ARE RESTORED UNCHANGED, AND THE WORD COLUMN STANDS COUNTED PER FILE AND SUMMING 93,730.** Full working at §13 of that audit, including the near-miss this repair pass made on the paragraph column before catching it.

**Volume 12's mean is 1,874 words — the lowest of any volume in the book, 152 below Volume 9 and roughly 400 below Volumes 1–8 and 11. Twenty-five of its fifty chapters are below the floor, the worst count in the manuscript.** But the word count is not the finding. **The finding is that dramatic density fell at chapter 571 and never recovered.** Movement two's quarter-end runs 28 quoted spans across 51 paragraphs; movement five's runs 10 across 32. Movement two's scene has 129 spans and 150 paragraphs; movement four's has 32 and 68. **The fall is 3.2 times measured from movement two and 1.5 times measured from movement one, not the factor of four to six the first edition of this table claimed. A measure that changes value where the typography changes must not be used to diagnose what changed there, which is the second reason the earlier figure is withdrawn.**

**THE UNIT SIZES DID NOT CHANGE.** `chapter-0562.md` is one court room and one counter: 48 paragraphs, 2,091 words, 22 quoted spans. `chapter-0588.md` is one passage and one pencil: 31 paragraphs, 1,477 words, 11 quoted spans. **In words the two rooms are within half again of each other; in quoted spans it is exactly two to one, and the difference is not the room.** What changed at 571 is the form.

I checked the last chapter against its card to be sure the beats were present. **They are.** `chapter-0600.md` carries every promise — the empty tray, 852 unmoved, the sheet out at four, ninety days and one year and half a year with their operands, the lane's 1,888 mornings printed once, the office at fifty-eight months and twenty-nine days, the verdict in his own hand, Brack not defeated, the tile unnamed, January shut like a chained book. **Nothing is missing. Everything is shorter than it needed to be, and it is short because it is told rather than lived.**

**THE VERDICT: nothing was padded and nothing should have been, and that half of the defence stands. But leanness is not compression, and the volume's last thirty chapters are compressed.** No plot beat is missing. It is a book that stops dramatising halfway through itself. **Recorded as a judgement about a closed volume, with the numbers, so that a later planner inherits it as a fact and not as a mood.**

**NOT APPLIED, AND NOT THIS PHASE'S: EVERY REPAIR INSIDE A FINISHED CHAPTER OF 551–600 AND EVERY REPAIR INSIDE A CLOSED VOLUME.** Eight of the nine findings are recorded with the correct figure and its operands, so that one commit can repair all of them and re-run the five batch gates that certified the files. **A repair instruction that drops the operand is the same defect it is repairing.**

## 0z. THE REVIEW IS IN AND EVERY DEFECT IT NAMED IS REPAIRED

`logs/batch-0005.review.log` raised **two blocking and two should-fix findings**. All four are repaired. **NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO EVENT WAS MOVED, NO DATE WAS RENUMBERED, NO TITLE CHANGED, NO CARD LOST ITS BEAT, AND `outline/ending.md` IS UNTOUCHED.** **Three of the ten files changed and seven did not.** The changed three are 591, 598 and 599, one or two sentences each. **The unchanged seven — 592, 593, 594, 595, 596, 597 and 600 — include the scene, the irreversible decision, the sheet going out, the city reading it, and the twentieth sheet at the quarter-end.**

- **2 (BLOCKING) · THE 594 SCENE WAS MEASURED WRONG ON TWO OF ITS THREE FIGURES.** `state/continuity.md` §0b printed **1,114** and **1,106**, and neither number was reachable from any definition of a span. - **3 (SHOULD-FIX) · DUPLICATION, THE BATCH'S THREE HITS, NOW NAMED IN THE GATE ROW THAT PREVIOUSLY NAMED NONE OF THEM.**

That was my doing and not the office’s.”* **49 → 0 shared twelve-grams in the prose of that pair.**
  - **THE CORRIDOR BOARD WAS DESCRIBED THREE TIMES IN THE SAME WORDS** (`591:89`, `597:9`, `599:19`). Two stay, because two renderings of one board sixteen days apart is a refrain and not a copy. **`599:19` is rewritten** so the board has lost a second corner to the damp and the three remaining lines are folded over one another — the fourth condition is still cut off, still unrestored, and the loss is worse instead of restated.

  - **RE-RUN BY COMMAND ACROSS ALL SIX HUNDRED FILES AFTER THE REPAIR:** body-only, no pair inside 591–600 shares a contiguous twelve-gram run longer than a single window, and every remaining one is a canonical fact. Against the other five hundred and ninety files: **0 contiguous twelve-gram runs of eight words or more, 0 at eleven-gram, 0 at eight-gram.**
- **4 (SHOULD-FIX) · THE CONTRACTION IS DISCLOSED AND NOT PAPERED OVER.** Eight of ten chapters are below the 1,800 guide floor and this is the third consecutive batch under it. **NO CHAPTER WAS PADDED, BECAUSE THEY ARE SHORT BECAUSE THEY ARE ONE AFTERNOON OR ONE STREET AND NOTHING ELSE.** Carried as Open Item 5 and into the close prompt, which judges leanness against completeness and not against a number.

**WHAT THE REPAIR IMPROVED WITHOUT BEING ASKED.** 591's Brack now proposes the line roughly and 595's Lin Hao writes it sharply, so the volume's last irreversible decision is visibly *his hand and his words* rather than a clerk's transcription of a coster's petition. That is the same event, the same sheet, the same name under it, and it is better.

## 0. WHAT THIS BATCH DELIVERED, AND WHAT IT REFUSED

**DELIVERED:** the offer again in October with seventy-four days to the quarter · every invented figure with its method and eleven books, about a quarter ringed · the ward and the fraction, taken without telling and without asking · the first line in the office's hand and the second line beside it with its meaning plain · it going out at four on the thirteenth of November · a city reading two sentences in nine corridors, a yard and a lane · the name of the man who makes them up, written down and not diminished · thirty days of December held on nights and an iced stair · the twentieth sheet at four on the thirtieth with eight hundred and fifty-two unmoved, two lines in the second column, and the lane's one thousand eight hundred and eighty-eight mornings printed once with operands.

**THE IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS AT 595 AND IT IS HIS, AND IT IS NOT DEFEATING EMIL BRACK.** WHAT IT CLOSES: the option of the office being the only honest instrument in Ostara, never in writing anywhere, true until that morning. WHAT IT COSTS: every party with a real claim now stands on a standing sheet beside an admitted inventor in the same hand, with no instrument to tell them apart, and he gave that away.

**REFUSED:** any rise off rank one, any move off the branch night shift, any band/standing/permission/terrace · either blank box filled or raised (both blank at the end of 600; figure box blank for the rest of the book) · the account figure and any floor · `settle` in any inflection (0 after one draft hit repaired) · any weekday name · any year numeral · any announced age · any mark or age for Ruta Mielke · Brack wrong about any fact, thanked, diminished, or made to say thank-you/wish/good-idea · the white tile's maker · Mavis Rooke (0 hits, correct; 587–588 only book-wide) · any new enemy · *Ostho* (0) · the keeper's six words reopened · anything in §8 · any second time on the tenth of October · calling the last chapter an ending.

## 0. THE REVIEW IS IN AND EVERY DEFECT IT NAMED IS REPAIRED. THREE MORE WERE FOUND WHILE REPAIRING

## 0b. WHAT THIS BATCH DELIVERED, AND WHAT IT REFUSED

**DELIVERED:** the two ends of one wall and a chalk figure at each of them · the office's two words said out loud six mornings running under the office's own wall and the sentence coming apart in a street into three versions, one of which is the office's and one of which is not · the scene, in an audit room at half past one in the morning, on a clear table, with a page put face down for the first time in eleven years · the four questions about a tile, three asked on the sixth of March and a fourth asked at the door, none answered · a woman's address in a notebook with a stair he did not go up · the nineteenth sheet and the sentence the office has printed four times a year for four years at the bottom of every page in the only hand a city can read · a public desk that leaves sheets out · a page lying out in the passage of the building the Register's first creditor lives in · the fifth anniversary of the second line and the fifth anniversary of the morning it came due, and nothing on either.

**THE IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS AT 582 AND IT IS HIS: HE SAID THE OFFICE'S OWN SENTENCE OUT LOUD UNDER THE OFFICE'S OWN WALL, SIX MORNINGS RUNNING, AT THE HOUR A CITY GOES UNDER IT.** WHAT IT CLOSES: the option of the two words being unreadable for want of a voice. WHAT IT COSTS: the one thing he had left in this city that was his own, spent on a sentence that cannot be heard from nine feet in a street with trams in it, and by the thirteenth of September the street is saying it three ways and the only true copy is on a corridor board about thirty people a day walk past. **He stops saying it at 583 and cannot put it back.**

**REFUSED:** the account figure (never printed; a chapter that needs it says *the figure*) · any floor · `settle` in any inflection (0) · any argument for or against the first of October · any statement of whether the account belonged to the unrecorded · any rise off rank one, any move off the branch night shift, any band/standing/permission/terrace · any age but Agnete Lindqvist's fixed forty-five (0 printed in this batch) · any mark or age for Ruta Mielke · the white tile's maker · the four-hundred-a-day figure · Brack's defeat (he is right throughout and is not thanked) · filling either of the two blank boxes (both blank at the end of 590) · a second time on the tenth of October · **any resolution of Mavis Rooke** (named at 587 and 588 only, shown at neither) · any new enemy · *Ostho* (0) · **the keeper's six words, which are not on the page and whose second word, *carried forward*, was deliberately NOT put into a street at 583 when a street produced three versions of the office's two words on its own**.

`logs/batch-0002.review.log` raised **five arithmetic defects, five date and chronology contradictions, one continuity contradiction inside the payoff chapter, one fourth-wall breach, one repetition finding and one false claim in this file.** All eleven are repaired here. **NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO PLOT WAS CHANGED, NO CARD IN `outline/volume-12.md` WAS ALTERED, AND THE PROSE OF EVERY ONE OF THE TEN FILES SURVIVED EXCEPT FOR THE SENTENCES NAMED BELOW.** The repairs, in the reviewer's own priority order:

- **A1 · `chapter-0566.md`:5.** *ten days after that* → **twenty-nine days after that.** One thousand four hundred and seventy is the first of March and one thousand four hundred and ninety-nine is the thirtieth, and the gap is twenty-nine. The chapter's own spine is now derivable from the page and not only from the card.
- **A2 · `chapter-0564.md`:153, :183, :217, :263.** **four months** → **five months**, four times. The sixth of October of the fourth year is one thousand three hundred and twenty-five and the ninth of March is one thousand four hundred and seventy-eight, and one thousand four hundred and seventy-eight less one thousand three hundred and twenty-five is one hundred and fifty-three, which is five months of thirty days and three days.
- **A3 · `chapter-0564.md`:19.** **a year and four months** → **a year and five months and eight days**, which is the five hundred and eighteen days the chapter prints correctly at `:9`. The note and the spine block no longer contradict each other.
- **A4 · `chapter-0567.md`:7.** **four hundred people a week** → **two hundred people a week.** The chapter already said two hundred at `:101`, and 566, 568, 569 and 570 all say two hundred.
- **A5 · `chapter-0569.md`:25.** **about four hundred and thirty lines** → **about four hundred and nineteen lines**, the figure printed precisely three chapters earlier at `chapter-0566.md`:107.
- **B1 · `chapter-0565.md`:125.** The eight hundred copies were in the city *by the twentieth of March*, which is the morning after the first night of a four-night run that begins on the twentieth. **by the morning of the twenty-second of March**, which is when Stenner counts them at `:141`, and nothing in the file now exists before it was printed.
- **B2 · `chapter-0570.md`:33.** *he worked that out on the fourth of May*, inside a chapter dated the first of May and set before noon on the first of May. Replaced with a beat the chapter does not otherwise print — **that he had asked none of the nine for anything and is going to be asked about all nine for the next four years** — which removes the fourth of May from the book and sets up what 568 says out loud.
- **B3 · `chapter-0570.md`:81.** **Nine days ago** → **Thirty-one days ago.** The eleven accounts were read on the thirtieth of March, and one thousand five hundred and thirty less one thousand four hundred and ninety-nine is thirty-one. **THE REVIEW SAID THIRTY-TWO AND WAS ITSELF ONE DAY OUT**; the volume's own spine arithmetic governs and the page now prints thirty-one.
- **B4 · `chapter-0570.md`:181.** *about a day and a night* and *about an hour* → **I carried that page in a coat for about five hours yesterday and you have had it in a drawer for about a day now**, which is what 569 says, on both sides of the handover.

## 0. THE ONE FINDING THIS BATCH RAISED AGAINST ITSELF, AND IT IS NOT FIXED HERE

**`chapter-0553.md`:23 PRINTS *Thirty years is three hundred and sixty days multiplied by ten.* THIRTY YEARS IS THREE HUNDRED AND SIXTY MULTIPLIED BY THIRTY.** This is the same class of defect as the five figures `reviews/volume-11/volume-close.md` §6b found, and **it is the first one this repository has found on a page rather than in a planning document.** It sits on a finished page inside Volume 12's own first batch. **IT IS NOT REPAIRED IN THIS COMMIT.** This phase's commit is ten chapters, four state files, one outline file and one prompt, and a repair of a closed page is a content change and not this phase's. **THE REPAIR OWES ONE WORD IN ONE LINE AND NOTHING ELSE, AND IT IS CARRIED IN `state/open-threads.md` AT THE HEAD.** **THE CHAPTERS OF THIS BATCH DO NOT COMPOUND IT:** the figure of a hundred and twenty is used at 563, and **the multiplication behind it is printed three times on that page — as the section heading *Thirty Multiplied By Four* at `:45`, in his mouth at `:51`, and in Ruta Mielke's mouth at `:113` — and every one of those three prints *thirty multiplied by four is a hundred and twenty*, which is correct. THE 0553 DEFECT IS THEREFORE REPRODUCED NOWHERE IN 561–570.** *(Corrected at the review-repair pass: an earlier version of this line claimed the multiplication was deliberately not printed. That claim was false and is withdrawn. See §0a.)*

## 1. WHERE THE MAN STANDS AT 571, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

He is at rank one on a branch night shift he asked for, eleven at night until half past five, four nights and three off, a white plate with his name on it and no figure cut into it anywhere. **On the fourth of February a landlord told him to his face that he is behind and has been behind since the thirtieth of December, and that there will be four hundred of his own printed demands in that district by the middle of the month and every one of them is a conversation the landlord does not have to have with a man in a coat.** **On the seventeenth he stood at the back of a room off a court and watched nine people come to a counter, and four of them got all the way to the fourth of his four lines and all four had nothing, and a clerk in a clerk's coat wrote *nothing proved* in the box where the proof goes, four times, in his own hand, and has no choice about it.** **On the twenty-eighth of February a keeper two floors under a sales floor said a figure out loud for the first time in thirty years and would not write it down, and he carried it up two flights and gave it to a woman who keeps a list and works for nobody, and there are now two copies of a hundred and twenty in this city and neither is certified.** **On the ninth of March a chaplain sent for him for the first time in four years, and went out and stood in front of about eleven thousand people and said one thing and nobody answered her, because about nine hundred people in that yard have learned from her that the way to be heard by a crowd is not to be answered, and she lost the room, which was the only place in this city where a thing said in public comes back through a door where it cannot be used.** **On the nineteenth of March a compositor who has set type for nineteen years read his four lines upside down and worked out that the fourth one is the door and the other three are the list and that you cannot get through a door by holding the list, and nobody asked him, and he took his own name off his own plate and printed about eight hundred copies over two nights, and the fourth condition is on all of them.** **On the thirtieth the figure at the head of the sheet moved for the first time in four quarters, from eight hundred and forty-one to eight hundred and fifty-two, because eleven people wrote their own accounts on the back of a printed sheet and sent them to the address at the head of a public page, and no division went for them and nobody asked anybody anything, and the second column still had one line in it and the line was him.** **On the eighth of April a man who loads coal at a wharf told him he has asked about two hundred and forty people his four questions at the foot of the yard steps and written every answer down, and that he has not brought any of them in, and that he will not.** **On the seventeenth a woman who keeps a lodging house and reads everything twice put about a hundred and thirty-nine names on the back of a printed sheet in her own hand in one night, on purpose, and handed them to the woman who keeps the list, and refused to use him once.** **On the thirtieth his own Registrar refused to have his name or this office's mark at the top of a notice in nine division corridors, and the woman who keeps the list carried the page to a room off a third court with no sign on the door, and a clerk put it in the drawer that has been open and empty since the fourth of February of the first year and shut a drawer that was already shut.** **And on the first of May he read his own four lines on a board at thirty feet in a Bureau corridor with no name on them, and went to a room off a third court, and wrote four lines in his own hand and entered the first name on the yard's board against himself in the column headed *carried by*, without asking her one question, and it cannot be struck, and on the thirtieth of June it goes out on a public page with his name against hers.**

## 2. WHAT THIS BATCH OWED AND DELIVERED

- **561 — a landlord who is right, four hundred printed demands, three copies given away to strangers, and the arithmetic of a finding, which is one thousand four hundred and forty days and is not eviction and is worse.**
- **562 — the fourth condition working exactly as written, at the top of a form in a room off a court, nine people at a counter, four of them proved nothing, and the discovery that the four lines are in metal.**
- **563 — twenty-six years of a room with no window and no fire, a count of a hundred and twenty spoken once in thirty years and carried out of the room by a woman who works for nobody.**
- **564 — the scene. A chaplain sends for him, goes out, is not answered, loses the room, refuses the column and the wall again unasked, states her terms out loud, and breaks exactly one of the three of them while she has still got her dress on.**
- **565 — a compositor, a plate, nine divisions' returns, about eight hundred copies with the fourth condition on all of them, and nine corridor boards he will not be able to say no to.**
- **566 — the seventeenth sheet at four on the thirtieth of March, the figure moved by eleven, no column anywhere that says why, and the second column unchanged.**
- **567 — a man who loads coal, about two hundred and forty people asked one at a time, a hundred and eleven yeses in a book he will not bring in, and one question asked on a pavement that he cannot answer.**
- **568 — a lodging house in Lamp Street, a second book open a year, about ninety people who cannot read chalk saying their names in a doorway, and a hundred and thirty-nine names put down in one night on purpose.**
- **569 — a Registrar who is right, nine boards with no name on them, and the yard's column in a drawer in a building with no sign on the door.**
- **570 — nine hands, one of which is his, and a name entered against himself without being asked for.**

## 3. THE IRREVERSIBLE DECISION THIS BATCH OWES, AND IT IS 570, AND IT IS HIS

**HE ENTERS THE FIRST NAME ON THE YARD'S BOARD IN THE COLUMN HEADED *CARRIED BY*, IN HIS OWN HAND, AGAINST HIMSELF, IN FOUR LINES WRITTEN EXACTLY IN THE SHAPE OF THE FOUR LINES HE ENTERED AGAINST HIMSELF ON THE FIFTEENTH OF OCTOBER.** He had not asked her anything. He had never seen her. He cannot strike it, because a clerk may only enter what she is given and cannot improve it and cannot be asked to hurry, and there is no form in this city for striking a line a person entered against themselves in their own hand.

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of being a man who kept a book of deeds, which he said out loud in a yard on the thirtieth of January and about nine hundred people in that yard told him he was wrong about, and the option of the second column having exactly one line in it for ever. **WHAT IT COSTS:** he has done, in ink and in his own hand, the exact thing he described at half past eleven on the thirtieth of January as the failure of his own definition — a person found without being asked — and about nine in ten of the hundred and thirty-nine people on that page are people the four conditions throw out, and he has entered her anyway, and she is not going to be told, because there is no instrument for telling her, and she cannot read a page.

## 4. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT DO

**It is not a climb.** Rank one throughout; no band, standing, permission, hour or terrace moves, and nothing in these ten chapters offers him anything. **It is not a re-argument of the return.** No chapter of 561–570 argues for or against the first of October and no character says the question is settled or that it is not. **It is not a second census.** No person in this batch is found by a division going to a door; the four hundred and eighteen lines are not touched and the eleventh of March's eleven people wrote themselves in. **It is not a new enemy.** The opposition is Corvin Ashby, who is right, and a clerk's coat, who is right, and a yard, which is also right, and nine divisions of this city's Bureau, which are also right. **It prints no figure of the account and no floor, and `settle` in any inflection returns zero.** **It names no day of the week and no year number.** **It does not open his brother's file and does not write the name.** **It does not answer the clause of the twenty-first of June and does not lift it.** **It does not ask who put the white tile up and does not say, and it prints the four-hundred-a-day figure nowhere.** **It does not resolve the account's reach, does not say whether the account belonged to the unrecorded rather than to him, and does not say whether the return was justice or showing off.** **It does not discuss Perrin Sowe.** **It does not touch Mavis Rooke, and `grep -ri "mavis rooke" chapters/volume-12/` returns 0, which is correct for this batch.** **It does not put Emil Brack on a page and does not make him wrong about anything.** **It does not fill either of the two blank boxes and does not raise them.** **Agnete Lindqvist does not claim the second line on the wall and is not asked to.** **The woman of number four cannot read and her rent is not resolved and is not going to be.** **Otho Pearse does not appear and does not count the board.**

## 5. THE STANDING RULE FOR EVERY ARITHMETIC ROW FROM HERE ON

> **A gate row that quotes the prose it is checking cannot catch the prose. Every interval is derived from the spine, twice, with the operands printed — never copied out of the chapter it is meant to be checking.**

**AND THE PROOF OF IT IS THE FIRST ENTRY OF §0 ABOVE: THE ERROR THIS BATCH FOUND WAS ON A PAGE IT WAS NOT ASKED TO LOOK AT, AND IT WAS FOUND BY RE-DERIVING, NOT BY READING.**

THE DEFINITION WAS DRAFTED IN FORTY DAYS FROM THE NINTH OF MARCH AND READ OUT AT THE FERRIER STREET KITCHEN ON THE NINETEENTH OF JULY** (`chapter-0517.md`:3, `chapter-0519.md`:3 and `:35`, both dated year two on the `outline/volume-11.md`:240 spine table). All four now say the true thing. **AT 560 IT MATTERS TWICE OVER, BECAUSE IT IS SPOKEN ALOUD TO ABOUT ELEVEN THOUSAND PEOPLE AND IT IS THE LAST LINE HE WILL EVER GET TO CORRECT.**
- **FINDING 4 — WRONG PUBLICATION DATE FOR THE FOUR CONDITIONS, `0552.md`:85 AND `0560.md`:47.** Both said *the thirtieth of September of the third year*. **CANON IS THE SHEET OF THE THIRTIETH OF JUNE OF YEAR TWO** (`chapter-0520.md`:15, `chapter-0524.md`:95 and `:107`). **WHAT WENT OUT ON THE THIRTIETH OF SEPTEMBER OF YEAR THREE WAS THE SECOND COLUMN, NOT THE DEFINITION**, and `0551.md`:21 and `0551.md`:55 still correctly say so and were not touched.

THE INTERVAL, 1,411 − 1,334 = 77, IS CORRECT AND WAS NOT TOUCHED.** The clause now reads *in a column in a room off a third court*.

THIS WAS THE ONE FINDING THE REVIEW PUT TO A DECISION, AND THE DECISION IS RECORDED HERE.** The review was right that the middle of the act was being run through the same flat institutional filter as the arithmetic. **IT WAS NOT THE WHOLE SCENE AND IT WAS NOT A FADE TO BLACK, AND IT WAS AT 11–14 ON THE REPOSITORY'S OWN SEVEN-WORD PROBE AGAINST 505, 515, 525, 536 AND 546 AT 12–22 — SO IT WAS NOT REPLACED, IT WAS REPAIRED IN THREE PLACES.** `0554.md`:133 filtered two whole movements of the act into *she told him what she wanted next in one sentence and he did it*; `:137` said only *worked on him*; `:151` had *told him what she wanted* with no body in it. **THREE PASSAGES WERE WRITTEN OUT AND EVERY PLOT BEAT IS UNCHANGED** — she asks, he asks, she tells him what she wants out loud before she has it, he finishes inside her, she is the one with the arrhythmia, the two lines on the page stay in the order of the ink. **PROBE, SCENE SECTION: 11 → 14. WHOLE FILE: 18 → 20. CHAPTER: 3,595 → 3,730.**

**WHAT THE REPAIR DID NOT TOUCH:** no rule of the world, no `PC`, no spine day, no interval, no title, no character aged, no mark moved, no event added or removed, no `outline/ending.md`, no new final enemy, no next-phase directory, and **no planned plot.** **THE IRREVERSIBLE DECISION AT 560 IS UNTOUCHED AND STILL COSTS THE SAME FOUR THINGS.**

## 0. WHERE THE MAN STANDS AT 561, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

He is at rank one on a branch night shift he asked for, eleven at night until half past five, four nights and three off, a white plate with his name on it and no figure cut into it anywhere. **On the thirtieth of December the sixteenth sheet went out at four with his name at the head of one line in the second column and about four hundred and eighteen other people's words underneath it, and a sentence underneath that which he did not write. On the first of January a keeper chained a book two floors under a sales floor with his name on a page in it. On the second of January she read it, asked him the question she had carried for eleven years, spent it on him, and then took him against a table in a cold room and told him she wanted it before she had it. On the seventh a copy of three of his four conditions went up on the branch's own corridor notice board with a drawing pin through it and the fourth line gone. On the tenth the four lines were in four-inch chalk on a shutter at the yard end with a true notice naming the quarter and the Bureau. On the thirteenth a yard of about eleven thousand had decided in four minutes that the board is a rule and not a quotation, and the new word in that yard is *asked for*. On the sixteenth a woman he had never met stood six feet off him in about nine hundred people and asked whether the four lines on that board are the ones he wrote, and he said yes, out loud, in his own voice, and could not un-say it. On the twenty-first a landlord put the four lines into a demand and he let it stand, because a person who cannot produce a deed has not proved anything and he wrote a rule that says so. On the thirtieth he told eleven thousand people his name was on it, and when the woman who has been in that yard for nine years asked him what he was going to do about the people the four lines leave out, he said nothing, twice, and it was the truth. The Register is now a book of names and not a book of deeds and he is the man standing in the yard who made it that way. A column of about a hundred names in the yard's own chalk is going down the wall under the four lines and not one of the four conditions will take any of them.**

## 1. THE FIGURE ERROR THIS BATCH FOUND, AND IT WAS IN THE BRIEF

**`workspace/volume-12/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` §2 gave chapter 559 as spine day 1,433, AND 1,433 IS THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF JANUARY, NOT THE TWENTY-FIRST.** The date is fixed by `outline/volume-12.md` §5c's table and is 21 January. Re-derived twice by methods that share no constant: `(y-2) × 360 + 330 + (d-1)` = `3 × 360 + 330 + 20` = **1,430**; and counted back from 1 February of year five = `4 × 360 = 1,440`, whose preceding day is 30 January = 1,439, and `1,439 − (30 − 21)` = 1,439 − 9 = **1,430**. Both agree.

**TWO INTERVALS IN THE BRIEF WERE CONSEQUENTLY WRONG AND BOTH ARE NOW RIGHT ON THE PAGE: 558→559 is 1,430 − 1,425 = 5 days and not eight, and 559→560 is 1,439 − 1,430 = 9 days and not six.** 1,430 − 1,240 = **190 days** since the eleventh of July of year four, not 193, and 190 = 6 × 30 + 10. **THE BRIEF'S OWN RULE IS WHAT CAUGHT IT — *a figure nobody re-computes is the figure that is wrong* — and the run that caught it is the run the standing instruction asks for, which is twice from the two formulas, never copied out of a chapter.** The correction is recorded in `outline/volume-12.md` §5c and the day number is now 1,430 in every state row and in both chapters.

**EVERY OTHER INTERVAL IN THE BATCH RE-DERIVES.** The spine 551–560, monotonic, zero violations, and every interval printed with its operands: 59 · 3 · 1 · 1 · 5 · 3 · 3 · 3 · 5 · 9. 1,410 = 3 × 360 + 330. 1,406 = 30 × 46 + 26, so the office is forty-six months and twenty-six days old at 551. 1,406 − 758 = 648 = 360 + 270 + 18, which is the age of the second column at 551. 1,409 − 1,334 = 75. 1,411 − 1,334 = 77. 1,408 − 1,409 = 13. 1,439 − 1,409 = 30. 1,439 − 1,411 = 28.

## 2. WHAT THIS BATCH OWED AND DELIVERED

- **The sheet at 551**, one page, eight hundred and forty-one and about four hundred and nineteen and one, one question from a clerk who is permitted one, a drawer that has been open since the fourth of February of the first year and is not opened.
- **The sixteenth sheet at 552**, the figure standing still through a third quarter, two blank boxes at the foot of a repainting form, about nine thousand people under a wall a city cannot read, and a shutter at the top of a hill with four lines on it and no name at the top of them.
- **The book off the shelf at 553**, a rule about the first block of a year, six words with no ordinal, a woman at a far table asked nothing for the fifth time, and two hours of a man not being allowed to ask.
- **The scene at 554**, Hanneke Ruis, her one question in eleven years, spent, and the account still open because she wrote the six words first.
- **A notice board at 555** with a drawing pin in it, a line cut off with a blade, and the first kind thing anybody has said about the column, which was not about him.
- **A shutter at 556**, a true notice, the reason he did not go up the hill said out loud on a street, and two words in chalk that about nine thousand people a day cannot read.
- **Thirteen days and a new word at 557** — *asked for* — a man who shuts doors, and the first list in this volume that anybody made for free.
- **Nine hundred against nine hundred at 558**, worked out on their own, not the same nine hundred, and the other half of the difference is that about nine in ten of what a person walks in with cannot name the day.
- **A demand at 559** with four lines off a shutter chalked onto it, and one hundred and ninety days of a man choosing not to do anything.
- **He says yes out loud at 560**, and the true answer to the only question anybody has asked him in a yard in four years, which is nothing.

## 3. THE IRREVERSIBLE DECISION THIS BATCH OWES, AND IT IS 560, AND IT IS HIS

**HE SAYS YES. Out loud, in his own voice, in a yard in daylight, to a woman he has never met, with about eleven thousand people present: the four lines on that board are the ones he wrote.** He does not add why the fourth of them is finished, because a man who explains the limits of his own sentence to a crowd is making a speech. And when the woman who has been in that yard for nine years asks him what he is going to do about the people the four lines leave out, **he says nothing, and he says it again, and it is the true answer.**

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of the four conditions being a test that only his own office administers, and with it the option of undoing any of it. A thing said out loud in a yard cannot be unsaid, and a definition written in a kitchen off Ferrier Street cannot be withdrawn by the man who wrote it once he has told eleven thousand people in public that he wrote it.

**WHAT IT COSTS:** the condition he spent four years building is finished as a test for ever, and the people it was written for are the people it now discards. The Register becomes a book of names and not a book of deeds. **No one in that yard agreed with him, no one said a good word to him on the way down the hill, nobody was thanked, Agnete Lindqvist was not in the yard and was not asked about, and the fourth condition was not on the board at all because somebody had cut it off with a blade and pinned it up in a corridor off a third court.**

## 4. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT DO

**It is not a climb.** Rank one throughout; no band, standing, permission, hour or terrace moves, and nothing in these ten chapters offers him anything. **It is not a re-argument of the return.** No chapter of 551–560 argues for or against the first of October and no character says the question is settled or that it is not. **It is not a second census.** No person is found in this batch by a division going to a door; every person in it arrives the way people arrive, which is that somebody brings a sheet to a door or a woman writes four lines and hands them to a clerk. **It is not a new enemy.** The opposition is Corvin Ashby, who is right, and the yard, which is also right. **It prints no figure of the account and no floor and `settle` in any inflection returns zero.** **It names no day of the week and no year number.** **It does not open his brother's file, and it does not write the name.** **It does not answer the clause of the twenty-first of June and does not lift it.** **It does not ask who put the white tile up and does not say.** **It does not resolve the account's reach, does not say whether the account belonged to the unrecorded rather than to him, and does not say whether the return was justice or showing off.** **It does not discuss Perrin Sowe.** **It does not touch Mavis Rooke, and `grep -ri "mavis rooke" chapters/volume-12/` returns 0, which is correct for this batch.** **It does not fill either of the two blank boxes and it does not print the four-hundred-a-day figure at `chapter-0545.md`:87, which contradicts `chapter-0489.md`:21 by more than twenty times.** **It does not put Emil Brack on a page, and it does not make him wrong about anything.**

## 5. THE STANDING RULE FOR EVERY ARITHMETIC ROW FROM HERE ON

> **A gate row that quotes the prose it is checking cannot catch the prose. Every interval is derived from the spine, twice, with the operands printed — never copied out of the chapter it is meant to be checking.**

**AND THE PROOF OF IT IS THE FIRST ENTRY OF §1 ABOVE: THE ERROR THIS BATCH FOUND WAS IN ITS OWN BRIEF, AND IT WAS FOUND BY RE-DERIVING, NOT BY READING.**

## WHERE HE STANDS AT 551, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

He is at rank one on a branch night shift he asked for, eleven at night until half past five, four nights and three off, a white plate with his name on it and no figure cut into it anywhere, and the rota page at that branch now carries four names on it. The Register of Unrecorded Merit has been open forty-one months and ten days as counted off the first of February of the first year, has a room with two chairs and a drawer that has been open and empty since the fourth of February of that year, and a book in which the second column, ruled on the ninth of March of the third year, was empty for seven quarter-ends (**561 days at the thirtieth of September of year four — 1,319 − 758 — and 576 at the fifteenth of October — 1,334 − 758; REPAIRED 14 OCTOBER 2026, it printed *five quarters* on eight lines**) and has one line in it. **That line is his.** On the fifteenth of October he entered, in his own hand and entered exactly as given by a clerk who may only enter, a claim against himself: that the thing returned on the first of October was taken from about a million people and that he decided who received it. Of the four conditions, three were met and the fourth was not, and the reason is that the thing it describes is on a page in this city. It cannot be struck. It goes out on a public desk on the thirtieth of December. A woman who dug a street for six years and was never paid is the Register's first creditor and she is two flights up off Cordwain, and **he is never going to tell her, and the page will — whether she ever reads that page is open and is carried to the next volume.** A street four streets off the yard end that has been counted four times in thirty years was read once in ninety days and every person it found lost the fourth condition of his own definition. The Church has about eleven thousand in its yard, a column it intends to fill, and a chaplain who has lost it. Emil Brack's offer — every figure he has invented, the method, and nine he decided off nothing, for one line in the office's hand saying he makes them up — **is unanswered and he is undefeated and he is right about every fact.** The slates are still in a lane off Cordwain, the wall still cannot be read by anybody, and the white tile under the last tile still has his name on it in chalk and nothing whatever under it, and nobody in this city has put it there.

## WHAT THIS PHASE SETTLED

1. **A volume may close with its last scene four chapters before its last page.** 546 is the fifth and last scene; 547–550 are prose and none is a scene. The four chapters after it are where the engine turns in institutions, and each changes something. The reader is left holding an undecided man who has just made himself the first debtor of his own register and a wall that four years of his handwriting have not made legible to anybody.
2. **The arithmetic failed five times in Volume 11, not once, and all five were applied on 14 October 2026 in the commit that wrote `outline/volume-12.md`.** **542's forty went out for three days and not four, and the figure carried on ten lines including the title and a section heading** · **544's sheet is the fifteenth quarter and not the sixteenth** · **544's satchel came in on the eleventh of July and not the ninth** · **the column had been ruled one year and twenty-one days at 537 and one year, six months and twenty-one days at 544, and two chapters said four years** · **548 enumerated seven empty quarter-ends and then said five, and the figure carried on eight lines in five chapters.** The rate of failure of the sentence at `outline/volume-11.md` §9 was V8 ×5, V9 ×2, V10 ×1, **V11 ×5**; the count does not fall when a repair is applied, it only stops growing.
3. **The title bank was re-run by command in the same commit and stands at zero duplicates across all five hundred and fifty files.** `chapter-0542.md` is *Three Days Of Looking* and **no written chapter was renamed to make room for it.** `chapter-0534.md` is still *What The Church Did Not Bring* and §10 of `outline/volume-12.md` rules on it.
3. **`PC-150`'s thread is open, live and dated.** Hanneke Ruis's last appearance is 544, ten days before the name exists. Nothing in 501–550 shows her reading it. **The second of January, year five.**
4. **The contradiction at 548 is repaired and the question is open.** Whether Mavis Rooke ever learns what was done for her is not decided anywhere.
5. **All four defects the 14 October review repaired hold.**
6. **All twelve proposals `PC-143`–`PC-153` were earned and none was adopted silently.** `PC-153` is printed at one chapter of Volume 11 and no other.
7. **The corrected spine is right and is now the spine every later phase checks against**: 541 = 1,240 · 542 = 1,261 · 543 = 1,284 · 544 = 1,319 · 545 = 1,324 · 546 = 1,325 · 547 = 1,329 · 548 = 1,334 · 549 = 1,341 · 550 = 1,347. Monotonic, zero violations, summing 117 from 1,230.

- **Aged's sentence and Agnete's forty at 542**, ninety-one doors and no questions, and the two words *useful* and *owed* coming apart in a yard in three days in front of the woman who spent nine years making them one word. **THE FIGURE WAS *FOUR DAYS* ON THE PAGE ON TEN LINES OF 542 UNTIL 14 OCTOBER 2026.**
- **A street that lost its fourth condition at 543**, a woman with a half page in one book and a step in another, and neither page wrong and neither able to be joined to the other.
- **Six words under a keeper's name at 544**, a quarter that entered nothing for ninety days, and a woman telling him what *no holder* actually means, which is the thing he is about to break.
- **A broom and four feet of stone at 545**, the reason he does not ask, and the leaving that is the decision 548 completes.
- **The scene at 546**, Agnete Lindqvist, the fifth and last scene of the volume, in a room that has no door on a street where nothing said can be used against anybody.
- **A creditor at 547**, up two flights on the morning a debt with no creditor came due, and a building with no door on it.
- **The claim against himself at 548**, four lines and a name in the one column nobody had been able to fill.
- **Nine things at 549**, said in a kitchen, none of them telling him he was right.
- **An ordinary morning at 550**, a rota with four names on it, and a verdict on the volume written out in his own hand.

## 2. THE IRREVERSIBLE DECISION THIS BATCH OWES, AND IT IS 548, AND IT IS HIS

**HE ENTERS A CLAIM AGAINST HIMSELF.** Four lines on the office's foolscap at eleven minutes past ten on the morning of the fifteenth of October, in his own hand, into the column headed *carried by*, which has been ruled since the ninth of March of the third year and had been empty through seven quarter-ends. **REPAIRED 14 OCTOBER 2026; this row printed *five quarters*.** A clerk reads it twice at the same speed both times and enters it exactly as given, and reads the four conditions aloud before she does, and three are met and the fourth is not, and she says why: the thing it describes is on a page in this city.

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of being a man who did a thing rather than a man who owes one. For four years he has been the instrument that asks and has asked for nothing traceable to his own hands; 518 spent a woman's safety, 530 spent the appearance of evidence, 537 spent the option of being a man nobody looks for, and 548 spends the last of it.

**WHAT IT COSTS:** the Register's first creditor is a man who cannot pay it. Every person who comes to that counter afterwards is coming to a register with a debtor in it. Six words that have held an account open for thirty years on the strength of there being nobody's name on them now have a name against them, in a chained book, two floors below a sales floor, and a keeper who does not know yet. The sheet of the thirtieth of December puts it on a public desk. **And the woman it was done for is two flights up off Cordwain and will not thank him and he would not want her to, and he is never going to tell her, and the page will.**

## 3. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT DO

**It is not a climb.** Rank one throughout; no band, standing, permission, hour or terrace moves, and nothing in these ten chapters offers him anything. **It is not a re-argument of the return.** No chapter of 541–550 argues for or against the first of October and no character in them says the question is settled or that it is not; 547's is about a creditor, 548's is about an entry, and 550's is about a verdict on a book. **It is not a new enemy.** **It is consequence-free in nothing.** **It does not answer Ines Duarte's question about the white tile, which was asked in three parts on the sixth of March of the first year** — 545 puts him four feet off the only person in this city who has offered to tell him what she knows, he does not ask, and the leaving is the point. **It does not open his brother's file, which `outline/ending.md` alone decides, and it does not write the name.** **It does not fill the second column by arrangement, and it does not make the column the Church's** — the Church has decided to fill it and he has put one line in it ahead of them, and Agnete has told him she is not going to stop them and has lost her yard. **It does not treat Emil Brack's offer as accepted or refused; he is at the wall end of the lane at about a quarter to seven on the last morning and neither of them says a word and he goes back to his stools.** **It does not let the Bureau reach a slate.** **It prints no figure of the account and no floor.** **It names no day of the week and no year number.**

## 4. THE TWELVE PROPOSALS, AND WHERE THEY ARE

`PC-143` the satchel and the four hundred and nineteen accounts at 541 · `PC-144` the Church's forty and the parting of *useful* from *owed* at 542 · `PC-145` Sela Voel and the death of the fourth condition at 543 · `PC-146` what *no holder* means and what it costs to break it at 544 · `PC-147` the broom, the four feet of stone, and the reason he does not ask at 545 · `PC-148` the arrangement in the Vestry at 546 and the yard she has lost · `PC-149` Mavis Rooke as the Register's first creditor, at 547 · `PC-150` the claim against himself, the failed fourth condition, and the one line in the second column at 548 · `PC-151` the clause of the twenty-first of June reaching six people into a kitchen on the twenty-second at 549 · `PC-152` the rota page with four names, the woman with the bucket, and the verdict at 550 · `PC-153` the lane's age, printed once in this batch, with its operands, at 550. **All twelve are PROPOSED, all twelve are on the page, `PC-142` is not renumbered and nothing below it moved.** The full table is at the head of `state/continuity.md`.

## 5. THE ONE SENTENCE THIS BLOCK HANDS ON

> **A man at rank one put his own name in the one column of his own book that nobody had been able to fill, in a hand of his own, against a debt he cannot pay, on a day when the woman he did it for came up two flights without a hand on the rail and asked him whether being found was being owed or being caught — and he could not answer her, and nine women told him what it had cost in order since a kitchen in July of the second year, and not one of them told him he was right.**

**MOVEMENT FOUR, SWEPT, IS WRITTEN AND CLOSED ON THE PAGE AT 540. ## 0. WHERE THE MAN STANDS AT CHAPTER 541, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

He is at rank one on a branch night shift he asked for, eleven at night until half past five, a white plate with his name on it and no figure cut into it anywhere, and he is still not climbing and nothing in this batch offered him anything. **The Register of Unrecorded Merit has been open thirty-three months and three days**, has a room with two chairs in it, a clerk who may only enter, a ratchet on a corridor wall that stood at one thousand and sixty-nine on the thirtieth of December of the third year, and **a standing sheet whose quarterly figure stood still for ninety days and then went from thirty-nine to eight hundred and forty-one in one quarter, which are two opposite things under one heading on one page**. The second column, headed *carried by*, has been empty since the ninth of March of the third year and went through a quarter with eight hundred and forty-one things in the first column without being touched. **A woman who fed a boy from a school off Cordwain for nine days is still on the sheet and cannot be taken off it.** A man in a delivery coat has offered every figure he has invented in two years, and the method, and about nine he decided off nothing at all, for one line in the office's hand saying he makes them up, and has not been answered; he was at the wall end of a lane on the thirtieth of March of the fourth year at about a quarter to seven and looked at Lin Hao for a while and went back to his stools. A landlord off Lamplit Row has a finding against a woman who cannot read and has been in number four for nine years, and the finding is what landlords show when they refuse rooms, and the board that made it will not count its own book. **A division of nine named Ilma Reese, Coen Ardry, Tenna Vole, Sef Ossick, Marget Halle, Duri Peck, Annis Cray, Toller Vine and Beatrix Halm has been to about nine hundred doors in a district that is not anybody's, and about four hundred and nineteen turned lines with a time beside every one and no figure on any of them are on a piece of paper, and nineteen of them were fetched by his asking.** He has spent a woman who was the reason he could do anything, on a roof, in the wind, and cannot write any of it down. He has not taken back the sentence of the thirtieth of September of the third year and it has stopped his counter for a quarter and it was read out in a ward by a doctor who is not grateful for it. And in about ninety-nine days it is the tenth of October of the fourth year and there is a morning in this city that has been named for four years by a woman who has never been thanked for naming it.

## 1. WHAT THIS BATCH OWED AND DELIVERED

- **Four hours at the head of a lane at 531, a figure that is a sum and not a reading, nine people sent by the person who owes one of them, and Brack's sentence about four hours of a woman being indistinguishable from a person who did something.**
- **A woman sent back for a witness at 532, a rule that predates the office by two years, and the first sentence in this batch that puts the second instrument and the first one together and does not come from him.**
- **A rent board that takes anything at 533, a landlord who writes his own figure's origin into the book and cannot stop it mattering, and a woman who cannot read and is not in the room.**
- **A quarter with nothing in it at 534, thirty-four unchanged for ninety days, and the reason his own sentence stopped his own counter.**
- **The division of nine at 535, their rule carried and named by nobody, and the price she names: a straight sentence about what the office does with a return.**
- **The scene at 536, the roof, the terms never stated, and the first time in this book that anybody has asked him for anything.**
- **The sweep at 537, asked for in writing on a tally board, entered by a clerk, and the forty days he did not spend.**
- **Four hundred turned lines read straight through at 538, and nineteen he sent her to fetch, and the nineteenth is a chair up a stair.**
- **A Bureau officer who could not refuse him and does not at 539, and the knock instead of the finding.**
- **A ward, a body in a bed, and a true sentence that does the opposite of what it was written for at 540.**

## 2. THE IRREVERSIBLE DECISION THIS BATCH OWNS, AND IT IS HIS

**537. HE ASKS FOR THE SWEEP.** A Bureau division of nine, door to door, reading what people did that was never written down, in a district that is neither his nor hers, over about nine hundred doors. Four lines, a date, and his own name at the bottom of a bin-room tally board at four in the morning, entered exactly as given.

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of being a man nobody looks for. He has been unmeasured for four years, and a man who is unmeasured is a man nobody comes for, and he says on the page that he cannot have both.

**WHAT IT COSTS:** every person it finds is a person somebody has decided was owed something and finding them is not the same as owing them, which is the second line of his own request; a doctor gave him both figures in January of the second year and he printed them on a sheet in September and it cost him a quarter of a counter; **he had forty days and did not spend one of them, and he writes that down in ten words**; a knock makes a street visible to about four neighbours; a return no classification in this city covers has to be carried by one officer in a satchel for ninety days; and a third of what it finds is in a bed by the first of July with a sentence read out to it. **There is no instrument in this city that can stop it and none that will stop him, because he is the whole instrument of an office that has none.**

## 3. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT DO

**It is not a climb.** Rank one the whole way through; no band, standing, permission, hour or terrace moves, and nothing in these ten chapters offers him anything. **It is not a re-argument of the return.** No chapter of 531–540 argues for or against the first of October and no character in them says the question is settled or that it is not; 537's sentence is about owing, 539's is about a knock, 540's is about a page, and the one line that gestures forward at 540 is about a morning in October and not about what was returned. **It is not a new enemy.** **It is consequence-free in nothing.** **It does not answer Ines Duarte's question about the white tile** — it puts a man in a corridor reading a page and looking at the board at the bottom of the hill and prints nothing under the tile. **It does not open his brother's file, which `outline/ending.md` alone decides.** **It does not fill the second column, and it does not make the column the Church's.** **It does not treat Emil Brack's offer as accepted or refused, and it does not make him a villain, a criminal, or diminished; at 533 his honesty is written into a court's book and it does not help anybody, which is worse for the office than it is for him.** **It does not let the Bureau reach a slate.**

## 4. THE ELEVEN PROPOSALS, AND WHERE THEY ARE

`PC-132` the cost of four hours in a line at 531 · `PC-133` the woman sent back for a witness at 532 · `PC-134` what a room does with a figure at 533 · `PC-135` the quarter's fall at 534 · `PC-136` the division of nine and its rule at 535 · `PC-137` the arrangement on a roof at 536 · `PC-138` the reading round itself at 537 · `PC-139` the four hundred and nineteen at 538 · `PC-140` what the man who asked for it is told at 539, and by whom · `PC-141` Tessa Okonkwo's figures landing in a ward at 540 · `PC-142` the lane's age at 531 and 537. **All eleven are PROPOSED, all eleven are on the page, `PC-131` is not renumbered and nothing below it moved.** The full table is at the head of `state/continuity.md`.

## 5. THE ONE SENTENCE THIS BLOCK HANDS ON
