## 0. WHERE THE MAN STANDS AT CHAPTER 531, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

He is at rank one on a branch night shift he asked for, eleven at night until half past five, a white plate with his name on it and no figure cut into it anywhere. An office called the Register of Unrecorded Merit has been open thirty-two months, which is thirty-one months and twenty-nine days off the letter of the first of February, has a room off a third court with two chairs in it, a Registrar, a clerk who may only enter and cannot improve what she is given, a ratchet counter in the corridor that says nine hundred and eleven people past the door in twenty-one months, thirty-four entries, and **two columns, the second of which is headed with the two words off the foot of the ranks wall and has never had anything under it.** He has bought a notebook with a printed label on it and a second ledger four years after he handed the first one over, and the chapter says out loud that the difference between them is that nobody will ever read this one. A woman of forty-one sat on a chair in her own rooms with her left hand flat on a closed book for a whole evening and gave him a figure for Lamplit Row out of her own memory with nothing behind it, and told him it did not go in the Register, and he entered it on the twenty-seventh of January. A woman who fed a boy from a school off Cordwain for nine days and paid for it out of her own hands has an entry and has asked to be struck out of it and cannot be, because a clerk may only enter and there is no striking in this city, and her brother has read the sheet and said out loud in a kitchen with his sister there that a thing on a page is a thing she can be asked to do again. A man in a delivery coat has offered every figure he has written in two years, and the method, and about nine he decided off nothing at all, for one line on a public sheet saying that he makes them up, and has not been answered. And on the thirtieth of September of the third year he wrote four words longer than a clerk is permitted to add and they went out at four with an address at the head of every line: *where this Register has nothing, it has not been given anything, and it does not say that there was nothing.* The Church's hundred stopped coming in the first week of October. The white tile under the last tile of the ranks wall still has his name on it in chalk and nothing whatever under it.

## 1. WHAT THIS BATCH OWED AND DELIVERED

- **The wall at 521, the fourth hundred re-set so that it cannot be divided, a counter nobody had ever read, and a ratio said out loud once: one in nine hundred.**
- **A second ledger at 522, bought six days before the question that made it, and four things said to him by the only person who could say them.**
- **The Register's first entry that is a person's complaint at 523, a landlord who asked the wrong question and got the standing answer, and the door that opens on the first of January.**
- **Agnete Lindqvist at that counter first at 524, as she promised in a kitchen, bringing the one thing she has, which fails the fourth condition because she wrote it down on purpose in order to refuse.**
- **The scene at 525, the terms never spoken, a broker's left hand on a closed book for a whole evening, and a figure entered against the giver's instruction.**
- **The wrong question at 526 and the two words off the wall in a woman's mouth four years before anybody put them in a column.**
- **The second column at 527, headed *carried by*, entered in the office's own hand, empty at four o'clock, and a clerk who says the emptiness is correct.**
- **Cordwain split at 528, a hundred of the Church down a hill in eleven days, an hour spent standing against a wall doing nothing, and an offer made and not taken.**
- **A number that went down at 529 and was not wrong, and a prediction written nine days before it came true.**
- **A sentence printed at 530 about what the Register is not, and a woman who cannot be taken off a public sheet.**

## 2. THE TWO IRREVERSIBLE DECISIONS THIS BATCH OWNS, AND BOTH ARE HIS

**525. HE ENTERS A FIGURE AGAINST THE WOMAN WHO GAVE IT TO HIM'S EXPLICIT INSTRUCTION.** A street and a figure and no name, on the twenty-seventh of January, entered by a clerk who read it twice at the same speed both times and asked him nothing.

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of the Register being a record of deeds and nothing else. The first entry in that book that is not a deed, not a name and not a complaint, and the first number in it that anybody in Ostara would want.

**WHAT IT COSTS:** the only figure a senior broker knows out of her own memory and her guild cannot account for is now connected by a door he stands in to the only office in Ostara that cannot be audited. She traded for eleven years on the proposition that nothing she carries is ever said out loud, and she said it out loud once, to one man with no instrument, in a room where the rule is that nothing is said out loud. He never apologises to her in this batch.

**530. HE PRINTS THE SENTENCE THAT SAYS WHAT HIS OWN REGISTER IS NOT.** *Where this Register has nothing, it has not been given anything, and it does not say that there was nothing.* Nine seconds, the office's foolscap, his hand, and a clerk who enters it exactly as given because a clerk may enter what she is given and not make it shorter.

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of the Register's number being usable, by anybody, including the people it was built for. A standing sheet goes out at four with an address at the head of every line and cannot be un-entered by anybody including the man who wrote it.

**WHAT IT COSTS:** it protects nobody and he says so himself; about four thousand people are told that the only thing anybody in this city can hand in without being asked for it is not what a public page means; the Church's hundred stop coming in the first week of October; Agnete Lindqvist does not come and does not write; and he has spent the last thing the office had, which was the appearance of being evidence. It is the sentence Tessa Okonkwo asked for in a room off a ward on the twenty-first of January of year two and he has had it in a drawer for a year and a half.

## 3. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT DO

**It is not a climb.** Rank one the whole way through; no band, standing, permission or hour of his own moves. **It is not a re-argument of the return.** No chapter of 521–530 argues for or against the first of October and no character in them says the question is settled or that it is not; 522's one hard sentence is about what a ratio of people-at-a-door counts and is not about the return. **It is not a new enemy.** **It is consequence-free in nothing.** **It does not answer Ines Duarte's question about the tile, which is still on the page from the sixth of March of year one and is unanswered** — 521 and 530 both print the tile and print nothing under it, and 521 re-sets the four hundred deliberately so that no later phase can derive a rate from it. **And it does not open his brother's file, which `outline/ending.md` alone decides.**

## 4. THE SIX PROPOSALS, AND WHERE THEY ARE

`PC-126` the ratchet counter in the corridor and the figure on it · `PC-127` the notebook and the second ledger · `PC-128` the Register's first entry that is a person's complaint, and the landlord whose door opens on the first of January · `PC-129` the card, the scene's rule, and the figure entered against the giver's instruction · `PC-130` the second column and why the two words off the wall were chosen, which is on the page a chapter before the column · `PC-131` Emil Brack's offer, unanswered. **All six are PROPOSED, all six are on the page, `PC-125` is not renumbered and nothing below it moved.** The full table is at the head of `state/continuity.md`.

**AND THE DISCREPANCIES CARRIED AND NOT REPAIRED, UNCHANGED BY THIS BATCH:** `chapter-0500.md:65` prints an interval that does not derive, and **no chapter of 521–530 prints it or derives anything from it** · `chapter-0496.md:119` and `chapter-0507.md:7` disagree about which block of the keeper's quarterly is the hundred and twenty-first, and **no chapter of 521–530 prints an ordinal for that block at all**, describing it as the fourth block with six words and her name · **and the requisition of the twentieth of February is still carried with five seals and unanswered.**

## 5. THE ONE SENTENCE THIS BLOCK HANDS ON

> **A man at rank one spent an hour leaning against the wall of a lane doing nothing, was offered every number in it by a man in a delivery coat for the price of one line on a public sheet, and said nothing at all — and then printed, on the same kind of sheet, a sentence admitting that where his Register has nothing it has not been told anything, which saved nobody, stopped the only organised body in the city from bringing it anything at all, and was the last thing the office had that looked like evidence.**

> In this volume, **Lin Hao must write the definition of an unrecorded deed for an office that has no instrument, no money and no column**, or **Ostara's second line gets a threshold under it that a man with a chalk chose, and the city's unmeasured half is settled in lanes**.

**MOVEMENT TWO, DEFINED, IS WRITTEN AND CLOSED ON THE PAGE AT 520. IT DELIVERED THE ROOM, THE JOKE, THE CHURCH'S DECISION, THE FIRST DEED, THE SCENE, THE LOCKED DOOR, THE FORTY DAYS, THE RECUSAL, THE DEFINITION AND A NAME IN A BOOK. IT DELIVERED ITS IRREVERSIBLE DECISION AT 518.**

## 0. WHERE THE MAN STANDS AT CHAPTER 521, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

He is at rank one on a branch night shift he asked for, four nights and three off, eleven at night until half past five, a white plate with his name on it and no figure cut into it anywhere. An office called the Register of Unrecorded Merit has a room off a third court with two chairs and a table in it, and for eight months two people came to that door, and it has taken a sentence from him that is four lines long and has a threshold in it: a deed is unrecorded if all four of four things are true of it, and it excludes about nine in ten of what people will bring, and he said the number out loud in a kitchen and did not explain it. To get that sentence classified he had to lift the recusal of the only officer in this city who can classify a word, and he lifted it on a slip the size of a quarter of a page in about four seconds with eleven words on it, and she is fifty-seven and was back on a record in four days and refused four classifications out loud and did not thank him, and the price of it is a room on that floor with no window and a file in it that he stood outside for about a minute and a half and did not knock. The Church of the Quiet Number is about four thousand and has decided that if the Register ever gets a column it will fill that column before the Bureau does, over about nine years, at the pace of one street. A woman who does needlework for four houses off Cordwain has been owed four loaves since the second month of the first year and cannot put the reason on a sheet, because the sheet goes out at four with an address at the head of every line, so the Register's first entry off a street is her name and not her deed. And the white tile under the last tile of the ranks wall still has his name on it in chalk and nothing at all under it, and he has now told one other person in this city that he does not know who put it there.

## 1. WHAT THIS BATCH OWED AND DELIVERED

- **The room at 511, and the office's first real deadline: eighty-two days to the thirtieth of December, and a blank form in a drawer since the fourth of February that nobody in that building will fill in.**
- **The joke said out loud once at 512, by the man whose title it is, to the first person he has ever told it to, and neither of them explains it. The name was on the form before the office existed.**
- **The Church's four thousand and its nine-year decision at 513, made out loud and against his own interest, with forty of its own paying a grain a head to stand in a lane off Cordwain and the figure on the slate not going up by forty.**
- **The first deed offered to the office at 514, and the reason it cannot be put on paper, and the reason is not resolved.**
- **Tessa Okonkwo's scene at 515 and the quiet carried by a latch, and the division of nine that is about to start going to doors, and what a third of what it finds will cost her.**
- **The locked door at 516: the only door in this city a definition can come out of, and one man who can open it, and four hundred and sixty-eight days of a recusal he has not lifted. Five hundred and fifty-eight is the thirtieth of June, which is 518, and it is printed there.**
- **The forty days at 517, derived on the page, and six words in a keeper's hand that are worth everything because nothing is behind them.**
- **The batch's irreversible decision at 518: he lifts the recusal, on a slip, in four seconds.**
- **The definition at 519, narrow, read out to nine women who each break it once and improve none of it, and the number it excludes.**
- **A name in the Register at 520, entered by a clerk who may only enter, three days short of two years.**

## 2. THE IRREVERSIBLE DECISION THIS BATCH OWNS, AND IT IS HIS

**518. HE LIFTS ALMA QUAYLE'S RECUSAL.** *This recusal is lifted by me now and by nobody else.* Eleven words, his name, the date, a slip the size of a quarter of a page, no form, about four seconds, on the thirtieth of June of the second year, on the last working day of the quarter.

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of never spending another woman's safety, which was the last one he had that had cost nothing. He had spent the other one on the thirtieth of September of the first year and entered thirty-eight unbreakable words on the twenty-first of June before that.

**WHAT IT COSTS:** a woman who has spent thirty years arranging to be the person who can finally say what a thing is goes back on a record at fifty-seven, without being asked, in the building where a room with no window holds a file he has not opened in two years. She is not diminished. She refuses four classifications out loud in her first four days. She does not thank him and tells him to be more frightened if she turns out ordinary than if she turns out like this.

## 3. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT DO

## 0. WHERE THE MAN STANDS AT CHAPTER 511, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

Four months after the wall was repainted, and a hundred and twenty-one days after he wrote a woman down a hill in October, a letter arrived on the paper the Correction Office has and said three things: the office exists, **Yusuf Adeyemi is its Registrar**, and **Lin Hao is the only person the office may ask**. He did not ask for it and there is nothing to refuse it into, and he is not the Registrar and was never offered the title. He then paid a grain of merit to stand in a lane off Cordwain behind nine other people for about four hours and let a man in a delivery coat write his name on a slate in chalk about two inches high beside a figure of nine, and he has not asked who put a white tile with his name on it under the last tile of the ranks wall and nobody has told him. **On the thirtieth of September the Registrar put the office's one question — strike nine private slates out of this city by writing a sentence that goes out at four with an address at the head of every line — and he refused in one sentence, and the refusal was entered whole, and the slates stay, and there are going to be more of them.** A landlord off Lamplit Row is quoting one of them into a rent. A keeper two floors under a sales floor has written six words out of her own memory for the hundred and twenty-first time and one of them is going onto that slate in chalk. The lane is three hundred and fifty-eight mornings old and three days short of a year.

## 1. WHAT THIS BATCH OWED AND DELIVERED

- **The office, named on the page at 501, with the title Adeyemi's per `outline/ending.md`:255, and the office's one permitted question put to Lin Hao.** The ruling in `outline/volume-11.md` §2c item 2 held and is on the page: **no chapter of 501–510 calls him the Registrar of Unrecorded Merit, offers him a title, has him refuse one or has him take one.** He is handed a sentence with no number in it that makes him the answer to the only question an office with no instrument, no money and no column may put to anybody, and that is why the Registrar comes to him at 510 and can go to nobody else.
- **Emil Brack, named at 502 and described before he speaks.** An opponent who cannot be defeated, checked, licensed or appealed, who is right about every fact and is not a criminal and does not profit where a person does not lose. The flag `AGENTS.md` requires is raised in `state/continuity.md` and `state/open-threads.md`.
- **Four hours and a grain at 504, and a name in chalk that nobody will ever attach to him.**
- **Ines Duarte's scene at 505: the confession that spends the last private thing he had, and the question about the tile that is asked and not answered.**
- **The Bureau's worst answer to the best problem at 506, in a clerk's own hand, going out at four.**
- **The hundred and twenty-first block and the newest instrument in the city meeting in a room with a lamp in it at 507, and not agreeing.**
- **The Register's first entry and its first public figure at 508: one, about one person in nine hundred, and nothing on the page to check it against.**
- **Nine slates in nine districts that do not agree, and two of them right about a thing the Register has wrong, at 509.**
- **The refusal at 510, in one sentence, closing the option of striking down a rival measure.**

## 2. THE IRREVERSIBLE DECISION THIS BATCH OWNS, AND IT IS HIS

**510. HE REFUSES TO STRIKE THE SLATES DOWN.** *Two words went onto that wall off a sheet I answered, and I am not going to take another man's two words off a piece of slate for standing on the wrong surface.*

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the option of striking down a rival measure, for four years and on this evidence for ever. The slates stay in nine districts and become the thing he has to answer for.
**WHAT IT COSTS:** he is the reason there is a second institution in Ostara. He cannot afterwards be a man who defended measurability in a room of ninety people and watched a private chalk measure his city.

## 3. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT DO

**It is not a climb.** He is at rank one the whole way through and no band, standing, permission or hour of his own moves. **It is not a re-argument of the return.** No chapter of 501–510 argues for or against the first of October and no character in them says the question is settled or that it is not; 499's two-things sentence is referred to at 508 and 509 and is not awarded in either direction. **It is not a new enemy.** **It is consequence-free in nothing.** **And it does not open Lin Wei's file, which `outline/ending.md` alone decides.**

## 0. WHERE THE MAN STANDS AT CHAPTER 501, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

Three days after he read a figure off a public desk that was not zero, a letter arrives at a branch office off a third court on the paper the Correction Office has, and it says three things: the office exists, Yusuf Adeyemi is its Registrar, and Lin Hao is the only person the office may ask. He is rank one, with a white plate in his coat and no figure cut into it anywhere, and he has been on an eleven-at-night-to-half-past-five rota for eleven weeks and three days because rank one gets assigned work and because he asked. Below the second line on the ranks wall there is a white tile with his name on it in chalk and nothing under it, and he stands under it about two minutes at a time and has done about sixty times since the first of January and about four hundred people have gone past him doing it, which is a total and not a rate, and he does not know who put it there. In a lane off Cordwain a man in a delivery coat has taken a stool and a line every morning since the evening of the second of October and takes a grain a head off anybody who wants to stand in it, and chalks a figure in four-inch letters, and has never once said the Board took his words: a hundred and twenty-seven mornings of it by the ninth of February, and three hundred and fifty-eight by the thirtieth of September, which is three days short of a year. And somewhere under the Reading a keeper with a lamp is writing a street's composition into a chained book out of her own memory with nothing behind it, four times a year, for thirty years.

## 1. THE SHAPE OF VOLUME 11, AND EVERY FIGURE IN IT IS A PLAN

- **Chapters 501–550. It comes due in about four years, on a morning nobody has chosen, in a building with no door on it."* **Four years is 1,440 days at 360 to the month. The sitting was the tenth of October. The morning is the tenth of October of year four, which is 1,329 days after the first of February, which is CHAPTER 547.**

## 2. THE FIVE IRREVERSIBLE DECISIONS VOLUME 11 OWNS, AND ALL FIVE ARE HIS

**510 — HE REFUSES TO STRIKE THE SLATES DOWN.** One sentence, on the ground that he put two words on a wall himself and cannot be the man who takes another man's two words off a slate for standing on the wrong surface. **Closes: the option of striking down a rival measure. Costs: he is the reason there is a second institution in Ostara.**
**518 — HE LIFTS ALMA QUAYLE'S RECUSAL**, unilaterally, on a slip, in about four seconds, because only the subject may lift one and he is the subject. **Closes: the option of never spending another woman's safety. Costs: a woman who has been careful for thirty years goes back on the record at fifty-seven, and the record is what put his brother in a room with no window.**
**519 — HE WRITES THE DEFINITION AND IT IS NARROW AND HE KNOWS IT IS NARROW.** **Closes: a Register that measures everything or nothing. Costs: about one person in nine hundred, and the threshold is his handwriting.**
**537 — HE ASKS FOR THE SWEEP.** A Bureau division, nine people, going door to door, reading turned lines, looking for what people did that was never written down. **The first census of the unrecorded in the history of this city, and it cannot be stopped by anybody. Closes: the option of being a man nobody looks for.**
**548 — HE ENTERS A CLAIM AGAINST HIMSELF** in the one column nobody has ever filled. **Closes: the option of being a man who did a thing rather than a man who owes a thing. Costs: the Register's first creditor is a man who cannot pay it.**

## 3. FOUR THINGS THIS PHASE FLAGS, AND NONE OF THEM IS ADOPTED SILENTLY

1. **THE MAN WITH THE SLATE IS NAMED EMIL BRACK, AT 502.** He is unnamed in 491–500 and **on the page six times — 489, 490, 491, 492, 493 and 498, and in a delivery coat in two of them, 491 and 493.** **He is not a new final enemy and this is the required flag: he is not new, he is rung three of the ladder in a form nobody has seen, and he is not defeated.** He is right about every fact and by the last movement he is the only man in Ostara who can say where every figure in Cordwain came from, because he wrote them.
2. **`outline/ending.md`:255 HAS FIXED THE APPOINTMENT OF YUSUF ADEYEMI AS REGISTRAR OF UNRECORDED MERIT SINCE BEFORE ANY CHAPTER CARRIED IT, AND `grep -rn 'Registrar' chapters/` RETURNS NOTHING ACROSS FIVE HUNDRED FILES.** Volume 11 puts it on the page at 501. That is carrying a fixed ending forward, not inventing an office. **THE RULING, WHICH IS NOT OPTIONAL AND WHICH THIS BLOCK PREVIOUSLY GOT WRONG IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION: THE TITLE IS ADEYEMI'S FROM THE DAY THE OFFICE EXISTS. The first draft of this block, of `outline/volume-11.md` and of `workspace/volume-11/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` all had card 501's letter appointing Lin Hao, which contradicted line 255 and the volume's own room row. The letter at 501 now says the office exists, names Adeyemi as its Registrar, and names Lin Hao as the only person the office may ask. No chapter of 501–550 may call him the Registrar of Unrecorded Merit, may have him refuse a title he is never offered, or may have him take one. It is also why 510 works: a Registrar with no instrument and one man to ask has to ask that man.**
3. **`chapter-0500.md:65` PRINTS AN INTERVAL THAT DOES NOT DERIVE AND A GATE BLOCK CERTIFIES IT.** *About six hundred and forty-five days off* for the books that would take the account meeting again on the fifteenth of November. **Derived twice against the twenty-eighth of January: `(30 − 28) + 30 × 9 + 15 = 287` to the fifteenth of November of that year, and `287 + 360 = 647` to the next. Neither is six hundred and forty-five, and the live gate block re-derives it as *ten months to the twenty-eighth of November, then thirty plus thirty times eleven, less fifteen*, which sums to 615.** **THIS PLANNING PHASE DID NOT REPAIR PROSE IT WAS NOT ASKED TO REPAIR AND DOES NOT ADOPT A SILENT CORRECTION. THE FIGURES ARE RECORDED WITH THEIR OPERANDS IN `outline/volume-11.md` §2c item 3 AND THE PROPOSAL IS CARRIED HERE.**
4. **THE REQUISITION OF THE TWENTIETH OF FEBRUARY HAS NO STATUS ON THE PAGE.** Carried with five seals on the thirtieth of June, administered weekly for a quarter, and the disposition of the first of October says *given away in the open, the first of October, holder none.* **Nothing in 451–500 discharges it, withdraws it or answers it. Volume 11 answers it once, in a room, and nobody in Ostara ever files it.**

## 4. WHAT VOLUME 11 DOES NOT DO

**It is not a climb.** He is at rank one and stays there; no chapter raises his band, buys a permission, offers him a standing or puts him back on a terrace. **It is not a re-argument of the return.** `outline/ending.md`:229–236 fixes the reach of the return as deliberately open and no chapter of 501–550 may argue for or against the first of October or say the question is settled or that it is not. **It is not a new enemy.** **It is not consequence-free.** **And it does not open Lin Wei's file, which `outline/ending.md` alone decides and which names the brother once, at 486.**

## 5. THE ONE SENTENCE THIS BLOCK HANDS ON

> **Four years after a man emptied an account at a wall, a letter arrives telling him an office exists, naming the man who is its Registrar, and naming him as the only person it is allowed to ask — and the only institution his act actually created is a lane off Cordwain where a man takes a grain a head off anybody who wants to stand in a line and writes the figure on a slate in letters the height of a name on a plate.**

# VOLUME 10 BATCH 0005 — CHAPTERS 491–500 ARE ON DISK. THE EIGHTH OF OCTOBER TO THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF JANUARY. **REPAIRED 13 OCTOBER 2026 AGAINST `logs/batch-0005.review.log`: TWELVE FINDINGS, ALL APPLIED, NO CHAPTER RESTARTED AND NO PLOT MOVED. THE VOLUME IS CLOSED IN PROSE AT 500. THIS IS THE LIVE BLOCK AND IT SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT.**

> **In this volume, **Lin Hao must decide who the reservoir belongs to and give it back while the Council watches**, or **Ostara keeps measuring the wrong thing for another twenty years**.
>
> **THE VOLUME IS WRITTEN. FIFTY CHAPTERS, 124,452 WORDS COUNTED PER FILE AND SUMMED BY COMMAND AFTER THE 13 OCTOBER 2026 REVIEW REPAIR (124,447 BEFORE IT). THE CENTRAL QUESTION WAS ANSWERED AT 486 AND PAID FOR FOUR THINGS, AND WHAT 491–500 CARRIES IS THE COUNTRY AFTER THE ANSWER: A WALL NOBODY CAN READ, TWO DAYS OF QUIET, A MAN DEFENDING THE BUREAU FROM THE BOTTOM OF THE LADDER, NINE WOMEN DISPERSED AND INTACT, A FAMILY THAT LOST ITS FILE, THIRTY YEARS OF ONE HANDWRITING, THREE WHO NEVER LIVED HERE AGAIN, A NIGHT SHIFT, FOUR HUNDRED LINES THAT ARE NOT HIS, AND A NUMBER THAT IS NOT ZERO. NOT ONE OF THOSE TEN CHAPTERS ARGUES THE RETURN AGAIN.**

## 0. WHERE HE STANDS AT THE END OF CHAPTER 500, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

On the eighth of October an office painted two words and a dash and two more words along the foot of the ranks wall at six in the morning, out of a repainting form, in a clerk's hand, and no person in Ostara has ever found out what it means. For two days Cordwain tried to decide whether the wall had been lying for thirty years, and Agnete Lindqvist read the line, said it was the Church's four words with the hand cut off, and refused to claim it. On the tenth he stood in a room that could carry nothing with ninety people in it and asked them to keep the Bureau, and a keeper of the Vant's real books read him the best case anybody in that room had heard, and he refused to argue for or against the first of October in front of them, and the minute went out at four with an address at the head of every line, and from that afternoon the Bureau of Merit survives on a sentence a man with no standing said in a room, and he has said out loud that it is his position and not the Board's merit and that he will not go back outside the door. On the seventeenth of October nine sworn women sat at one table in a kitchen off Ferrier Street, each of them said one thing and nobody answered anybody, and the ninth said hers last and the room agreed with her, and three of them have not slept in that house since the first of October and all three of them are on the December rota at his branch. On the twenty-fourth a carter off Lamplit Row was told at a public desk that his file is not lost and that nobody in this city can find it. On the thirtieth a keeper two floors under a sales floor set out four times a year for thirty years in one hand and said that the rule which has always been a defect is now the only thing standing. On the thirtieth of November he did not ask the three why they had gone and said out loud that he never will. On the first of January he cleaned washrooms from eleven at night until half past five because rank one gets assigned work and because he asked, a woman he has been living beside for four years filed a correction on the twenty-ninth of December and did not press it and neither of them said one word about what was in it, three women came in on the rota and worked, and on a wall two streets away there is a white tile with his name on it in chalk and nothing under it. On the fourteenth a woman read the second line on the wall, came down to a counter and asked whether she could put a thing she did in a doorway into a form, and he told her she cannot, and spent ninety seconds looking for her on a page of four hundred clock times and could not find her. And on the twenty-eighth of January he read the standing sheet of the quarter of the thirtieth of December at a public desk, and under the disposition there was one figure in a clerk's hand, and it is two hundred and eleven thousand, eight hundred and forty grains, and about one in seven of everything that went out on the first of October did not go anywhere, and it is not zero, and it belongs to nobody, and it will be on a page again on the thirtieth of March in a woman's handwriting under her own name.

## 1. WHAT THE FIGURES ARE NOW, AND WHERE EACH ONE LIVES

- **THE REQUISITION OF THE TWENTIETH OF FEBRUARY.** 130 days old at the thirtieth of June, and `130 + [days after the thirtieth of June]`. **228 printed at 491, 280 at 497, 324 at 499, each derived inside its own chapter with its operands, AND 499 ALSO PRINTS THE LADDER IT COMES OFF — 222 at the second of October, 228 at the eighth, 292 at the twelfth of December, 308 at the twenty-eighth. THE PRE-REPAIR 499 PRINTED A COUNT NO ARITHMETIC REACHED, INCLUDING A THIRTY-FIRST, AND SUMMED OPERANDS THAT DID NOT ADD UP; THE REPAIR OF 13 OCTOBER 2026 RE-SET IT ON THE REQUISITION SO THAT THE CHAPTER NOW DERIVES ITS OWN RESULT.** 492, 493, 494, 495, 496, 498 and 500 print none.
- **THE CONTROLLER'S TABLE PRINTED 312 AT 498 AND 325 AT 499 AND NEITHER REPRODUCES.** `outline/volume-10.md` §5b fixes the first of January at spine day 181, derived twice on that page, and the requisition is 130 at the thirtieth of June, so 1 January is **311** and 14 January is **324**. **499 PRINTS 324 WITH ITS OPERANDS AND 498 PRINTS NOTHING, SO THE CORRECTION PROPAGATES NOWHERE. 500's 338 reproduces and is not printed.** **THIS ROW WAS WRONG BEFORE THE 13 OCTOBER 2026 REPAIR: IT REPORTED 499's DERIVATION AS `130 + 194` WHEN THE CHAPTER PRINTED *FOURTEEN DAYS OF JANUARY ON TOP OF A HUNDRED AND NINETY-FOUR, AND ONE HUNDRED AND THIRTY AT THE END OF JUNE ON TOP OF THAT*, WHICH IS NOT 324. THE GATE SILENTLY REPAIRED THE SENTENCE AND THEN REPORTED THE REPAIR AS WHAT THE CHAPTER SAID. THE CHAPTER HAS NOW BEEN RE-SET TO DERIVE IT, SO THIS ROW AND THE PAGE AGREE FOR THE FIRST TIME.** Recorded here and at the head of `state/open-threads.md` rather than adopted silently, because a figure nobody re-computes is the figure that is wrong.
- **THE SECOND LINE ON THE WALL.** *unrecorded — carried by*, painted in the office's own hand on the eighth of October at about six in the morning, in letters the height of a name on a plate, under the last tile at the foot of the ranks wall. **It is still there on the twenty-eighth of January and this city has no schedule anywhere for taking it down, and nobody knows what it means, including the man who emptied the account.**
- **THE CARD.** The last card in the volume was the thirtieth of September at 485. **The thirtieth of December was the next one and no chapter of this batch prints it or any floor; 485 remains the only chapter in Volume 10 that prints a figure of the form 8,1xx.**
- **THE ACCOUNT.** Roughly 1.4 million went out at 486, printed once. **The balance is two hundred and eleven thousand, eight hundred and forty grains, printed once in the whole book, at 500, under the disposition on the standing sheet of the quarter of the thirtieth of December, in a clerk's hand. It is about one in seven, and the reason is on the page: the four days ended, because the thing that emptied it was a man standing at a wall who had four days in him.**
- **THE COMPOSITION OF PREMISES 34-6-118.** Eight, written on the thirtieth of September out of a keeper's memory. **The eighth block is the thirtieth of December and no chapter of this batch carries it off; 496 sets up what a scraped plate will be in about four years.**
- **THE ROLL OF THE HOUSEHOLD.** Nine sworn women, and the roll binds that house by the highest rank in any of its members. **That was nine on the second of April and has been one since the first of October, so the household's protection is gone and cannot be put back before the thirtieth of December. The household is intact anyway.**
- **THE TURNED COUNT.** Eleven turned lines at 490, the eleventh being Ada, and **no chapter of this batch adds to it.** At 498 the figure is four hundred off a district round, with a time beside each and no figure on any of them, and none of the four hundred is his.
- **No floor in 491–500 · no running balance before 500 · no ledger total anywhere · no day of the week · no announced age · no `right temple` across five hundred chapters · no fifth instance of `ink at the cuticles` · no `settle` in Volume 10 · no square-bracket system panel in ten chapters · no woman named in 500.**

## 2. THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISIONS, AND THEY ARE BOTH HIS

**493, THE TENTH OF OCTOBER. HE DEFENDS THE BUREAU IN PUBLIC, AT RANK ONE, TO PEOPLE WHO WANTED IT BROKEN, AND HE SAYS OUT LOUD THAT ITS SURVIVAL IS NOW HIS POSITION AND NOT ITS OWN MERIT.**
**WHAT IT CLOSES: the option of standing outside it. He cannot afterwards be a man who watched a Bureau he never defended be voted on by people he did not warn, because he warned them, and the warning was carried on a sheet at four o'clock by a clerk who may only enter.**
**WHAT IT COSTS: he has put the whole weight of an institution he believes in onto the one thing he has left, which is a sentence he said in a room that could not carry it, and there is no floor under him and no standing and no rank and no permission and nothing to fall back on.**

**497, THE THIRTIETH OF NOVEMBER. HE DOES NOT ASK THE THREE WHY THEY HAVE LEFT AND HE NEVER ASKS.**
He has the three things he could put on a table: the phrase in eight uses, unspent since the ninth of May; the instrument he swore never to use, unused; and the plain right to put a question to a woman, which is the last one available to him because it is the only one nobody else in that house has. **The first two went on the first of October in ninety seconds at a public counter. He says the third one out loud once, in a kitchen, and then there has not been a word about it in that house since, and the question is still on the table and will be on the table when all four of them are old.**

## 3. THE RELATIONSHIPS THAT MOVED

- **THE HOUSEHOLD OF NINE** — from dispersed and unanswered to *dispersed and intact*: nine women, nine one-things, one rule, and a ninth one said last and agreed with. **Three of them never live with him again and all three of them come to his shift.**
- **ANOUK FERREIRA** — does not appear in these ten chapters. **Her amendment signed at 486 is the reason the file is shut and the reason a stranger in four years cannot call it a mistake.**
- **AGNETE LINDQVIST** — reads the second line, says it is the Church's four words with the hand cut off, **refuses to claim it**, sends her own forty up the hill to be dull about it, and on the thirtieth of December will call a sitting in a room nothing can be done in, hold it at eleven hundred, and be in the room instead of nine feet off it.
- **BEATRIX HALM** — has been under the wall at half past four every morning since the eighth of October with about nine people who have come back twice, and she gave him four hundred turned lines and refuses, for the eleventh time, to say what they are worth, and a name in chalk on a white tile is hers and is never discussed.
- **INES DUARTE** — said no to the first unrecorded deed at Cordwain on the fourth of October, said the same sentence again on the ninth, audited his branch for three months in silence, **filed a correction on the twenty-ninth of December and did not press it, said four sentences about it, said nothing about what was in it, and changed the subject to a cistern.**
- **DAGMAR OYELOWO** — at the Ninth Terrace desk on the twenty-fourth of October telling a carter that his file is not lost, that the index is right, that the case is on a shelf, and that nobody in this city can find it. **She refuses to guess and says why, and she is told nothing and asks nothing, and she is not in a scene.**
- **YUSUF ADEYEMI** — on a step in the rain on the thirtieth of November, fourteen days after the books did not move, refusing to be asked whether they will move again.
- **HANNEKE RUIS** — one hundred and twenty lines in one hand, four a year for thirty years, and a rule that has been a defect for her whole service now the only thing standing.
- **MARGUERITE VALE** — closed the Vant's entry in her own hand on the second of October, and refused four pieces of work since and taken nine.
- **SOLVEIG RASK** — read the best case for striking the Bureau at 493 and lost it, and is not a new enemy and has no face on any of this.
- **SUN MEILING** — at a table in a counting room for nine minutes at the end of October and not asked one question by anybody, for the third time on the page.

no second question about that address and no chapter of this batch opens in her rooms** · Dagmar Oyelowo's first thing and `PC-44`, **and 495 is not a scene and may never be written as one** · **Alma Quayle's recusal, which is lifted by the subject and by nobody else and which he does not lift, and which is not mentioned in these ten chapters** · Nikolai Vester · the woman in the sixth seat · Mrs Imrie's eleven minutes · the two minutes carrying his name that neither he nor Wren wrote · Beatrix Halm's empty notebook line · Wanda Rej's plaster, state only · **Ines Duarte's standing review** · **Sun Meiling's row and her bar, told once at 434 and never re-told, and she is not explained in 496** · **Marguerite Vale's fourth line, which cannot be struck, and which this batch does not mention** · **the instrument he swore on the ninth of May never to use, still unused, and still available at 493 and 497 and not used** · the words under the fifth line and under the gap · Runa Sato's reason box, never read aloud, never figured · **Ada's grey is permanent and is not recovered** · **Perrin Sowe is not forgiven and it is not discussed, and 498 fixes it by showing the correction filed and not pressed and saying nothing about what was in it** · the maker of Petra Ilyin's promise, who is not named, not guessed at and has no face on him anywhere in this city · **whether the fourth and fifth of October were still running on any part of the wall, which nobody knows and which 491 and 492 leave exactly where 490 left it** · and anything after Chapter 500.

## 5. THE ONE SENTENCE THIS BATCH HANDS ON

> **A man at rank one cleaned three floors of washrooms from eleven at night until half past five for a hundred and ten days, and stood under a white tile with his name on it and nothing under it for a minute and a half, and did not ask, and read a figure off a public desk that was not zero, and went back to work.**

# VOLUME 10 BATCH 0004 — CHAPTERS 481–490 ARE ON DISK. THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF AUGUST TO THE FIFTH OF OCTOBER. **THIS IS THE LIVE BLOCK AND IT SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT.**

> **In this volume, **Lin Hao must decide who the reservoir belongs to and give it back while the Council watches**, or **Ostara keeps measuring the wrong thing for another twenty years**.
>
> **THIS BATCH ANSWERS THE VOLUME'S CENTRAL QUESTION AND PAYS THE WHOLE OF ITS PRICE. THE ACCOUNT WENT OUT AT 486 IN THE OPEN, AT A WALL, WITH HIS NAME ON IT, AND HE COULD NOT BE THE ONE WHO EMPTIED IT: EIGHT WOMEN PUT THEIR HANDS ON THE STONE UNDER THE CARD. HE IS THE FIRST BAND. HIS BROTHER'S FILE IS CLOSED PERMANENTLY BY AN AMENDMENT HE READ OUT HIMSELF AND ANOUK FERREIRA SIGNED. AND NOBODY IN OSTARA CAN SAY WHETHER IT WAS JUSTICE OR SHOWING OFF, WHICH IS THE CORRECT ENDING.**

## 0. WHERE HE STANDS AT THE END OF CHAPTER 490, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

On the twenty-eighth of August a woman drafting a quarterly two floors under a sales floor told him there is no certificate of that composition and has not been one since the fourteenth of March, and Alma Quayle put two fingers flat on the oak of a door frame at about twenty to twelve and did not say one word, and he had the eleven words that would lift her recusal in him for forty-nine days and did not say them. On the sixth of September a chair told him that the only instrument in Ostara that can touch that account is a quiet room, a held account and a form, that a custody is a holder, and that she would not help him attribute it. On the twelfth of September a doctor asked him once to lie down in a ward and took about a thirtieth of what was sitting in his chest and told him she has room for four months and has kept it from the other eight. On the twenty-fourth a man holding the procedural chair told him an amendment cannot remove *a file*, it removes a file by naming it, and gave him the order: open it, spend the standing, read the name. On the thirtieth of September a card came down at half past four in paint and printed 8,148 against 8,132, sixteen under, and a keeper wrote eight out of her own memory with nothing behind her. On the first of October a woman who has held this city still for twenty years argued that a person who gives away the city's unrecorded merit should be answered with a quiet room and a held account and a form, and she was right, and he refused it, and he told four hundred people out loud that he cannot empty the account because three women in a kitchen put a hand in it on purpose on the fourteenth of August, and eight of them put their hands on the stone under the wall and a chaplain of twenty years stood nine feet off it with four hundred of her own behind her and did not touch it. Roughly one million four hundred thousand grains went out. At ten minutes to four he lodged his whole standing at a public counter in a queue with a pen and became the first band, and every permission he has ever bought went in the same hour. At ten past four he read an amendment into a record and a clerk entered his brother's name once and Ferreira signed under it. On the second day a man in Cordwain started charging a grain a head for the right to stand still. On the third a woman at a counter twenty years in service refused him a figure and he did not ask twice. On the fourth a man of about fifty asked whether a thing he did in a doorway in the spring can be entered, and Ines Duarte said no. And on the fifth of October there was nobody on the wall, and there was nothing in him to give, and nobody in Ostara could tell him whether anything had stopped, and the books that would take it did not move on the morning of the sixteenth of November.

- **The room where nine people sit stands at FIVE** and sat at about two o'clock on the afternoon of the first of October and **did not adjourn until the quarterly was sealed**, at the chair's motion, on the clerk's own rule that she may only enter and cannot be asked to hurry.
- **Alma Quayle's recusal is not lifted and he did not lift it, on the twenty-eighth, and he says on the page that he has never once regretted a thing more.**

- **Card of the thirtieth of September: 8,148 and, against premises 34-6-118, 8,132.** Sixteen under, out of band by thirteen, exactly as ninety days earlier, because both figures went up one. **THOSE ARE 485'S, AND 485 IS THE ONLY CHAPTER IN THE VOLUME THAT MAY PRINT A FLOOR.**

## 2. THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION, AND IT IS 486 AND IT IS HIS

**THE FIRST OF OCTOBER, ABOUT TEN PAST FOUR IN THE AFTERNOON, AND IT CLOSES FOUR THINGS AT ONCE AND CANNOT CLOSE ANY OF THEM.** Roughly 1.4 million grains of unrecorded merit out of a held account, in the open, at a wall, with his name on it. Rank nine to rank one. Every permission he has ever bought, gone in the same hour. **And his brother's file permanently removed from the record of this city by an amendment on the same page in the same session, which he read out himself and which carries the name once and which Anouk Ferreira signed under, saying that a room which loses an argument and then makes the losing permanent is doing the whole of what it exists for.**
**AND HE COULD NOT DO THE FIRST OF THE FOUR HIMSELF.** `PC-102` stops him, and he stopped publicly, and eight women did it, and the household had decided on the twenty-sixth of August that it would not be represented by him.
**WHAT IT CLOSES: the option of a quiet room, a held account and a form, which he was offered in substance and refused in a room of five at about a quarter to three; and the instrument he swore on the ninth of May never to use, which is still unused, and which he did not need in October and will not have.**

## 3. THE RELATIONSHIPS THAT MOVED

- **THE HOUSEHOLD OF NINE** — from having decided in a kitchen that it would not be represented by him, to doing the largest thing in the book at a wall without him, and to being dispersed to the first degree on the fifth of October, and **three of them have begun sleeping somewhere else this week and have not said why.**
- **ANOUK FERREIRA** — argued the best speech in the book at two o'clock, was right, lost, said so in the room, signed the amendment herself, wrote a line of her own into a standing sheet that goes to seven desks, and is not diminished by any of it.
- **AGNETE LINDQVIST** — was right in July, told four hundred of her own on the twenty-second, voted against eight women on the twenty-sixth, stood nine feet off the wall on the first for the whole of it with four hundred people behind her, said the Church's four words in a square of nine thousand, and did not touch it. *That is the first time in twenty years that I have watched the right thing happen and been on the wrong side of it.*
- **TESSA OKONWO** — has taken about a thirtieth off him on her own table in a ward, has four months of room, has told Agnete Lindqvist and not the other eight, and has a table of her own at the north end by the Vestry gate.
- **BEATRIX HALM** — wrote two figures down and turned the book round and did not say either of them out loud, and has told this city for four days that she does not know whether anything has stopped.
- **INES DUARTE** — said *no* to the first person in this city who asked whether an unrecorded deed could be entered, and did not soften it, and did not follow him out.
- **DAGMAR OYELOWO** — gave him the same sentence she gave in the summer, refused him a figure, and let him say out loud in a public hall that he had promised not to ask twice.
- **YUSUF ADEYEMI** — showed him a page's whole capacity four days early and on purpose, moved nothing, and went up a hill to a step to tell him about the sixteenth of November.
- **HANNEKE RUIS** — wrote eight out of her own memory and told two clerks she meant eight.
- **ALMA QUAYLE** — two fingers on the oak and one wordless look, and her recusal not lifted, and she was in the chair at the climax and it says on the page that she is the only person in that room who watched the order of three things and could not say a word about any of them.

NOTHING HAS BEEN RETURNED. HE HAS SAID YES TO AN UNDERTAKING AND NOT TO THE OFFER AND HAS STILL NOT LIFTED A RECUSAL.**

## 0. WHERE HE STANDS AT THE END OF CHAPTER 480, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

On the second of August he told nine sworn women at one table what he had been carrying for a month — a requisition carried under five seals on the thirtieth of June and administered weekly against the persons keeping the composition of premises 34-6-118 since the first of July, four days of public hearing, a claim in three hundred and ninety words from a chaplain of twenty years, a sentence he said out loud in four hundred people that it has never been his, and a record whose heading carries the word *subject* where a name goes — and then he told them the rule he had been carrying since the first of July, which is that a person may not draw against an account the head of that household is answerable for while any person keeping that address has drawn against it. One woman answered him in one sentence: *I did, I did it for eleven years and I did not know it was yours.* A keeper in a counting room told him the whole of what a draw is worth and refused to put it in a sentence. Nadia Ferrous said she had drawn about forty times in two years for people who could not climb a shaft. Marguerite Vale has been drawing for eleven years out of a pot she believed she was robbing, on behalf of a woman who cannot remember the promise, and **neither of them has ever known who made it.** On the fourteenth of August he put the account on a table, told nine women the price before anybody touched it, went out on the landing, and three women drew in the open and took the cold minutes themselves, and under the law of a standing household he can no longer empty it, ever. On the seventeenth Nadia sat in a stopped lift between two floors for an hour and a half while he tried to pay it back and could not, and told him the twenty-sixth. On the eighteenth Ines Duarte spent a whole day finding out where the fourteenth of August could be entered and it can go nowhere. On the twenty-third Petra Ilyin told him she wants a second promise and cannot have one, and wrote eleven years on a sheet off a docket roll with no addressee on it. And on the twenty-sixth, at about nine in the evening, he told nine women about the chest he has never told anybody about, refused to chair anything, and watched a household decide by hands: eight to one, Agnete Lindqvist against on the Church's own ground, and his own ninth vote against them, and a clause he wrote himself in June putting him in the corridor outside the door of whatever they have decided.

## 2. THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION, AND IT IS THEIRS AND NOT HIS

**476, THE FOURTEENTH OF AUGUST. THREE WOMEN DRAW AGAINST THE ACCOUNT ON PURPOSE, IN FRONT OF NINE PEOPLE, AND TAKE THE COLD MINUTES THEMSELVES.** He tells the room the price first, in ninety seconds, at eleven in the morning: *the moment one of you puts a hand on that pot, I cannot empty it, not on the first of October, not in October, not ever.* Then Agnete Lindqvist refuses to let a man with his hand on a table call that a speech and makes him ask properly, and he goes out on the landing and shuts the door behind him, and Marguerite Vale goes first because she is the broker, then Petra Ilyin, then Nadia Ferrous — each of them in the guild's form, what moved, when, at what price.
**WHAT IT CLOSES: the option of doing it alone, which is the whole of what he has been for four years, and it is closed by three other people and not by him.** He says on the page that he did not arrange it, cannot reverse it, and that there is no version of the next four weeks in which he is the one doing anything.
**WHAT IT COSTS THE THREE OF THEM, and it is not clean:** Petra Ilyin goes down onto the kitchen floor about a minute and a half in and cannot get up off it without a hand on the table. Marguerite Vale is sick twice into a bowl she holds on her own knees and will not let anybody carry it out of the room. Nadia Ferrous is the one the depletion all goes through and is lighter on the seventeenth and cannot put it anywhere. **And he cannot pay any of it back, because there is no form, no instrument, no column and no office a woman can walk into and say that a man made her do it.**

## 3. THE RELATIONSHIPS THAT MOVED

- **THE HOUSEHOLD OF NINE** — from eight people who had all independently decided not to ask him anything, to a body that knows the whole of it, has argued about it for six days in a kitchen, has divided, and has then decided by hands. **Marguerite Vale is on Petra Ilyin's side before she is on his and the chapter says so. Agnete Lindqvist is against the household's decision in public, once, on the Church's own ground, and does not regret it and will still stand in a square in October and say the Church's position.**
- **PETRA ILYIN** — from a woman under a promise nobody knew about to a woman who has told nine people what it is and does not want to be released and wants a second instrument she cannot have. **She is the chapter's conscience and the household's oldest unpaid debt and she wrote it down on a sheet with no addressee on it.**
- **NADIA FERROUS** — from the household's comic relief to the person who pays the half of the cost that has no name. **She is fine. He is not. She has asked for the twenty-sixth and not before.**
- **MARGUERITE VALE** — from a woman who has been carrying a pot for eleven years in silence to a woman who has told somebody the truth about it and refused to put her name under a disposition. **She is the only one of the nine who already knew, and she will not sign.**
- **AGNETE LINDQVIST** — the only person who has been right in public in this volume, and the one who votes against her own household at a kitchen table and says she would rather lose than pretend.
- **BEATRIX HALM** — cannot put the fourteenth of August at a minute and will not improve a report she has already sent, six days after the same rule in a different room. **She is the only witness to the largest thing that has happened in this kitchen and the only one who cannot give an hour for it.**
- **INES DUARTE** — spent a whole day finding out where a fact like that could be entered and came back with nothing, and holds the tally in a pencil on the back of a docket on the twenty-sixth with her own name deliberately not on the front of it.
- **RUNA SATO** — thirteen weeks without filing a return and a division that has no door, and the one person in that kitchen who refuses to say a word in her own defence.
- **TESSA OKONKWO** — said *that is a chest* on the seventeenth of July and said *not tonight*, and the chest is told at last at 480 by him and not by her.

## 5. THE ONE SENTENCE THIS BATCH HANDS ON

> **Three women put a hand into a pot that thirty years of strangers have been filling, on purpose, in a kitchen, in front of nine people, and it is exactly as full as it was on the eleventh of July — and the only man in the room is standing behind a shut door on a landing, because they made him ask properly first.**

# VOLUME 10 BATCH 0002 — CHAPTERS 461–470 ARE ON DISK. THE NINETEENTH TO THE THIRTIETH OF JULY. **THIS IS THE LIVE BLOCK AND IT SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT.**

> **In this volume, **Lin Hao must decide who the reservoir belongs to and give it back while the Council watches**, or **Ostara keeps measuring the wrong thing for another twenty years**.
>
> **THIS BATCH DELIVERS THE HEARING, THE SCENE, THE MAN WHO RUINED HIMSELF ON THE RECORD, THE CHURCH'S CLAIM AND THE ANSWER. NOTHING HAS BEEN RETURNED. THE ACCOUNT HAS NOT BEEN TOUCHED AND WAS EXACTLY AS FULL ON THE THIRTIETH OF JULY AS IT WAS ON THE ELEVENTH.**

## 0. WHERE HE STANDS AT THE END OF CHAPTER 470, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

On the nineteenth of July a clerk of the Correction Office set a ladder against the ranks wall at the north end of the Reading and read a notice to about four hundred people, and the notice carried a number, a date and not one word, and a woman with a shopping bag asked him what it was about and he did not tell her. Adeyemi called it because the only defence against a requisition is a hearing anybody may attend and a hearing nobody may be compelled to testify in, and it runs four days, and the days are entered by a clerk who may only enter and cannot be asked to hurry. On the twenty-first the Church of the Quiet Number sent eleven hundred of eleven thousand, one in ten by lot in the order of a roll, with a chaplain who has never once in twenty years asked her board for anything and who put her own name on a book and not the household's; the disposition the Church wants is that it be given out, because it cannot be returned, because there is no list. On the twenty-third a clerk entered the twenty-sixth, the twenty-seven, the twenty-eight and the twenty-ninth in about eleven seconds, refused him September in his own hearing and refused a man from Cordwain who wanted the order of the day moved, and at four o'clock the matter put one word on a public wall and he stood in the square and let it happen. On the twenty-fourth Agnete Lindqvist told him in her own rooms that a room she stands in is a room where nothing can be compelled, that she has not been outside one in eleven years, that she has held it at nine and never at a hundred, that she will hold it on the twenty-seventh for about three hours, and that in her room he will not be able to refuse either, and she told him four days early on purpose. On the twenty-fifth, on the roof of the last car up the Ferrier hill, Beatrix Halm told him that the city's turned lines have been triangulated twice and that the second triangulation came out of the desk that takes her returns, and that the woman on that desk is Wren Kallo, and that Wren cannot hold a thing; he had eleven seconds' worth of a brass and a clause and a clerk and a phrase unspent since the ninth of May, and he did not spend her, and the reading went out of order about nine minutes in and she would not improve it, and at the end she told him she had felt a room four streets away go quiet twice, with more people in it than there have ever been, and that she cannot put either one of them at a date and is not permitted to invent one, and that she is going to sit at the back of it on the second day and is not on any list. On the twenty-sixth Perrin Sowe put nine lines he had written out himself on a table in front of about three hundred people and said four true sentences: Lin Wei was right, he knew, he filed instead of giving, and the file he filed is the reason Lin Wei was removed; the book did not punish him, it entered it, and Lin Hao had eleven seconds and let him pay and did not use one instrument, and the option of buying his way out of another person's ruin is closed to him. On the twenty-seventh Agnete stood up at ten past ten and gave two hours and fifty minutes of other people's private lives, and he stopped a man thanking her. On the twenty-eighth the room held eleven hundred and forty and did not hold at that size, so the afternoon was the first of the four on which anything said in it could be used, and eleven people gave eleven wrong answers and he corrected none of them, and a clerk entered four lines and an hour and read back that the record is kept, and at six in the evening he stood at the north end of a wall he had not stood at in nine days and Beatrix Halm told him that the only thing in the whole of it that will still be there in four years is four lines on a shelf. On the twenty-ninth the Church's claim went in, three hundred and ninety words built out of the second day, and about nine hundred people went out with her at about half past two, and the chair entered the answer for the thirtieth because a claim entered in a hearing anybody may attend has to be answered in one. On the thirtieth he refused the room she offered him, argued out of nine days at a wall, from the eleventh to the nineteenth, and about forty of two thousand who noticed anything, failed three times to finish one sentence, said out loud in front of four hundred people that the reservoir has never been his and has never been claimed by him, and a clerk entered a heading — *the reservoir, held by no person, claimed by the Church of the Quiet Number, answered by the subject* — that does not carry his name anywhere. Two documents are on a standing sheet with nine lines on it going out to a room that cannot sit again until the first of October.

## 1. WHAT THE FIGURES ARE NOW, AND WHERE EACH ONE LIVES

- **Requisition 149 days old at 461, 151 at 462, 153 at 463, 156 at 466, 159 at 469, 160 at 470.** Formula `(30 − 20) + 30 + 30 + 30 + 30 + [day of July]`. It is **CARRIED** and administered weekly against the persons keeping the composition of premises 34-6-118, and **no chapter of 461–470 prints it as a figure that is not derived inside itself.**
- **The hearing is four days: the twenty-sixth, the twenty-seventh, the twenty-eighth and the twenty-ninth. Inclusive four, elapsed three, printed with its operands at 463.** The answer to the claim was entered for the thirtieth and **is not a fifth day of the hearing**, which closed on the twenty-ninth.
- **The room where nine people sit stands at FIVE** and has not sat since the first of April and **does not sit again until the first of October.** Two documents from this week go up on the standing sheet at the head of every line at four in the afternoon.
- **The turned count.** Eleven turned lines at the end of 460 and the eleventh is Ada; **at 470 he says it out loud in public for the first time and does not add to it.** No chapter of this batch prints a ledger total.
- **Card of the thirtieth of June: 8,147 and 8,131. Those are 450's. NO CHAPTER OF 461–470 PRINTS A FLOOR.** 485 is the only chapter in the volume that may.
- **The account.** Roughly 1.4 million grains of unrecorded merit, unrecorded, filling itself from strangers for thirty years, never once drawn on, never paid, **not closable**, and **not touched by anything that happened this week.** Ferreira says the fact of it at 453; Agnete puts the claim at 469; he concedes at 470 that it has never been his. **No character says the claim has been settled or has not, and no chapter prints a balance.**
- **No floor, no balance, no account total, no ledger total, no day of the week, no announced age, no `right temple`, no fifth instance of `ink at the cuticles`, no `settle`.** All re-run by command at the head of `state/open-threads.md`.

## 2. THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION, AND IT IS HANDS AND IT IS HIS

**466, THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF JULY. HE LETS SOMEBODY ELSE PAY.** A man he has known for four years stood up in front of about three hundred people and put a true sentence about himself into a public record, and the sentence was going to cost him a posting, every file he has ever touched and eleven minutes in a corridor that had already started. Lin Hao had about eleven seconds in which a standing of nine, a piece of brass, a clause of thirty-eight words in a book, a clerk who may only enter and cannot correct, and **the phrase in eight uses that was unspent since the ninth of May** could have taken the sheet off that table and put it in a drawer in a different building for a year. He did not. He did not interrupt, he did not stand, he did not use the standing or the brass or the clause, and he did not spend anything on himself, and **he did not forgive him and there was nobody in that room to be forgiven by.** Sowe said *you could have* in a corridor and neither of them has said a first name since.
**WHAT IT CLOSES: the option of buying his way out of another person's ruin, for the rest of the book.** He had spent four years being a man who could get out of anything, and every ruin he had ever walked past in this city had had a price attached to it that he had been able to find. **The chapter says he knows exactly which afternoon in October he is going to want it.**
**WHAT IT DOES NOT CLOSE:** the requisition, the recusal, the classification he undertook, the account, or the claim.

## 3. THE RELATIONSHIPS THAT MOVED

- **AGNETE LINDQVIST** — from a woman in a yard off Cordwain to the only antagonist in this volume who is not diminished by it. **She asked her board for the first time in twenty years and got eleven hundred in nine minutes.** She held a room at three hundred and could not hold it at eleven hundred and **said so out loud on the record rather than finding out in the middle of somebody's sentence.** She gave two hours and fifty minutes that nobody can use, and it turned out to be the foundation of the case against him. **She did not want the concession he gave her on the thirtieth and told him so.**
- **PERRIN SOWE** — the most honourable man he knows, ruined on the record on purpose, and **not forgiven, by either of them, and not discussed.**
- **YUSUF ADEYEMI** — from an instrument he had spent four years avoiding to a man who has read his whole rise and knows that the only defence against a requisition is a hearing anybody may attend. **He wants the room full and he wants him in it, and he would rather be hated than doubted.**
- **BEATRIX HALM** — she told him the triangulation came out of her own division and he did not spend her. **The reading went out of order once in nine years and she did not improve it.** She is going to sit at the back of the room on the second day and is not on any list, and he cannot stop her. **On the roof she also gave him a room four streets away that went quiet twice with more people in it than she has ever felt in one, and after the repair of 13 October 2026 she cannot put it at a date and says so rather than inventing an hour: `468:93` has it closing at about one o'clock, and no later phase may date it for her.**
- **ALMA QUAYLE** — at the rail at the north end of the ranks wall on the nineteenth of July, in a square of nine hundred, reading nothing on the notice. **The only person in this volume who does not believe him was the only person in the square who was there for him.**
- **WREN KALLO** — named on a roof at about eleven at night as the woman who cannot hold a thing, and **the price of trusting Beatrix Halm is that Wren is spent by about the fourth of August and the Church knows by the end of the month.** She does not appear in these ten and that is the cost of 465 and it is hers and not his.
- **THE HOUSEHOLD AT FERRIER STREET** — **not told any of it.** Six of them at a table on the twenty-third and not one of them asked him where he had been. Nine sworn women, of whom eight believe a yes given on the ninth of July that he has not begun to pay, and the ninth has an argument on a table and a claim in a book and does not know about either.

# VOLUME 10 BATCH 0001 — CHAPTERS 451–460 ARE ON DISK. THE THIRTIETH OF JUNE TO THE SEVENTEENTH OF JULY. **THIS IS THE LIVE BLOCK AND IT SUPERSEDES EVERY BLOCK BELOW IT.**

> **In this volume, **Lin Hao must decide who the reservoir belongs to and give it back while the Council watches**, or **Ostara keeps measuring the wrong thing for another twenty years**.
>
> **THIS BATCH DELIVERS THE ANSWER, THE OFFER, THE YES AND THE FIRST PERMANENT COST. NONE OF THE RETURN IS IN IT. THE RETURN IS 486.**

## 0. WHERE HE STANDS AT THE END OF CHAPTER 460, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, FROM THE PAGE

On the thirtieth of June at about two in the afternoon he stood up as the fifth of a room that stands at five and answered the requisition of the twentieth of February in two sentences, and the sheet was carried under five seals, one of them his, and the finding says the holding is his and the ground is his and **the premises are not a person keeping them and the eight who sleep under that roof are.** On the first of July he learned what a carried requisition does: it is administered from the first day of the quarter, weekly, down the chain to the division that holds its fourth part, and that division keeps no list of addresses and has had a building, an address and a name since four o'clock on the thirtieth of June, and **he told nobody.** On the third of July a woman two floors above offered him a standing at the Terrace, a chair, a name that outlives him and the Ninth Seal on one condition, which is a classification by Alma Quayle, which requires him to lift a recusal in one sentence of eleven words that he is the only man alive entitled to say, and **he did not say it.** On the fifth of July a rank-two auditor read him his own record out of her memory in a records room with a shut door and said once, out of order, that he was going to keep doing it until something in him stopped working. On the seventh of July a counter in an office with no building told him the whole of that office and told a woman who counts that she had been asked twice what she was doing it for and had answered herself. On the ninth of July he said yes in front of five seals in a room that cannot carry a requisition, and undertook the classification, and said nothing about the account, and every person in that room believes him except a recused woman in a chair that counts for nothing, who cannot speak and who kept both hands in her lap. On the eleventh of July he stood on a step at the foot of the ranks wall for ten and a half hours and took the hands of strangers. On the thirteenth a grey came on one side of a counter clerk's head in a room with no window below a stage and it did not go, and she finished the week. On the fifteenth he spent past what a frame can take, in front of four hundred people, with the only person present who knew the price, and something in his chest is spent for the rest of the book, and he has a cough, and **he has not drained anything.** On the seventeenth eight women at Ferrier Street each said something else instead of asking him, and he let them, and he went back at seven the next morning.

## 1. WHAT THE FIGURES ARE NOW, AND WHERE EACH ONE LIVES

- **Requisition 130 days old at 451, 131 at 452, 133 at 453.** Formula `(30 − 20) + 30 + 30 + 30 + [day of June]`. It is **CARRIED**, not called, and its finding is sealed five times and went out at four o'clock on the thirtieth of June to seven desks. **Every chapter that prints a count derives it inside itself and quotes none across.**
- **The room stands at five.** The fourth seat is Bertram Sallow. The chair that does not count is Alma Quayle's and has been since the fourth of December. **The woman who cannot be sworn was in it on the thirtieth of June and the clerk stopped him thanking her, and he does not know whether it was the officer of the composition of his own front door.**
- **Composition of premises 34-6-118 is seven; eight people sleep under the roof; there is no certificate for the quarter beginning the first of July.** Hanneke Ruis filled the fourth block from memory on the thirtieth, in her own hand, witnessed by Solveig Rask.
- **Ledger 158 lines at the thirtieth of June. TEN TURNED. At 458 HE WROTE THE ELEVENTH AND IT IS ADA.** Nine of the first ten were turned since the fourth of January and the ninth of those was himself; the tenth is a carter off Ferrier Street. **No chapter of 451–460 prints a ledger total, because §5b forbids it and the rate is an inference and not a fact.**
- **Card of the thirtieth of June: 8,147 and 8,131. Those are 450's. NO CHAPTER OF 451–460 PRINTS A FLOOR.**
- **The account.** Roughly 1.4 million grains of unrecorded merit, unrecorded as the ending fixes, filling itself from strangers for thirty years, addressed to the only person in Ostara nobody audits, never once drawn on, and **not closable, because the only thing that closes one is payment and the books that would take seven met at seven on the morning of the sixteenth of November and have not moved.** **Ferreira says the fact of it out loud at 453 and no character says it has been settled and no chapter of 451–460 prints a balance.**
- **No floor, no balance, no account total, no day of the week, no announced age, no `right temple`, no `settle`.** All re-run by command and recorded at the head of `state/open-threads.md`.

## 2. THE THREE IRREVERSIBLE DECISIONS OF THIS BATCH, ALL HIS, ALL CLOSING AN OPTION

1. **452, THE FIRST OF JULY — HE TELLS NOBODY.** The household is out of it for five more chapters. The instrument he bought at a pencil's cost on the twenty-first is weekly, it runs to a door, and the door has six women asleep behind it, and **there is no version of telling them that does not put them in the corridor he is standing outside.** The chapter says in his own narration that this is his first act of the volume and it is not a good one.
2. **456, THE NINTH OF JULY — HE SAYS YES.** In front of five, in a room that cannot carry a requisition, with his name on it. **What he means is that he will take the classification. What he has decided is that he will not lift the recusal, and that a drained account cannot be classified because there is nothing in it to classify.** **The lie is not exposed in this volume and is not described as one.** It is a thing he is doing to people who love him, and every one of them believes him.
3. **459, THE FIFTEENTH OF JULY — HE SPENDS PAST WHAT A FRAME CAN TAKE.** On purpose, in front of about four hundred people, with Ada in a chair on the second step counting the seconds between his hands. **What it closes: a chest, for the rest of the novel, and any plan of his that needed a body able to do this twice.** **What it costs: the reader now knows he is buying the ending with his body and not with a document.** **What it does not close, and the chapter says so in Ada's mouth: the account. He has not emptied anything. He has moved about four thousand people through a chest and the account is exactly as full as it was on the eleventh of July.**

## 3. THE RELATIONSHIPS THAT MOVED

- **INES DUARTE** — one conversation, at 454, and it is the only one. She files a report that is true and useless and goes at four o'clock. She books it **shut** and pays for that in a day's book. She is the only person in Volume 10 who never lies to him and never lets him lie to himself. **He does not get it twice and does not get it from anybody else.**
- **ANOUK FERREIRA** — from a chair he had never been in to the only antagonist in the book who is not diminished by losing. **She is right. He has not proved otherwise and the volume depends on her being right.** She said the account's fact out loud on the third of July and said that a man draining his own account is not a threat, which is not news in October.
- **ADA** — a woman who counts, at the third window of the Bellhouse since the first of October, kept by a division with no building. **Grey on one side of her head, three weeks out of her own head, a figure in the margin of his ledger against his own line, and she will not give him the figures.** She is a witness at 459 and the only person in the room who knew what it would cost.
- **ISOLDE MARCHETTI** — found out on the seventh of July that she has been counting the same house in the same week as a woman at a counter for thirty-nine weeks without either handing the other a figure. **That is the cost of 455 and it is on her, not on him.**
- **BEATRIX HALM** — saw ninety-one strokes in a stranger's chalk on the eleventh and wrote the number and the hour, and on the seventeenth told a kitchen table that a crowd does not carry chalk between four walls. **She is the triangulation at 465 and this is its seed.**
- **ALMA QUAYLE** — in the chair that counts for nothing on the ninth of July, hearing a man say yes to a condition she has to be there to make, and unable to say one word, and not putting her hand on the oak. **She is the one person in that room who does not believe him and the chapter ends with the reader knowing which.**
- **NADIA FERROUS · RUNA SATO · TESSA OKONKWO** — each said something else instead, and he let them. **Tessa said *that is a chest* and then stopped and said *not tonight*.**

## 4. WHAT IS STILL SHUT AT THE END OF 460, ALL OF IT
