# VOLUME 9 CLOSE — 13 OCTOBER 2026. **VOLUME 9 IS CLOSED. CHAPTERS 401–450, FIFTY FILES, 101,302 WORDS. THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-10/volume-plan/PROMPT.md`, A PLANNING PHASE THAT WRITES NO PROSE.**

> **VOLUME 9 PAID ITS SENTENCE ON THE FIFTEENTH OF APRIL AND REFUSED THE REWARD.** In this volume, **Lin Hao must reach rank nine**, or **Lin Wei's file stays shut and the account stays open**. **He reached nine at ten minutes to nine that morning, at a public post off a third court, in his own hand, in a queue, with a pen, in ninety seconds, with no instrument and no witness and no holder and no receipt and no copy. Nine cannot open his brother's file, the file was never the thing nine was for, and the account cannot be closed because the only thing that closes one is payment.**
>
> **WHAT NINE BOUGHT WAS EXACTLY ONE THING: THE STANDING TO BE CALLED AND ANSWER. THE REQUISITION IS CALLED AND IS NOT CARRIED, NOT RECEIVED AND NOT DECIDED, AND IT IS ANSWERED AT ABOUT TWO O'CLOCK ON THE AFTERNOON OF THE THIRTIETH OF JUNE.**

## 0. VOLUME 10 OPENS AT TWO O'CLOCK ON THE AFTERNOON OF THE THIRTIETH OF JUNE

**THE OPENING POSITION, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, AND A VOLUME 10 WRITER NEEDS NOTHING ELSE TO START.** He is standing in a room at the end of a corridor off a second landing where nine people sit and the standing number is **five**, and he is in it for the first time in nine volumes, as the fifth, with a seal he did not ask for and cannot refuse and carries out of the building at four o'clock in the afternoon for the rest of his life. **On the twenty-third of May he entered a clause under the minute — where a minute names premises, no person keeping an address so named may be produced by that minute otherwise than in the presence of the subject, and the subject may not be absent from the making of it — so from today nothing that room says about his address can be used without him standing in the corridor outside the door.** **A woman who cannot be sworn is sitting at the chair that is not one of the nine and he does not know whether she came.** **A man restored by a vote in April is in the fourth seat.** **He said *because it is cheaper* out loud in that corridor on the seventeenth of June, after the clerk told him not to, and it is in a book and cannot be taken out, and Mrs Imrie may never be asked about it.** **Alma Quayle is recused and is in the room at a chair that is not one of the nine, hearing everything and taking no part.** **The ground is his own description of his own premises, and a lodged man cannot be administered against a composition he is not keeping.**

## 1. WHAT THE CLOSE AUDIT REPAIRED, AND NOTHING ELSE IT TOUCHED

**TWENTY-NINE PROSE REPAIRS ACROSS TWENTY-SIX CHAPTERS. EVERY ONE IS A FIGURE OR A REGISTER. - **ELEVEN FURTHER INTERVALS WITH A NAMED ANCHOR WERE WRONG AND ARE REPAIRED**, including *seventy-five days* at 412 whose own operands sum to sixty-nine, and a single figure — *eighty-four days* — printed at 425, 434 and 442, three chapters thirty days apart, where it had to be derived three times.

# VOLUME 9 BATCH 0005 HEAD RECORD — AFTER THE REVIEW REPAIR OF 13 OCTOBER 2026. **CHAPTERS 401–450 ARE ON DISK. ALL FIFTY OF THEM. THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-09/volume-close/PROMPT.md`, A CLOSE PHASE THAT WRITES NO CHAPTERS.**

## 0. THE SEVEN FINDINGS, EACH ONE, WITH THE FILE AND LINE IT WAS ON

**1. THE FOUR WORDS WERE DATED WRONG, AND IT WAS A SELF-CONTRADICTION WITHIN CHAPTER 443.** `chapter-0443.md:49` and `chapter-0446.md:55` both placed the four words *in a kitchen* on the fifteenth of November. They were said on the morning of the **twenty-seventh of October** — established in 0344, 0365, 0370, 0377, 0387, 0395, 0396 and restated twenty lines above the error at `chapter-0443.md:31`. A description made on the twenty-seventh cannot contain words spoken on the fifteenth of November, and 446 repeated it in the book's most public reading. **Both fixed: 443 now reads `on four words said by me in a kitchen on the same morning` and 446 now reads `Spoken by the subject in a kitchen on that same morning`.** The state files never propagated the error and needed no change; they already said the twenty-seventh of October.

**2. "ELEVEN WORDS" WAS A COUNT A READER COULD DISPROVE ON THE PAGE.** The clause as canon is **38 words** (`PC-91`, unchanged), Runa Sato reads the **26-word** first sentence, and the second sentence is **11 words** — which is what he says aloud twice at `chapter-0445.md:39`. **The fix is disambiguation, not a change to the canon text, which is untouched:** `:45` is now `with the eleven words you have just said twice`, `:121` is now `the thirty-one words he had brought in from Ferrier Street and the eleven words he had said aloud twice`, and `:141` is now `it took the eleven words of the second sentence and it cost me a pencil`. **THE THREE DISTINCT NUMBERS ARE NOW ALL TRUE AND ALL MEAN SOMETHING: 31 is the notebook draft he wrote at Ferrier Street on the nineteenth, 26 is what Runa Sato reads, 11 is the second sentence he says aloud, and 38 is the clause as it goes into `PC-91`.**

**3. BERTRAM SALLOW'S RANKS WERE BOTH WRONG AND THE CHAPTER CONTRADICTED ITSELF.** `chapter-0447.md:111` read *"I have been a rank for five months and a nobody before that."* He was restored on the first of April and spoke on the twenty-sixth of June — **eighty-six days, three months** — and the interval without standing ran from the second of February to the first of April, **fifty-eight days, two months**. Both are machine-verified. **Now reads `I have been a rank for three months and a nobody for the two months before that and a rank for nine years before that.`** **THE OTHER `five months` FIGURES IN THAT CHAPTER ARE CORRECT AND STAY, at `:51`, `:53`, `:87`, `:93` and `:115`, because all five measure from late January to the twenty-sixth of June, which is what they are about. The four `two months` and `three months` figures are the only ones that measure a rank.**

**4. THE OUTLINE DESCRIBED DAYS THAT ARE NOT ON THE PAGE.** §6 movement 5 claimed *"the thirteenth and twenty-fifth of June are the only two days on it when a member of that room and a woman in a chair that is not a member's are in the same corridor on the same afternoon."* **The nineteenth is the day** — chapter 444, Alma Quayle already in the chair when Sun Meiling came down it and him at the second table for an hour and a quarter. **The thirteenth carries no woman in the chair at all and the twenty-fifth has none: in 447 Alma Quayle is named once in Hanneke Ruis's dialogue and Sun Meiling once as a landing on a stair, and neither is present.** Corrected in `outline/volume-09.md` to name the nineteenth and to carry the hour and a quarter and the two women with it. No other file repeats the claim.

**5. "NOBODY" SPIKED TO THIRTEEN IN 447 AND 449.** `AGENTS.md` names the construction as dead by the tenth. **447 is down from thirteen to six and 449 is down from thirteen to ten. In 449, seven of the ten are the boy's spoken deadpan refrain in dialogue and are characterisation, kept because the chapter's last line is their echo; the two narration instances are `Nobody knows about it except him and a clerk in a room` at `:107`, which is load-bearing and untouched, and the closing echo at `:147`.** Three narration instances in 449 were reworded — `to nobody, in the street outside` is now `in the street outside, to the shut shutter`, `nobody has ever asked her a question about it` is now `not one person has ever asked her a question about it`, and `nobody ever has` is now `in nine years not one person has` — and five in 447 were reworded: `nobody wrote down that you did not come because nobody had to` is now `it was not written down anywhere, because nothing required it to be`; `nobody saying anything was the entire punishment` is now `being left alone was the entire punishment`; `Nobody in this city has ever once asked me` is now `Not one person in this city has ever asked me`; `nobody has drafted one` is now `no one has drafted one`; and `nobody confuses them` is now `it is not a confusion anybody makes`. **No fact, no argument and no beat in either chapter changed.**

**6. THE DATE MNEMONIC WAS ARITHMETICALLY WRONG IN ALL EIGHTEEN PLACES IT APPEARS, WHILE EVERY TOTAL WAS RIGHT.** *"Ten from twenty leaves ten, with thirty and thirty and thirty and twenty-one makes one hundred and twenty-one"* assumes a thirty-day February and three thirty-day months between it and June. **The construction runs from `chapter-0433.md` to `chapter-0450.md` and nowhere else in the book: eighteen occurrences in eighteen chapters. It is now `Eight from twenty leaves eight, with thirty-one and thirty and thirty-one and —` throughout, which is 28 − 20, then March 31, April 30, May 31, and the day of June. Eight + 31 + 30 + 31 is exactly 100, so the day of June and the total are the same number and the derivation is checkable on the page.** All sixteen June totals were already correct and **every one is unchanged**: 103, 105, 107, 109, 110, 112, 113, 115, 117, 119, 121, 123, 125, 127, 128, 130, each re-verified against a real non-leap-year calendar from the twentieth of February. **THE REPAIR CROSSED THE 442/443 SEAM ON PURPOSE, because leaving 435–442 on the old derivation would have created exactly the kind of seam-contradiction finding 1 is about.**

**7. IDENTICAL NUMBERS HAD NO DIFFERENCE, AND 446 IS THE LONGEST CHAPTER IN THE VOLUME.** `chapter-0447.md:105` read *"the difference between those two numbers was me"* about two fours. **The two fours are the two rooms, not the two figures, and it now reads `the difference between them was me`** — which is what he means, and which the next clause, `and in February I was not one of them`, now confirms. **446 is 4,646 words against a 1,800–2,700 guide. The reviewer called this defensible for a scene and it is left exactly as written. It is the volume's only scene chapter and it is the one the reader is asked to sit through.**

## 0c. THE STRUCTURAL NOTE THE REVIEW RAISED, TAKEN AND NOT ACTED ON

The review found `state/current.md` at 428 KB, `state/open-threads.md` at 529 KB and `state/continuity.md` at 405 KB, built by appending every superseded block in full, and called it survivable today on the head-block convention but not survivable for another volume without an archive step. **That is true and it is recorded here rather than acted on, because truncating a state file is not a review fix and because `state/archive/` already holds `current-v1-v2`, `current-v3`, `current-v5`, `continuity-v1-v2`, `continuity-v3`, `open-threads-v1-v2` and `chapter-summaries-v1-v5` — the archive step has precedent, and what the live files still carry in full is Volumes 6 to 9.** **The volume close is the phase that decides what the next volume's writer reads, and it is told about this in `state/open-threads.md`. This is a hand-off, not a fix, and nothing was truncated by this repair.**

**TWO FALSE NUMBERS IN THE SUPERSEDED BLOCK BELOW WERE CORRECTED IN PLACE RATHER THAN LEFT AS CLAIMS**, because a superseded block retained in full is still what a writer prompt finds when it greps the file. **The old `§0a` second irreversible decision said `he brought eleven words in a notebook` and the Batch 0005 summary paragraph in `state/chapter-summaries.md` said `he then wrote eleven words in a notebook`; both now carry 31 / 11 / 38.** Nothing else in either block was touched and no block was deleted.

## 0a. WHAT THIS BATCH DID, AND ITS THREE IRREVERSIBLE DECISIONS

| Created | What it is |
| --- | --- |
| `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0443.md` … `chapter-0450.md` | **eight finished chapters, the seventeenth to the thirtieth of June. 21,984 words counted per file and summed post-write.** |
| `outline/volume-09.md` | **§6 movement 5 rewritten against the page**, with seven named places the plan was wrong, **and §6a's row for 0005 and §10's title re-run corrected in the same commit** |
| `state/continuity.md` | **`PC-89`–`PC-93` recorded as this batch's own proposals; `PC-57`–`PC-88` re-derived; two new facts a later phase must not re-derive; a new live block at the head and the 442 block marked superseded, not deleted. A verbatim duplicated paragraph inside the superseded 442 block was also removed.** |
| `state/current.md` | **this record** |
| `state/open-threads.md` | **new live gate block with every figure re-run by command after the last edit to the prose** |
| `workspace/volume-09/volume-close/PROMPT.md` | **the one live phase prompt in the repository. A CLOSE PHASE. Ch —. No prose.** |

**THE BATCH'S FIRST IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS 443 AND IT IS HIS.** On the morning of the seventeenth of June, in a forty-foot corridor, **he said the four words out loud — *because it is cheaper* — after the clerk of that room had told him on the fifteenth not to say them there**, and the clerk wrote them on a slip in a drawer. He had said them. They were in a book now. Nobody could take them out. **WHAT IT CLOSED: the option of the description's origin staying dark.** Mrs Imrie may not be asked a single question about the entry she wrote, ever, by anybody, and in four years a stranger who has read both books will know she was transcribing a man in a kitchen. **AND WHAT IT DID NOT DO: it did not make the room need anything.** The clerk says that distinction out loud, because he did not bring her a need — the room was going to need an answer on the thirtieth whether he came or not, and he brought her something to put in it.

**THE SECOND IS 445 AND IT IS HIS AND IT IS THE ONE THAT BOUGHT SOMETHING.** On the twenty-first he brought a notebook to a room where nine people sit — **thirty-one words written at Ferrier Street on the nineteenth, and eleven words of the clause's second sentence said out loud twice in the corridor — and they went into a drawer under a lock that has never been used in ninety days: where a minute names premises, no person keeping an address so named may be produced by that minute otherwise than in the presence of the subject.** A woman who prices things told him the price to his face in front of the clerk. **WHAT IT CLOSED: his own isolation.** From the thirtieth of June nothing that room says about his address can be used without him in the corridor, and he has told the household in effect that he is a doorway rather than a man in a building, and a doorway cannot be moved, closed or got out of the way.

**THE THIRD IS 449 AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE THAT COST HIM INSTEAD OF SOMEBODY ELSE.** On the afternoon of the twenty-eighth, in a street off a third court, ninety seconds after a boy at a counter told him that a card goes up at the head of the Reading at six on the last working day of a quarter, by a boy with a paste brush, who is not told and may not ask — **he worked out that there is a woman who stands under that wall at six every morning and has done for nine years, and that if he is standing at the north end when she picks the chalk up then the first name that has gone on the black tile in twenty years is written with a man underneath it, and he decided he would not go.** He gave the version in which he watches ninety seconds in the street and did not take it with him.

## 0b. THE POSITION AT CHAPTER 450 — EVERY FIGURE ON THE PAGE

**THE CARD OF THE THIRTIETH OF JUNE, PRINTED AT SIX IN THE MORNING IN A DIFFERENT HAND ON DIFFERENT PAPER BY A BOY WITH A PASTE BRUSH AND A LIST OF NUMBERS: floor 8,147, and against premises 34-6-118, 8,131. Sixteen under, tolerance three, out of band by thirteen. THE FIGURE IS DERIVED ONLY FROM THE PAINTED CARD READ IN CHAPTER 450 AND FROM BEATRIX HALM'S NOTEBOOK, WHICH CARRIES THE FIGURE OF THE THIRTIETH OF MARCH — NOT FROM `bible/power-system.md` AND NOT FROM `outline/volume-09.md` §5a. NO BAND FLOOR IS PRINTED IN ANY OF THE EIGHT OTHER CHAPTERS AND THE STRING `8,1` APPEARS NOWHERE IN THEM.**

**The floor rose by one and the plate did not move a grain in ninety days, because a plate moves on attested deeds and a consignment is not a deed and has no floor.** A card reads the plate at the composition of that premises at the hour it is read; the composition has said seven since nine forty-one on the morning of the thirteenth of March; the card read it at seven and has never known about the eight people asleep under that roof. **The top tile at the head of the Reading is black, has had nothing on it for twenty years, and at about ten past six on the thirtieth of June it has a name on it in chalk in Beatrix Halm's hand, because there is no plate and no card and no floor to press into it and no office in this city that could have done it for her.** She wrote it in her notebook with the time against it and, under the line, a name — the first name she has written at the bottom of a line in nine years — and told him to give the book back and say nothing.

**The requisition is one hundred and thirty days old, it is called, and it is not carried, not received and not decided. It will be answered at about two in the afternoon of the thirtieth of June by a man standing up as a member in a room that stands at five, with a seal he cannot refuse, four words he said out loud on the seventeenth, and a clause he entered on the twenty-first.** **THE ROOM WHERE NINE PEOPLE SIT STANDS AT FIVE AND DID NOT MOVE IN 447.** A woman who is not on the roll and cannot be sworn is in that corridor on the afternoon of the thirtieth because nobody may ask her and there is no sheet. A man restored by a vote in April is in the fourth seat because a clerk sent for him and he took his coat off. A keeper is filling the fourth block of a quarter's sheet at about ten to nine out of her own memory with nothing behind it, and the only printed figure for that address that morning is on a wall she cannot reach. Ledger **one hundred and fifty-eight lines**, seven gained in seven weeks, **ten turned, the ninth himself and the tenth a carter off Ferrier Street**, which is the volume's last panel: `[Ledger. One hundred and fifty-eight lines. Ten turned. None of them is him.]`

**THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS 434 AND IT IS HIS.** On the evening of the first of June, in his own kitchen, **he told Sun Meiling the rest** — the weekly administration against the composition, the copy in her own division, the line in the Church book, the keeper's hand with no certificate behind it, that the account cannot be closed, that she is its officer for as long as it stands, and that she cannot be sworn and no roll can protect her. **WHAT IT CLOSED: the option of protecting her by silence.** She certified on the twenty-ninth knowing half of it and would have signed knowing all of it; the telling gives her the shape of the room, not a choice, and she neither thanks nor forgives him.

**THE BATCH'S FIRST IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS 423 AND IT IS HIS.** At about ten in the morning on the eleventh of May, in his own kitchen, in front of four women, **he broke the wax on the service of the requisition of the twentieth of February**, which had lain on the shelf at the head of that stair since the morning it was laid.

**WHAT IT CLOSED: the option of being a stranger to his own service.** A service on a described subject goes against the head of the premises and not at a door, because the description of those premises was made against the head in October; it carries the address and the composition and no name under the address; and **a person who opens one has taken the service and there is no way in this city of putting a service down again.** Five people have now read it. **In about four years a stranger with a folder will want to know who else has seen it, and there are five of them, and it is not going to be five in four years.** And the fifth line said *composition of the premises at the date of service: eight*, and the composition has been seven since nine forty-one on the morning of the thirteenth of March.

**THE SECOND IS 424 AND IT IS THE FOURTH NO IN NINE VOLUMES.** On the thirteenth of May Alma Quayle asked him for the only thing in this city he can give anybody — a sentence said out loud in front of a clerk, because a recusal is lifted by the subject and by nobody else. **He said no.** The reason is not a virtue and it is not mercy: **lifting a recusal in the last three weeks of a quarter in which the Council cannot sit is using the one instrument he swore on the ninth of May never to use again, and she had already worked out what he decided, correctly, from four months of behaviour, and he did not confirm it and did not deny it.** What it closed: the thirtieth of June, which is the only day on which her line could have entered a minute.

**THE THIRD IS 427 AND IT IS NOT HIS AND IT IS THE EXPENSIVE ONE.** On the nineteenth of May **Agnete Lindqvist put a consult to a board of eleven people of the Church of the Quiet Number**, asked whether the Church wished to be counted as one of the places a room's paper goes to, and it said yes in about ninety seconds with no vote and no minutes taken by anybody. **The capacity is now public and it is public in a book in a building with eleven thousand people in it, and the deadline was the twenty-ninth of May and a miss waits a quarter until the first of October.** The cost is hers and she chose it: **in a room where nobody can be compelled to testify she cannot make eleven people answer her either, so she will not know for the rest of her life whether they would have said yes in a room where she could have pushed** — and she has been out of that room twice in eleven years and both times she was carried.

**THE FOURTH IS 428 AND IT IS HERS.** On the evening of the twenty-first of May, in a loading bay off Cordwain, **Runa Sato told him that on the twenty-eighth of May she is going to write a reason into the reason box of her own standing order of the twenty-seventh of October** — in her own hand, unpriced, uninvoiced, not to be thanked tonight or in four years or at the end of it — **and that the reason is going to name him.** The box has been empty for four years because a box left empty on an order to observe a kitchen is, to anybody who opens a folder in four years, indistinguishable from a man arranging for a woman to be findable in front of his own door. **What it closed: the last thing she was going to price, and the first thing she has ever done in this book that cannot be put in an office anywhere.**

## 4. WHAT A LATER VOLUME WILL OTHERWISE RE-DERIVE WRONGLY

1. **A month is thirty days and has no thirty-first, and the thirtieth is the last working day of the month and of the quarter.** Subtract day numbers; never calculate against a real calendar. **THE COUNTS 423–432 MAY USE, EACH DERIVED INSIDE ITS OWN CHAPTER AND NOT QUOTED FROM ANOTHER:** the requisition is **81** days old at 423, **83** at 424, **85** at 425, **87** at 426, **89** at 427, **91** at 428, **93** at 429, **94** at 430, **95** at 431 and **97** at 432, being (30 − 20) + 30 + 30 + the date of May. **Days remaining in the quarter from the eleventh of May is (30 − 11) + 30 = 49 and from the twenty-fourth of May it is (30 − 24) + 30 = 36, and days capable since the fifteenth of April at 430 is (30 − 15) + 24 = 39, and thirty-nine is ALSO the number of days left in the quarter as at the twenty-first of May, which is why 430 carries no figure in its title.**
2. **Volume 9 names no day of the week at all, and NINE WERE IN THE FIRST DRAFTS OF BATCH 0002 AND FOUR MORE WERE IN THE FIRST DRAFTS OF 423–432 — INCLUDING A CHARACTER SAYING *YOU DECIDED ON FRIDAY EVENING* ABOUT THE NINTH OF MAY — AND EVERY ONE WAS REPLACED WITH A PRINTED DATE BEFORE ANY GATE WAS RUN. THAT IS THE STANDARD.**
3. **Ledger: 142 on the fourteenth of March, 149 on the twenty-third of April, 150 on the twenty-fifth, 151 by half past ten that night, ten turned. The number of lines is not the number of lines turned. NO CHAPTER OF 423–432 TURNS ON IT AND NONE OF THE TEN CARRIES A PANEL.**
4. **A requisition is not one thing.** Tabled ≠ served ≠ laid ≠ called ≠ heard ≠ capable ≠ carried ≠ received ≠ decided ≠ administered ≠ **entered as held** ≠ **minuted**. A service and a minute are different animals again, and 425 says so on the page.
5. **A consignment is entered in that room's book on the day it is lodged and is not entered out on any day.** It cuts both ways.
6. **Wren Kallo's bar holds and was not tested in 401–432.** On the ninth of May a transmission came up above her own part and she initialled it in four seconds. **On the fifteenth of May he worked out that a copy of the minute of the twentieth of February went to the division of classification and did not go and ask.**
7. **Sun Meiling is never sworn, is under a permanent bar, is the officer of a holding that five people in a room can serve anything on — and she knows it, and he still has not told her the rest.** **AND THERE IS NOW NO CERTIFICATE FOR THE QUARTER BEGINNING THE FIRST OF JULY, WHICH IS 433'S AND 434'S AND NOT 423–432'S.**
8. **Perrin Sowe asked in writing three years ago to be left off the distribution of anything further upon file One. He has not come down that corridor since the fourth of February and on the seventeenth of May he came out of his own door to answer a question in a corridor and did not knock on the second-floor door.**
9. **LIN WEI'S FILE IS OPEN AND STAYS OPEN, AND NOTHING OF IT WAS TOUCHED IN 423–432.**
10. **THE ONLY THING THAT COULD QUIETEN THE ACCOUNT WAS A COMPOSITION OF SEVEN. IT WAS DONE AT A COUNTER BY ONE WOMAN ON HER OWN ACCOUNT ON THE THIRTEENTH OF MARCH AT NINE FORTY-ONE, AND ON THE ELEVENTH OF MAY A SEALED SERVICE WAS FOUND ON A SHELF IN THAT KITCHEN WHICH SAYS THE COMPOSITION OF THAT ADDRESS WAS EIGHT ON THE TWENTIETH OF FEBRUARY.**

**THE BATCH'S FIRST IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS 413, AND IT IS THE FIRST SPEND IN NINE VOLUMES.** At about ten past nine on the morning of the twenty-third of April, in a queue eleven long at a public post off a third court, he put his hand on a stranger's hand and gave back something that had been owed since the ninth of April.

**HE DID NOT DECIDE TO DO IT, AND THAT IS THE ONE THING ABOUT THE MORNING HE CAN BE CERTAIN OF AFTERWARDS: THEIR HANDS WERE NOT NEAR EACH OTHER AND THEN THEY WERE.** The relief made the man finished rather than strong, cost him four or five seconds of cold in his own palm, and the man said *you have got a cold hand* and nothing else and went out into the street. The stranger is a carter who works a lane two streets from his own front door and carried an old woman up three floors to a clinic door at two in the morning on the ninth of April because carrying her was quicker than waiting for help.

**WHAT IT CLOSED: the option of never spending. Nothing he has done in nine volumes ever cost a person he had not spoken to, and he spent the whole of that protection on a man in a queue who had a slip in his good hand and had agreed to nothing. THE LEDGER SAYS ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-NINE AND TEN OF THEM ARE TURNED AND THE TENTH HAS A FACE ON IT.**

**THE SECOND IS 416 AND IT IS NOT HIS.** Isolde Marchetti had carried nine seconds since the third of January, set them for the first of April, and moved them every time the day got here. On the twenty-ninth of April she sang them in the back corridor of the Bellhouse, because that is the only room in this city where she can think and a corridor is not a room. Nothing came back up the stair. **And by the time he understood why, it was already too late: for nineteen years her gift has been called local, and local is not a distance. It is a fence, and she has put it where the walls are, and nobody has ever shown her where the walls are. A room within about two hundred yards has agreed with nine seconds that were not in it, and she does not know which.**

**THE THIRD IS 418 AND IT CLOSES A DATE.** Runa Sato decided on the twelfth of April at four in the morning to certify the composition of premises 34-6-118. She priced it in a loading bay on the twenty-seventh and refused to invoice it, and she said she would not be thanked for it tonight or in four years or at the end of it. **On the third of May, at a kitchen table, after Nadia Ferrous said her bill out loud in front of eight people, she un-decided it, told a Field office she is not attending, and gave no reason, because a reason is a thing that gets filed.** THE CERTIFICATION HAS NOT HAPPENED. It is owed at 428. **What it closed: the option of a composition certificate at that address, in her hand, with her name at the head of it, before the quarter turns.**

**AND THE BATCH'S FOURTH DECISION IS THE ONE NOBODY WILL EVER HEAR ABOUT. At about ten past five on the ninth of May he worked out that he cannot ask that room for anything and that he can make it need something, and that every time he does it a woman pays for it out of her own future and can never be told she paid. He decided he is never going to use it again and he is not going to tell anybody, and it is the only thing in nine volumes he has ever decided not to do.**

## 0b. THE EIGHT INHERITED LINES, ONE EACH, AND WHAT CHAPTERS 413–422 DID TO EACH

1. **8,131 against a printed floor of 8,146, fifteen under, out of band by twelve.** **UNCHANGED, AND IT DOES NOT MOVE UP IN THESE TWELVE. IT IS PRINTED TWICE, AT 415, AND NO BAND FLOOR IS PRINTED ANYWHERE IN THE TWELVE.**
2. **A requisition laid on the twentieth of February.** **CAPABLE OF BEING CARRIED SINCE ABOUT TEN PAST NINE ON THE MORNING OF THE FIFTEENTH OF APRIL, AND NOT CARRIED, NOT RECEIVED AND NOT DECIDED IN ANY OF THE TWELVE. At 422 it is seventy-nine days old, (30 − 20) + 30 + 9. On the seventh of May it was seventy-seven days old and had been capable for twenty-two of the fifty-three days remaining in the quarter — 421 says all three figures and derives each inside its own chapter.**
3. **A composition certified four times a year by a woman who now knows she is the officer of it.** **SHE KNOWS AND HE STILL HAS NOT TOLD HER THE REST, AND NOTHING IN THESE TWELVE CHANGES THAT.** What 413–422 add is that **the composition is seven and eight people sleep under that roof**, that the seven is Nadia Ferrous's bill and was named out loud at her own table on the third of May, and that **the woman who was going to sign it has not signed it.**
4. **A finding in a minute in counter-forensics with his premises at the head of it and his name in the fourth part.** **NOT TOUCHED AS A DOCUMENT, BUT IT IS THE PART A TRANSMISSION CAME UP ABOVE AT TEN PAST FOUR ON THE NINTH OF MAY, AND WREN KALLO INITIALLED IT BECAUSE IT WAS HER DESK AND THE RULE WAS HERS. NOBODY ASKED HER ANYTHING.**
5. **A guild broker's name in the fourth line of a quarterly return, filling no seat, unstrikeable.** **UNTOUCHED. IT IS NOT IN A WEEKLY RETURN AND THE ROOM NEVER SEES A QUARTERLY.**
6. **Mrs Imrie, eleven minutes, on the twenty-fourth.** **NOT IN THESE TWELVE AND NOT ASKED ANYTHING.**
7. **A brother in a room with no window on the third sub-level, whose medical return was administered on the thirtieth of March.** **NOT OPENED AND NOT MENTIONED IN ANY OF THE TWELVE.**
8. **AND WHAT IS STILL SHUT.** **Wren Kallo's bar has never been tested and was not tested in these twelve** — at 422 he stands in a corridor with her and neither of them says one word about an address, and that is the point. **Dagmar Oyelowo has no gift and is given none and does not move and does not appear.** **The turned count was nine at the start of the batch and is ten at the end, and the tenth is a stranger and not him. Nothing was spent at the start of the batch and something was spent on the twenty-third of April and cannot be unspent.**

**AND FOUR THINGS THE BATCH OWES AND HAS PAID:** `PC-28` is carried forward and not fixed and no chapter of 413–422 reproduces a figure from that section head · **the figure he carries is 8,131 and it does not move up, and the ledger is 151 by the end of the batch and 10 of its lines are turned** · **Sun Meiling still knows and he still has not told her, and no chapter of these twelve has her find out a second time** · **the requisition is capable and is not carried, and on the ninth of May it acquires its first entry ever in a book at the end of a corridor, which is not a carrying.**

## 1. CHAPTER BY CHAPTER, AND WHAT EACH ONE ACTUALLY DID

- **413 (23 Apr). A QUEUE OFF THE THIRD COURT. 1,891 words.** **The first spend of the volume and it is on a stranger, and he did not decide to do it.** A counter that shuts at four whatever is in it. The ledger says one hundred and forty-nine and ten are turned. Ends on what a turned line is for.
- **414 (25 Apr). THE LEDGER CROSSING A NUMBER. 1,953 words. THE VOLUME'S FIRST SYSTEM PANEL, AND IT IS HERE BECAUSE THE CHAPTER TURNS ON THE LEDGER.** `[Ledger. One hundred and fifty lines. Ten turned. It is not sorry about either of them.]` Two numbers and five years of managing the wrong one. **He says the number out loud at a Field counter and she makes him say it twice.**
- **415 (27 Apr). THE STANDING ORDER SHE WILL KEEP. 1,980 words.** **Runa Sato prices her own certification and refuses to invoice it.** The order of the twenty-seventh and the note of the thirtieth, three days apart, in the same breath for the first time in six months. **And the chapter's real discovery is a figure: the composition is seven, seven is Nadia Ferrous's bill, and there are eight people under that roof tonight.**
- **416 (29 Apr). THE SINGER WHO CANNOT SING HERE. 2,193 words.** Four doors, a fire door, and a corridor that is a road between rooms. **She sums aloud because nobody agrees with arithmetic.** Nine seconds carried since the third of January, set for the first of April, not done on the first of April. **She sings it in the corridor and a room she cannot name agrees, and local turns out to be a fence.**
- **417 (1 May). THE SCENE. NADIA FERREROUS. 3,793 words.** A lift between the Vestry arcade and the clinic level. **Not impaired, and she says so first, and the axis is broken and named on the page: she gives no terms at all.** Distance folds downward and the car narrows. She says out loud that she has wanted him since a stopped lift in the first year of knowing them. She goes up one floor and it takes ninety seconds. **Her ending beat is hers: the bill is not the address, it is the count.** Scene body 2,054 on the recorded span, 2,987 on the literal span, probe 24 against 30 at 406.
- **418 (3 May). NINE WOMEN AND A WITNESS LINE. 2,483 words.** **Beatrix Halm, who cannot hear anybody at that table, puts her hand flat on the wood and says the thing she has been not saying for thirteen days.** Runa Sato works out that the seven who signed are seven officers and the space protects one woman and nobody else. Isolde does the other sum. **Nadia prices her bill at the table and Runa does not go on the twenty-eighth.** Beatrix writes two lines in one sitting for the first time in nine years.
- **419 (4 May). FOUR IN THE AFTERNOON, TO SEVEN DESKS. 1,734 words.** He puts the one question a member may put and she gives him all seven. **He goes and finds the wing off the third court and there is a woman at its desk who has been there nine years and who has never been told the room stood at three.** The sheet goes up that stair with an address at the head of it for the first time in twenty years.
- **420 (6 May). THE CLERK WHO READS A GAP ALOUD. 1,844 words. `PC-72`'s chapter.** She reads the line twice into a minute and **she is not wrong: a space is not a hole.** Nobody asks why and the page gives all four reasons. **AND THE BOX UNDER THE SPACE HAD BEEN BLANK FOR NINETEEN YEARS AND A CLERK FILLS A SPACE, SO SHE WRITES THE SAME WORD UNDER THE GAP THAT SHE WROTE UNDER THE FIFTH LINE, IN THE SAME HAND, AND SAYS IT ALOUD, AND THE CHAPTER DOES NOT PRINT IT.**
- **421 (7 May). THE WEEK IT COULD BE CARRIED. 1,710 words.** Seventy-seven days, twenty-two capable days, fifty-three left, and nobody stands up. **A Council sitting is entered a quarter ahead and the next one is the thirtieth of June, so for fifty-three days nobody in this city can change the number of seats in that room.**
- **422 (9 May). THE DAYS A SHEET WAITS. 1,943 words.** **A room of five enters the sheet it has been holding, on its own initiative, with nobody asked, and a clerk's entry cannot be struck.** It goes to seven desks at four. **Wren Kallo initials the transmission above her own part in four seconds and neither of them says a word about the address.** And he works out the only instrument he has and decides never to use it.

## 2. THE POSITION AT CHAPTER 422 — EVERY FIGURE ON THE PAGE

Plate **8,131**, printed twice in the batch and nowhere else. **No band floor is printed in 413–422 at all.** **The room where nine people sit stands at FIVE** and the fifth is him and a requisition carried needs five. **A requisition laid on the twentieth of February is CAPABLE OF BEING CARRIED, is not carried, not received and not decided, is seventy-nine days old, and has acquired exactly one entry in its life — on the ninth of May, in the keeping book of that room, saying that it is held there and not carried, entered by a clerk who may only enter and who cannot be struck by anybody including the man it is about.** **Nine sworn women on one roll taken on the second of April with 8,131 against the household's name, which cannot be put back down.** **A witness line with seven names and a two-finger gap in it, read into a minute twice on the sixth, with a box under the space that now carries the same word as the box under the fifth line.** Ledger **151 lines by the end of the batch, ten turned, the ninth still himself and the tenth a carter in a lane off Ferrier Street. He has spent, on the twenty-third of April, and it bought nothing.** File 4,112, one folio, no second.

**THE COUNCIL DOES NOT SIT AGAIN UNTIL THE THIRTIETH OF JUNE.** For fifty-three days from the seventh of May there is nobody in this city who can restore a standing, take one away or lift a recusal. **THE COMPOSITION OF PREMISES 34-6-118 IS SEVEN AND EIGHT PEOPLE SLEEP UNDER IT, AND NADIA FERROUS HAS SAID SO OUT LOUD AT HER OWN TABLE.**

**THE NINE ON THE ROLL, AS THE PAGE HAS THEM:** Ines Duarte, Beatrix Halm, Tessa Okonkwo, Nadia Ferrous, Runa Sato, Marguerite Vale, Agnete Lindqvist, Petra Ilyin, Isolde Marchetti. **The three who are not sworn: Wren Kallo, Sun Meiling and Dagmar Oyelowo**, each for the reason `bible/characters.md` fixes, none of whom appears or moves in 413–422.

**THE EIGHT AT FERRIER STREET, PINNED AND UNCHANGED BY THIS BATCH:** Agnete Lindqvist, Runa Sato, Nadia Ferrous, Wren Kallo, Isolde Marchetti, Beatrix Halm, Petra Ilyin, Sun Meiling. **Tessa Okonkwo IS NOT ONE OF THEM** — she is on the roll from the Vestry and has never been at that table — **and Marguerite Vale is not one of them either.** Six of the eight hold a post a division can serve a paper at — **Field (Runa Sato), counter-forensics on the second sub-level (Wren Kallo), the Ferrier line (Beatrix Halm), the guild (Petra Ilyin), the Church of the Quiet Number (Agnete Lindqvist), and Veil-Research (Sun Meiling)** — **and the two with no post at all are Nadia Ferrous, who works a lift between two floors, and Isolde Marchetti, who sings four nights a week in a room in Bellhouse. THE VESTRY IS NOT ON THAT LIST AND DAGMAR OYELOWO IS NOT ONE OF THE EIGHT.**

## 3. WHAT BATCH 0003 OWNS, AND WHAT IT MAY NOT REACH

**423–432. The eleventh of May to the twenty-seventh of May.** The plan's beats: **the envelope a subject would have to open (423)** · **Alma Quayle and the recusal he does not lift (424), and the chapter where he says no for the fourth time in nine volumes** · **the minute that carries five seals (425)** · **the man on the third floor (426)** · **the consult she takes to a board (427)** · **428, THE SCENE, RUNA SATO, and it now owes the reader something for a certification she has not gone to** · **ten lines on a return (429)** · **the days a sheet waits (430)** · **431 Tessa Okonkwo and the two returns about one matter in one quarter** · **432 the fourth block of a sheet, and the block no form provides**.

**IT MAY NOT:** print a band floor — the next card is the thirtieth of June and **Chapter 450 prints it** · print a settle date or the string `settle` · name a day of the week · announce an age · open Lin Wei's file, add a folio, or put the two hundred and fourteen thousand beside a name · ask Wren Kallo about the address or open a chapter in her rooms · **give Dagmar Oyelowo a gift (`PC-44`) or move her** · put a brother in Sun Meiling's mouth · **carry, receive or decide the requisition, or describe the entry of the ninth of May as any of those three** · **lift Alma Quayle's recusal** · **name the wing off the third court** · **print the word under the fifth line, or the second copy of it** · introduce a new final enemy, or revive either offer · resolve whom Wren Kallo told · **describe the eighteen-chapter scene breach as closed** · **reproduce any figure from `chapter-0359.md`'s section head (`PC-28`, still open)** · **write a panel anywhere but in a chapter that turns on the ledger, and the second of this volume's two is at 450** · **give a woman a mark she does not already own (`outline/volume-09.md` §4a, read before a body is written and not after)** · **repeat the 380-to-398 scene breach** · **have the requisition carried, received or decided**.

## 4. WHAT A LATER VOLUME WILL OTHERWISE RE-DERIVE WRONGLY

1. **A month is thirty days and has no thirty-first, and the thirtieth is the last working day of the month and of the quarter.** Subtract day numbers; never calculate against a real calendar. **THE COUNTS 413–422 MAY USE, EACH DERIVED INSIDE ITS OWN CHAPTER AND NOT QUOTED FROM ANOTHER:** the requisition is **63** days old at 413, **65** at 414, **67** at 415, **69** at 416, **71** at 417, **73** at 418, **74** at 419, **76** at 420, **77** at 421 and **79** at 422, being (30 − 20) + 30 + [days in April] + [days in May]. **Days remaining in the quarter from the seventh of May is (30 − 7) + 30 = 53, and from the fifteenth of April it is (30 − 15) + 30 + 30 = 75, and THE FIFTY-FIVE AND THE SEVENTY-FIVE ARE THE FIGURES ON THE FIFTEENTH OF APRIL AND NOT ON ANY OTHER DATE.**
2. **Volume 9 names no day of the week at all, and NINE were in the first drafts of 413–422 and every one was replaced with a printed date before any gate was run.**
3. **Ledger: 142 on the fourteenth of March, 149 on the twenty-third of April, 150 on the twenty-fifth, 151 by half past ten that night, ten turned from the twenty-third. The number of lines is not the number of lines turned.**
4. **A requisition is not one thing.** Tabled ≠ served ≠ sealed ≠ capable ≠ carried ≠ received ≠ decided ≠ administered ≠ **entered as held**. **THE ENTRY OF THE NINTH OF MAY IS A NEW STATE AND IT IS NOT ONE OF THE OTHERS.**
5. **A consignment is entered in that room's book on the day it is lodged and is not entered out on any day. There is no paper for it and none has ever been one, and it cuts both ways: a subject may not take a sheet off a room's list, and a lodged citizen may not be entered out of one.**
6. **Wren Kallo's bar holds, was not tested in 401–422, and is still not tested. On the ninth of May a transmission came up above her own part and she initialled it in four seconds and neither of them said a word.**
7. **Sun Meiling is never sworn, is under a permanent bar, and is the officer of a holding that five people in a room can serve anything on — and she knows it, and he still has not told her the rest, and the rest is that the account cannot be closed and she is its officer for ever.**
8. **Perrin Sowe asked in writing three years ago to be left off the distribution of anything further upon file One. He has not come down that corridor since the fourth of February and does not appear in 413–422.**
9. **LIN WEI'S FILE IS OPEN AND STAYS OPEN, AND NOTHING OF IT WAS TOUCHED IN 413–422.**
10. **THE ONLY THING THAT COULD QUIETEN THE ACCOUNT WAS A COMPOSITION OF SEVEN. IT WAS DONE AT A COUNTER BY ONE WOMAN ON HER OWN ACCOUNT ON THE THIRTEENTH OF MARCH AT NINE FORTY-ONE, IT COST HER THE ONLY THING SHE HAD AND HIM THE ONLY INSTRUMENT HE HAD, AND ON THE THIRD OF MAY SHE SAID THAT BILL OUT LOUD AT A KITCHEN TABLE AND A WOMAN WHO HAD DECIDED FOUR DAYS EARLIER TO SIGN THE FIGURE UN-DECIDED IT IN FRONT OF EIGHT PEOPLE.**

**THE BATCH'S FIRST IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS 408.** On the evening of the fourteenth of April he asked eight women at his own table, out
loud, in front of all eight of them, **having said the price out loud first** — one movement, no partial, no top-up, no draw, no return, the
witnessing at the address of the holding, every woman's name under his for four years and none of them strikeable.

**Nobody helped.** Nobody argued. A hand that helped would have been a hand that had to be on it, and that arithmetic has been true since
the first of December. Two of the eight cried and the chapter does not say which two. **WHAT IT CLOSED: the option of reaching nine without
handing them over. There is no other door and there never was.**

**THE SECOND IS 409 AND IT IS THE ONE `outline/ending.md` FIXES.** At ten minutes to nine on the morning of the fifteenth of April he lodged
his whole accumulated standing at a public post off a third court, in his own hand, on one sheet of plain paper, in about ninety seconds and
about eleven minutes including the queue. No instrument, no permission, no witness, no holder, no receipt, no copy. **The boy behind the
counter is the boy with the paste brush and the list of numbers from the thirtieth of March, and he was not told what he was holding and did
not ask, and that was the whole of the safety of it.**

**AND HE IS THE REASON A REQUISITION HE HAS SPENT FIFTY-FIVE DAYS ENJOYING THE SAFETY OF BECAME CAPABLE OF BEING CARRIED.**
(30 − 20) + 30 + 15 = **55 days from the twentieth of February to the morning of the fifteenth of April. It is not three months.** The room
stood at three through twenty-six weekly returns, went to four at eleven minutes to ten on the first of April when the Council restored
Bertram Sallow, and went to **FIVE at about ten past nine on the morning of the fifteenth**, and a requisition carried needs five. **So he
did the second thing himself, to himself, in a queue, with a pen, and after it there is no version of him that is a man nobody can answer
for.**

## 1. CHAPTER BY CHAPTER, AND WHAT EACH ONE ACTUALLY DID

- **401 (1 Apr). A BROKER'S NAME IN THE FOURTH LINE.** Served and not admitted; out of band by twelve there is no reading of a hall that puts
  a floor under him; Alma Quayle passes him in the corridor and makes him a separate item of business. **The Council restores Bertram Sallow
  at eleven minutes to ten and the room stands at four.** And a restored standing is entered over the line it stands on, and the line carried
  *Marguerite Vale*, and a clerk cannot correct a book. **He goes to the Vant's smaller rooms first rather than to the kitchen and does not
  knock a third time.**
- **402 (2 Apr). NINE SIGNATURES ON ONE ROLL.** The nine named, not counted off a kitchen; three of them at their own plates; the three
  unsworn each give their reason once in her own mouth. **The third column takes the highest rank in the household on the morning the roll is
  taken and is entered at 8,131, the first roll in that hall ever below a band, and Dagmar Oyelowo reads it back before she signs it and writes
  it anyway.** Thirteen days to the binding.
- **403 (4 Apr). A MAN READ PAST AT HIS OWN GATE.** Four-day-old paint and nothing new on it; and at the Fourth Branch gate a man who has
  scanned him every morning for four years looks at him the way the gate looks at the wall, and says *go on through*. **Beatrix Halm gives him
  the volume's opening sentence.**
- **404 (6 Apr). THE ENTRY SHE NEVER CLOSED.** The consignment, in Marguerite Vale's mouth, with no cap anywhere and no tenth because nobody
  will hand their whole life to nine strangers. **She will not put her name under it and the reason is Chapter 405.**
- **405 (8 Apr). THE PROMISE SHE CANNOT REMEMBER.** Petra Ilyin says no in one syllable and the reason is arithmetic. **He does not argue**,
  which is new. Runa Sato prices the queue from the rail.
- **406 (10 Apr). THE SCENE. PETRA ILYIN.** A bonded store, a roller door half down. Not impaired and she says so first. Two spoken terms,
  the second not about him, and all three template axes broken on the page. **The cost she names before anything happens: he is asking nine
  women to be evidence, and evidence is what people were before they were people.** **She names a question she will not ask, and he has the
  answer and does not give it, and she lets it stand.** Scene body 2,079 words on the recorded span (2,596 on the literal first-`##`-to-last-`##` span), probe 30.
- **407 (12 Apr). THE ARITHMETIC OF EIGHT HANDS.** Six of the eight are reachable; the witness line is the same instrument as the ground he
  bought at 370; and four short sentences about January. **The cost of asking eight people in one room is his, and it is that he will never be
  able to say he did not know.**
- **408 (14 Apr). THE THING HE ASKS FOR AT HOME.** The price said first, then the ask. Nobody helps. Isolde says the true thing unkindly and is
  right. **Sun Meiling asks who is going to read the line, and he does not have it.**
- **409 (15 Apr). THE MOVEMENT THAT CANNOT BE UNDONE.** Ten minutes to nine, one sheet, about eleven minutes. **Nine from that hour, the room
  at five, the requisition capable, and a clerk's word under a fifth line that nobody in the city understands.**
- **410 (18 Apr). SEVEN OF THE EIGHT.** Seven hands and a gap two fingers wide, put there on purpose, for the best reason anybody at that table
  has given since the thirtieth of March. **A gap in a line tells the person who reads it exactly one thing, and the thing is that the person
  who put it there decided to.**
- **411 (19 Apr). THE HAND THAT DID NOT SIGN.** Sun Meiling's chapter, in her own room, with no form read and no minute and no file. **She
  works it out in the order it has to be done in and is not frightened, and she is the cheapest person in this city.**
- **412 (21 Apr). THE ROOM STANDS AT FIVE.** The first weekly return carrying five; a word under the fifth line that nobody understands; a
  return out to **seven** desks at half past four, the seventh of which has no name and has never been named. *(Six on 13 October; corrected on the 15th.)* **The saving fact and the endangering fact are
  the same door, at the same hour, on the ordinary stream, and nothing on the face of the service is objectable.**

## 2. THE POSITION AT CHAPTER 412 — EVERY FIGURE ON THE PAGE

Plate **8,131.** Band floor **8,146.** Both printed at Chapter 400 and neither has moved; **a consignment has no floor at all.**
**The room where nine people sit stands at FIVE**, entered at about ten past nine on the morning of the fifteenth of April out of a book in
the keeping, and **the fifth is him.** **A requisition laid on the twentieth of February is CAPABLE OF BEING CARRIED and is not carried, not
received and not decided; it is sixty-one days old.** **Nine sworn women on one roll taken at the Ninth Terrace on the second of April with
8,131 against the household's name, and the roll's third column binds the household by the highest rank in it on the fifteenth, and cannot be
put back down.** **A witness line with seven names and a two-finger gap in it is in the keeping of that room and can be produced against all
seven for four years.** Ledger **142 lines and rising, nine turned, the ninth still himself. Nothing spent, nothing drawn, nothing routed, no
folio, no settle clock, no settle date, no classification completed.** File 4,112, one folio, no second.

**THE NINE ON THE ROLL, AS THE PAGE HAS THEM:** Ines Duarte, Beatrix Halm, Tessa Okonkwo, Nadia Ferrous, Runa Sato, Marguerite Vale, Agnete
Lindqvist, Petra Ilyin, Isolde Marchetti. **Three of them hold their own plate and their own rooms and were not in the hall: Ines signed at
Lantern Row in person without arguing, and Tessa and Marguerite on their own paper.** Six sleep at 34-6-118. **The three who are not sworn are
Wren Kallo, Sun Meiling and Dagmar Oyelowo, each for the reason `bible/characters.md` fixes, each given once, in her own mouth, at 402.**

**THE EIGHT AT FERRIER STREET AND THE NINE ON THE ROLL ARE NOT THE SAME EIGHT AND MAY NOT BE COUNTED AS ONE.** Eight women at the address;
Chapter 400 puts eight at that table; six of those eight are on the roll and two of them are not.

**THE EIGHT, NAMED, AND PINNED HERE ON 15 OCTOBER 2026 BECAUSE CHAPTER 405 AND 407 GOT THEM WRONG TWICE AND NO STATE FILE HAD
THEM IN WRITING: Agnete Lindqvist, Runa Sato, Nadia Ferrous, Wren Kallo, Isolde Marchetti, Beatrix Halm, Petra Ilyin, Sun Meiling.**
**Tessa Okonkwo IS NOT ONE OF THEM** — she is on the roll from the Vestry and has never been at that table — **and Marguerite Vale is not
one of them either.** Six of the eight hold a post a division can serve a paper at — **Field (Runa Sato), counter-forensics on the second
sub-level (Wren Kallo), the Ferrier line (Beatrix Halm), the guild (Petra Ilyin), the Church of the Quiet Number (Agnete Lindqvist), and
Veil-Research (Sun Meiling)** — **and the two with no post at all are Nadia Ferrous, who works a lift between two floors, and Isolde Marchetti,
who sings four nights a week in a room in Bellhouse. THE VESTRY IS NOT ON THAT LIST AND DAGMAR OYELOWO IS NOT ONE OF THE EIGHT.**

## 3. WHAT BATCH 0002 OWNS, AND WHAT IT MAY NOT REACH

**413–422. The twenty-third of April to the ninth of May.** The plan's beats: **the first draw of the volume, made on a stranger (413)** ·
**the ledger crossing a number and the first of the volume's two system panels, which is due because the chapter turns on the ledger (414)** ·
Runa's own price invoiced and not invoiced (415) · Isolde Marchetti summing aloud in a corridor she cannot sing in (416) · **417, THE SCENE,
Nadia Ferrous** · the household finding out what it has signed (418) · **four in the afternoon, to seven desks (419)** · the clerk who reads a
gap aloud (420).

**IT MAY NOT:** print a band floor — the next card is the thirtieth of June and **Chapter 450 prints it** · print a settle date or the string
`settle` · name a day of the week · announce an age · open Lin Wei's file, add a folio, or put the two hundred and fourteen thousand beside a
name · ask Wren Kallo about the address or open a chapter in her rooms · **give Dagmar Oyelowo a gift (`PC-44`) or move her** · put a brother
in Sun Meiling's mouth · carry, receive or decide the requisition · **lift Alma Quayle's recusal** · introduce a new final enemy, or revive
either offer · resolve whom Wren Kallo told · **describe the eighteen-chapter scene breach as closed** · **reproduce any figure from
`chapter-0359.md`'s section head (`PC-28`, still open)** · give the ledger a figure a title names that the ledger cannot reach · **write a
panel anywhere but in a chapter that turns on the ledger, and Volume 9 plans two and has now spent none** · **give a woman a mark she does
not already own (`outline/volume-09.md` §4a, read before a body is written and not after)**.

## 4. WHAT A LATER VOLUME WILL OTHERWISE RE-DERIVE WRONGLY

1. **A month is thirty days and has no thirty-first, and the thirtieth is the last working day of the month and of the quarter.** Subtract
   day numbers; never calculate against a real calendar. **THE THREE COUNTS THE FIRST TWELVE CHAPTERS REST ON: 15 − 2 = THIRTEEN days from
   the roll to the lodge; (30 − 20) + 30 + 15 = FIFTY-FIVE days from the twentieth of February to the fifteenth of April; (30 − 20) + 30 + 21 =
   SIXTY-ONE days to the twenty-first. 30 − 15 = FIFTEEN days remain in the quarter from the fifteenth, exclusive. Ninety days from the first
   of April to the thirtieth of June.**
2. **Volume 9 names no day of the week at all, and nine were in the first drafts of 401–412 and every one was replaced with a printed date.**
3. **Ledger: 142 on the fourteenth of March and rising because he keeps his round. Nine turned throughout Batch 0001. The number of lines is
   not the number of lines turned.**
4. **A requisition is not one thing.** Tabled ≠ served ≠ sealed ≠ carried ≠ received ≠ administered. **AND IT IS NOW ALSO NOT ONE OTHER
   THING: IT IS CAPABLE. Capable, carried, received and decided are four separate states and only the first has happened.**
5. **A consignment is entered in that room's book on the day it is lodged and is not entered out on any day. There is no paper for it and
   none has ever been one, and it cuts both ways: a subject may not take a sheet off a room's list, and a lodged citizen may not be entered
   out of one.**
6. **Wren Kallo's bar holds, was not tested in 401–412, and is still not tested. She has not told him whom and he has not asked.**
7. **Sun Meiling is never sworn, is under a permanent bar, is line one on the fifth sheet, and is now the officer of a holding that five people
   in a room can serve anything on — and she knows it, and he still has not told her the rest, and the rest is that the account cannot be
   closed and she is its officer for ever.**
8. **Perrin Sowe asked in writing three years ago to be left off the distribution of anything further upon file One, and that is why there is
   no production off that door and why he answers in corridors. He has not come down that corridor since the fourth of February.**
9. **LIN WEI'S FILE IS OPEN AND STAYS OPEN, AND NOTHING OF IT WAS TOUCHED IN 401–412.**
10. **THE ONLY THING THAT COULD QUIETEN THE ACCOUNT WAS A COMPOSITION OF SEVEN, IT WAS DONE AT A COUNTER BY ONE WOMAN ON HER OWN ACCOUNT
    ON THE THIRTEENTH OF MARCH, AND IT COST HER THE ONLY THING SHE HAD AND HIM THE ONLY INSTRUMENT HE HAD — AND IT PUT SOMEBODY ELSE IN
    CHARGE OF IT, AND THE SOMEBODY IS THE WOMAN WHO CERTIFIED IT.**

## 0. THE OPENING POSITION, ENTERING CHAPTER 401

**Volume 9 is Rank Nine, chapters 401–450, and `outline/series.md` fixes it there, puts it in stage five — he chooses
what he personally sacrifices — and names its pressure rotation *V9 ascent and action*. It also fixes this volume's
five scenes at 406, 417, 428, 438 and 446.** `outline/ending.md` fixes **rank nine at Chapter 409** and is not touched.

### THE EIGHT INHERITED LINES, ONE EACH, ALL FROM THE PAGE AT CHAPTER 400

1. **A MAN WHO IS FIFTEEN UNDER HIS BAND AND OUT OF BAND ON THE FIRST OF APRIL.** 8,131 against a floor of **8,146,
   which is printed on the card that came down at six on the morning of the thirtieth of March and is not inferred.**
   The two words went up the ranks wall in paint at about four the same afternoon, ninety days after the fourteen
   points that carried him there came back down. Tolerance three, so out of band by twelve.
2. **A REQUISITION THAT CROSSED INTO THE QUARTER WITH THE STANDING OF A ROOM THAT HAS BEEN ONE SHORT SINCE THE TWELFTH
   OF FEBRUARY.** It went across at four o'clock on the thirtieth of March with everything written on it. **It was
   never carried, never received and never decided.** What crossed was the standing, which is three, because only the
   Council can restore one and **the Council did not sit before the first of April.**
3. **A COMPOSITION THAT MUST BE CERTIFIED FOUR TIMES A YEAR BY A WOMAN WHO HAS BEEN DOING IT SINCE NOVEMBER WITHOUT
   KNOWING SHE WAS APPOINTED.** The fourth block of the carry-forward sheet is printed off the certificate of the
   composition of the thirtieth of November, **which is in Sun Meiling's hand** — so she certifies the composition of
   her own household four times a year, in her own division, in her own hand, under her own name, at the head of it,
   for as long as the account is open. **The account cannot be closed because payment is impossible. He has not told
   her. She does not know. She cannot be sworn.**
4. **A FINDING IN A MINUTE IN A COUNTER-FORENSICS DIVISION WITH HIS PREMISES AT THE HEAD OF IT AND HIS NAME IN THE
   FOURTH PART.** The ground is a description of premises 34-6-118 made over it on the morning of the twenty-seventh of
   October at about eleven against the head, by a clerk called Imrie, on four words said by him: *because it is
   cheaper.*
5. **A FOURTH LINE IN A WEEKLY RETURN CARRYING A GUILD BROKER'S NAME, WHERE THE FOURTH SEAT HAS SAID *VACANT* FOR SIX
   WEEKS AND ONLY THE COUNCIL CAN FILL IT.** Marguerite Vale wrote her own name in her own hand. **It fills no seat,
   cannot be struck by anybody including her, and is on no roll anywhere in the city.**
6. **A WOMAN WHO READ FOUR WORDS BACK TO A MAN AT A COUNTER ON THE SECOND FLOOR OF A BUILDING SHE HAS NEVER BEEN
   INSIDE IN HER LIFE.** Mrs Imrie, eleven minutes, on the twenty-fourth. **She would not say whose line the words
   were, because the moment she says it the house becomes an office's way of reaching her.**
7. **A BROTHER IN A ROOM WITH NO WINDOW ON THE THIRD SUB-LEVEL, WHOSE MEDICAL RETURN WAS ADMINISTERED ON THE THIRTIETH
   OF MARCH BY A DOCTOR WHO HAS PROMISED TO SAY NOTHING ELSE.**
8. **AND WATTS LEFT: WREN KALLO'S BAR HAS NEVER BEEN TESTED, DAGMAR OYELOWO HAS NO GIFT AT ALL (`PC-44`) AND HE HAS
   BROKEN TWO OF HER THREE THINGS, AND HE HAS A FLOOR, A DATE AND A DIRECTION AND NO MEANS OF GETTING THERE. THE TURNED
   COUNT IS NINE AND THE NINTH IS STILL HIMSELF. NOTHING SPENT IN NINE VOLUMES.**

### THE THREE DECISIONS THE VOLUME OWES, SETTLED IN THE OUTLINE BEFORE THE FIRST CARD

1. **WHAT RANK NINE COSTS, AND IT WAS SETTLED BEFORE IT WAS PLANNED.** **The Ninth Seal is not a band. A band is a
   figure and a figure is read off a card, and the Ninth Seal has no card, no floor and no interval — which is why
   the top tile on the Board is black and has never held a name in living memory.** To be held at nine a citizen
   **lodges their whole accumulated standing** into a consignment: one movement, in the citizen's own hand, at a
   public post, in the ordinary way. **It cannot be partial, cannot be topped up, cannot be drawn against and cannot
   be given back, and a lodged citizen is held while it stands.** **He cannot climb on merit and cannot climb on the
   reservoir — not one grain of it is scoreable — so the only way into nine is to give the whole of himself away,
   and the only thing that can be given is a figure other people have already put under him.** Every one of those
   names is now a document somebody else can produce. `PC-57` to `PC-60`.
2. **WHAT HAPPENS TO THE REQUISITION.** It is live, administered weekly from the first of April, and a room of three
   cannot carry it. **He is the fifth.** On the first of April the Council restores one standing and the room stands
   at four; **at ten minutes to nine on the morning of the fifteenth of April he lodges, the fifth is entered in the
   room's book, and for the first time since the twenty-third of December the requisition is capable of being
   carried — and he did that himself, in a queue, with a pen.** It is answered at 441–445 and it is **not** carried,
   **not** received and **not** decided against him.
3. **WHAT HAPPENS TO THE CERTIFICATE.** **She works it out from a gap in a witness line at Chapter 411 and he tells
   her the rest at the climax.** She certifies the next quarter anyway, in her own hand, because it is her job and
   there is nobody else. **Knowing is the one thing he cannot give back.**
